# SETTLED-DECISIONS — read before re-opening an area

> **Created 2026-09-11.** This file exists because of a specific, measured problem the project
> owner raised: *"I'm struggling to understand why there's so many issues despite AI looking
> over and working on these areas many times over."*

---

## Why this file exists (read this part)

The question was investigated on **2026-09-11** and the answer was uncomfortable: **most of the
churn is not new bugs — it is old decisions being re-opened without anyone being told they were
already settled.**

Hard evidence, from GitHub issue history:

| Area | First settled | Re-opened | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Gate vs score separation** | **#638** — closed 2026-08-16, checklist asserts *"flat constants removed"* | **#789 / #790** — 2026-09-11 | **26 days** |
| Signal denominator | #758 — fixed, then **fixed again** (first fix still read a runtime value) | #761 — recalibrated 2026-09-15, **closed 2026-10-07** | 1 day |
| Classification thresholds | #638 removed the constants | #761 — rescaled against the frozen denominator 2026-09-15, **closed 2026-10-07** | 26 days |

And the mechanism that made it silent:

> **#638 was closed on INTENT, not on ENFORCEMENT.** Its pre-close checklist ticked
> *"flat constants removed"*. `ENTRY_THRESHOLD = 40` was **still live** in
> `protocol-gating.service.ts` (found 2026-09-11, L324), with a code comment beside it recording
> that this exact constant already caused an incident on 2026-08-12 — and it stayed live until the
> 2026-09-15 rescale, which is why trading stopped for five days. The box was ticked; nothing
> enforced it. `#761` now carries the missing enforcement: `signal-threshold-scale.spec.ts`.

**A checklist box is a claim. A build-time guard is a fact.** That distinction is the whole point
of this file.

---

## The three loop-generators (each makes the next worse)

### 1. An issue is closed on intent, not on enforcement

An agent does the work, verifies it by reading the diff, ticks the boxes, and closes. Nothing
prevents the next change from undoing it, and nothing re-checks it later. The decision lives in a
closed issue body that no future agent reads.

**Countermeasure:** every settled decision below names either (a) a build-time guard, or
(b) explicitly says **UNGUARDED**. An unguarded decision is one that WILL be re-opened.

### 2. A fix in the seed file is not a fix in the database

`ProtocolDbService.seedProtocolsToDatabase()` is **upsert-only** — it creates and updates but
**never deletes**. Therefore:

- removing a directive from the seed does **not** remove its DB row
- moving one between groups does **not** move its DB row
- **re-seeding cannot converge them**

The diagnostic lies: the seed endpoint returns `entries: N`, which counts rows **upserted**, not
rows that **exist**. It reads exactly like a successful removal.

**Measured 2026-09-11** (`scripts/diff-seed-vs-db.cjs`, before the fix):

```
seed     : 47 protocols, 419 placements
database : 45 protocols, 449 placements
ORPHAN   : 30 rows — live config that the seed no longer contains
```

`rsi-overbought` accounted for **28** of them: removed from the seed for `LONG_ONLY` protocols,
still live in 30. Because it is `directionBias: 'bearish'` and those protocols are `LONG_ONLY`,
it was excluded from **both** numerator and denominator — so it did not read as a zero, it
silently lowered the **reachable ceiling** of 28 protocols by 140 points each (**3,920 points**).
That is issue **#792**, and the orphans were the mechanism that made it live.

**Countermeasure:** `scripts/diff-seed-vs-db.cjs` (run it after ANY seed edit). Explicit SQL for
removals — the seeder has no delete path.

### 3. Nothing recorded which areas were settled

The contracts doc lists invariants that **are** guarded. There was no companion list of decisions
that are settled but **NOT yet enforceable** — which is exactly the set that gets re-opened.

**Countermeasure:** this file, plus `scripts/detect-circular-work.cjs`.

---

## Settled decisions — consult before changing the area

**Legend:** 🟢 enforced by a build guard · 🟡 partially guarded · 🔴 **UNGUARDED — will be re-opened**

| # | Decision | What it means | Settled | Guard status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **A directive `score` is a MAGNITUDE**, not a percentage. Direction lives in `signalClass` | Never `Math.min(score, 100)`. `maxScore` expresses weight; the protocol total is the sum of `maxScore`s | ADR-010 | 🟢 `no-score-clamp` scan |
| 2 | **ONE stable, config-derived denominator** = `configuredSignalMax` | Percentage = earned ÷ **configured** capacity. Never divide by an observed sum (it shrinks when a directive goes quiet, so the same conditions score differently each tick) | ADR-011, #758 (fixed twice) | 🟢 `no-observed-denominator` scan + `INV-STABLE-DENOMINATOR` canary |
| 3 | **Direction eligibility comes from CONFIG, not the tick** | So the denominator is identical across symbols in the same tick | ADR-012 | 🟢 `INV-STABLE-DENOMINATOR` |
| 4 | **A risk gate may contain only `category: 'structural'` directives** | A directional voter in a gate is **erased, not downgraded** — no score, no direction, no capacity, and nothing reports it | **#638 (2026-08-16)** then **INV-GATE-MEMBERSHIP (2026-09-11)** | 🟢 `validate-directive-group-kind.cjs` |
| 5 | **Gate capacity is excluded from the denominator** | `if (!isGateGroup) capacityMax += maxScore` — a gate expresses a hurdle, not conviction | #758 | 🟢 `INV-STABLE-DENOMINATOR` |
| 6 | **Absence of evidence must never become a number or a veto** | An unmeasurable directive returns `score: 0` **with `unmeasured: true`**. It contributes nothing and can never trip a gate | #766 / #755 | 🟢 `no-fabricated-baseline` scan + 2 specs proven to fail without the guard |
| 7 | **Group weights are NOT applied in assembly** | Aspirational until #718. Do not assume weights affect the composite | ADR-013 | 🔴 **UNGUARDED** |
| 8 | **Re-seeding is ADDITIVE-ONLY** | Any removal/move needs explicit SQL. `entries: N` is not proof of a removal | #789 (2026-09-11) | 🟡 `diff-seed-vs-db.cjs` (manual run, not gated) |
| 9 | **`!isGateGroup` alone is correct for capacity — do NOT add a `directionBias` test** | Tried and **reverted** 2026-09-10: declared bias massively understates what directives emit (e.g. `macd-crossover` declares `neutral`, fired directionally 5,252×). Gating on declaration excludes the directives doing the real work | #761 comment, ADR-011 | 🟢 `directional-capacity-contract.spec.ts` (5 tests; **proven to fail** when the reverted bug is reintroduced — 2 tests fail) |
| 10 | **Thresholds are recalibrated ONLY against the frozen CONFIGURED denominator, from a measured distribution — and the resulting scale is PINNED by a test** | The bands (#761's 80/40) were invalidated by the #758 denominator change; recalibrating while the denominator moved is the loop. **Completed 2026-09-15**: entry 40→6, strong 80→13, gate 65→10 — a ÷~6.3 units migration (ordering preserved, derivation recorded at the code site), after which live executions resumed the same day (before: zero executions 09-11→09-14). ⚠️ Re-tuning afterwards is legitimate but must be DELIBERATE (maths-change policy: copied config + monitored comparison) and must update the spec | #761 + #758 (recalibration 2026-09-15; closed 2026-10-07) | 🟢 `signal-threshold-scale.spec.ts` — 5 tests, **canaried 2026-10-07**: reverting entry 6→40 fails 2; breaking the style ordering (swing 9→14) fails 1; both restored byte-for-byte |
| 11 | **A bar gap after a bridge restart is EXPECTED during development — it is not a defect. It also self-heals.** | `BarRefreshService` re-fetches **by count** (200 bars ≈ **16.7h** at `5m`, 3.3h at `1m`), so a normal re-fetch reaches back **past** any plausible development gap and refills it. Nothing needs to be built to obtain the data. A gap is only unrecoverable if it **exceeds the fetch window**, or if a symbol stops being refreshed entirely | #795 (filed `priority-high`, **closed as NOT-A-BUG** 2026-09-11) | 🟡 `INV-BAR-ORDER` in `contract-probe.cjs` now **distinguishes** a data gap (passes, reports) from a scoring bug (fails) |
| 12 | **A strategy IS a bot's whole configuration** — protocol + role + **market** + broker + execution policy. Any difference at all makes a different strategy; two byte-identical bots are a **DUPLICATE (a defect), not a merge** | Identity is `strategyKey = sha256(protocolId \| role \| symbol \| brokerId \| canonicalTreatment)`, 16 hex. `symbol` and `sessionWindow` are **in** the key: excluding `symbol` collapsed **26 strategies to 18**, and omitting `sessionWindow` gave **15**. Both were real errors by real agents, both are recorded in the guard. Canonicalisation is **behavioural, not textual** — `1` and `1.0`, bare `london` vs `["london"]`, and alias `sydney`→`asian` must all hash identically | `docs/trading-bot-model.md` **§1c / §1d**, owner-confirmed **2026-09-18**; #876 (derivation) / #875 (naming) | 🟡 `nest/scripts/verify-strategy-derivation.ts` — drives the **REAL** service against the **REAL** database and asserts the canonicalisation rules (24 pass). **Manual, not gated** — it needs a live DB and `ts-node`, which `prebuild` does not have, so this is by design and **not** an oversight. ⚠️ Its fixture counts are **dated**: measured **27** strategies on 2026-09-22 against the fixture's **26**; every invariant holds, so the movement is a bot being added, not an identity regression. The guard's own rule is *"if they move, that is a finding, not a test to update"* |

A/B exists to test a MINOR change, so the two sides usually signal together and merge into one position almost every time. **The live pair (row 25) is therefore a LOCK, not a measuring instrument** — see row 26 for where the measurement went | owner question 2026-09-23 ("is it possible different notions of A/B testing have merged?") | 🔴 **UNGUARDED** |

It is not a test and it does not trade. **A/B** = a **LOCKED PAIR** (row 25) — two protocols, or two bots, active together over one period, differing in ONE declared field, compared on outcomes. Nothing else is "A/B". **Design variant (Old / New)** = two renderings of one page; presentation only (`VariantSwitcher`, param `?variant=A`). ⚠️ The `ab_test` table + `AbTestService` + the `TCS AB-A` / `TCS AB-B` broker tags are the **snapshot TRIAL** — the one instrument that ran two simultaneous live positions. | 25 | **An A/B pair is LOCKED — this is the definition, not a nicety** | Requirement (2026-09-23): protocols will have A/B testing in a way that locks both protocols to ensure that activity matches — **and the same for strategies.** A comparison is valid only if: **(1)** both sides are registered as a pair with a direction (which one is the incumbent); **(2)** both are `active = 1` for the whole comparison; **(3)** the SAME symbol set — the symbol is the constant, never the variable; **(4)** exactly ONE declared difference, named at registration; **(5)** activate/deactivate **together** — a lifecycle change applies to both sides; **(6)** neither side's config is edited while running, and the falsification test (#944) is recorded **before** it starts; **(7)** neither side is deleted until the comparison reports. Substrate that already exists: `trading_strategy.duplicatedFromId` (**0 rows** — but the copy path `TradingStrategyService.duplicate()` **DOES** set it, preserves both lineage links, inherits the symbol exactly and gives the copy a working subscription; **0 rows means nobody has ever run a copy, not that the code is missing**), `protocol.derivedFrom` (**7 protocols**), and `/trading/ab-strategies`, which already compares realised P&L per side (#894) with `—` never `0`. **The genuine gap is the LIFECYCLE — activate/deactivate together, and refusing an edit mid-comparison** | requirement 2026-09-23 → **#963** | 🟡 **Enforced at RUNTIME, not by a build guard.** `startPair()` activates both sides in ONE InnoDB transaction and registers `ab_pair.state='running'`; while running, `update()` **REFUSES any behavioural field on either side** ("behavioural" taken from the canonical `BOT_RULES` minus `NON_BEHAVIOURAL_COLUMNS`, so a field added later is covered automatically); `stopPair()` deactivates both. 23 specs in `trading-strategy-pair.spec.ts`; the GROUP guard and the freeze guard were each canaried. ⚠️ No build-time gate exists, so a future second write path to `trading_strategy` could bypass it |
| 26 | **A/B is MEASURED by BACKTEST, not by the live pair** | Requirement (2026-09-23): running strategies in a way that still tells us which is marginally better, without having to wait or them colliding because their trades won't be real, just calculated. In a backtest there is **no broker**, so there is no merging, no collision and no "who owns the closing" — both sides are scored over the **identical** bars, which is fairer than any live test, and in minutes rather than three months. The live pair (row 25) keeps its LOCK and becomes the **final confirmation step**, not the instrument. Measured 2026-09-23: `/api/backtest` is real and populated — **11 runs / 391 persisted trades**, with `optimize`, `walk-forward`, `benchmark`, `runs/compare` and a UI at `/education/backtest`. ⚠️ **But the comparison NAMES A WINNER ON 3 TRADES** — `best: {metric: sharpeRatio, value: 42.31}` on a 3-trade sample, with **no minimum-sample bar**. That is the very defect `AB_MIN_TRADES_FOR_VERDICT` was added for in the snapshot trial ("noise wearing a result's clothes"), and it is the FIRST thing to fix before any backtest verdict is trusted. ⚠️ Also measured: `compare` disagrees with the run result (`netPnl −0.0009` vs `totalPnl 0`; `maxTradeDrawdown −0.0003` vs `maxDrawdown 27`). ⚠️ `BacktestRunRequest` takes a `protocolId` — there is **no bot id and no pair id**, so "backtest a strategy" and "backtest a pair" are not yet expressible. ⚠️ Work belongs to **#816** (existing sprint epic) and **#823** (workstream), with the replay view in **#819** and indicator rendering in **#606** — **NOT a new sprint** | owner question + direction 2026-09-23 | 🟡 **PARTIAL — the FIRST thing this row named is now BUILT AND GUARDED; the rest of the row is not.** ⚠️ **The 🔴 "a backtest verdict can be issued on 1 trade" is NO LONGER TRUE — corrected 2026-10-07, re-measured, not inherited.** `runs/compare` now assesses **both** populations (`assessSamples`, shared `MIN_SAMPLES_FOR_VERDICT` from `common/trading/verdict-bar.ts`) and returns `best: null` / `verdict: insufficient-data` / `metric: null`. **Observed live 2026-10-07** (`POST /api/backtest/runs/compare`, two runs at 22 and 24 trades): `best: null`, `requiredPerSide: 30`, with counts per side for trades **and** signals. I was thinking A based on the working of the Note → A agreed, **with the unit corrected to currency** | #894 | 🟡 `bot-rules.ts` declaration + `validate-bot-rules.cjs` in `prebuild` (a new column with NO declaration fails the build — observed: `columns=34 declared=30`); 36 specs including the exclusive boundary **canaried both directions** (flipping `>` to `>=` failed exactly the boundary test); seed convergence declares ONE bot only (dry-run: 1 change, 26 unchanged). ⚠️ NOT guarded: nothing compares `schema.prisma` against the generated Prisma client, so a column can exist in the DB **and** the schema and still be rejected at runtime (measured on this very change) |

| 29 | **An OBSERVATION is marked as one — the decision VALUE is the claim, not an empty column** | `decision_log.decision` now distinguishes what used to be one word: **`OBSERVED`** = the ladder scored this symbol and **NOTHING consumes the row** (no bot is subscribed, so nothing will ever trade it); **`SCORED`** = a bot scored a signal it may act on; **`BLOCKED`/`SKIP`** = a consumer existed and **refused**; **`QUEUED`/`EXECUTED`/`FAILED`** = the signal reached the order path. Before this the ladder wrote `'SCORED'` with `strategyId: ''`, so "nothing reads this" had to be **inferred from an empty column** — measured 2026-09-23: **706 no-bot rows today, 539 of 577 scored rows in the issue's own measurement**. ⚠️ **History is NOT rewritten**: `strategyId = ''` on a `SCORED` row IS the historical marker, and `src/core/decision-display.js` reads it as an observation — so the distinction is answerable for the whole table without touching log history. **One vocabulary, one palette, one reader** (`src/core/decision-display.js`): three pages had three private decision colour maps (Decision Trail, Symbol Focus, Execution Queue) and adding a marker meant editing three silent places | owner sprint #960 Tier 0 prerequisite → **#971** | 🟡 **Two-sided, canaried, and partly gated.** Backend: `automation-loop-decision-units.spec.ts` asserts an `OBSERVED` row has **NO** `strategyId` (writing a bot's row as OBSERVED would hide a real signal) and still declares its score unit — canaried **both** directions (`OBSERVED`→`SCORED` failed `Expected: >= 1`; a bot id on the observation row failed `Expected: true`), restored byte-for-byte. Frontend: `decision-display.test.js` asserts an observation and a signal differ in **label AND colour** (both directions) — canaried by giving OBSERVED the signal colour (`fail 1`). ⚠️ **The frontend test runs only via `npm run test:decision-display`; it is NOT in a prebuild gate**, so it is partial, not gated |

| 31 | **The primary agent is the arbiter, and every decision has THREE homes** | Requirement 2026-09-24: the Max-thinking primary chat may **override any other agent** and decides freely, but a decision is not recorded until it is in **all three** of — (1) a `DECISION (date, agent)` comment at the code site, (2) a row **here**, (3) the issue. The point is **reversibility**: the requirement reverses by reading, so a decision nobody can find is one nobody can undo. ⚠️ **#919 is the worked example of a TWO-home decision** — the work landed in `38147f1` citing the issue and the code was correct, but this register was never written and the issue stayed open three days, long enough to block sprint #924. The **Development area is confirmed as the EXPERIMENT SANDBOX**: an agent may build a competing approach there, but it must end in a decision, registered in `src/data/theories.js` with a **falsification test**, and with `evidence.basis` graded (`measurement` / `code-read` / `cited`) so a citation can never masquerade as a measurement | requirement **2026-09-24** | 🟡 `scripts/validate-decisions.cjs` + `scripts/validate-theories.cjs` — both canaried (`--self-test`: 8 and 13 cases, every rule observed to fail) — ⚠️ **CORRECTED 2026-09-28:** `validate-decisions.cjs` is now **IN `prebuild`** (16 self-test cases), because a re-open guard that only runs when someone remembers does not close a gap — see the 🔒 *Settled subjects* section and RULE C, which fails a row that names a settled subject without citing the row that settled it. `validate-theories.cjs` (13 cases) remains **ON DEMAND**, stated rather than implied |

| 32 | **`'group-weighted'` KEEPS its historical name — the field is declared inert, not renamed** | Requirement 2026-09-24, answering review row **R17** with *"Keep"*. #919 asked to rename `'group-weighted'` → `'configured-capacity'` on the grounds that *"it selects a denominator"*. **It selects nothing:** `weightStrategy` is only echoed into the payload, and the denominator is hardcoded `'configured-capacity'` (`protocol-gating.service.ts` L1152). Measured — the composite is IDENTICAL for `'group-weighted'`, `'maxscore-weighted'` and a deliberately bogus value. **The rename was rejected as cosmetic**: it would have made the documentation read correctly while the control stayed dead, which is the defect #919's own item 4 names (*"a registry field that reads as a control and has no effect"*). ⚠️ Cost was measured BEFORE the ruling: **44 references across 17 files + 28 stored rows** (a rename without the DB update would silently rescore). The dead control is fixed by DECLARING it — the service doc states **IS INERT**, group weights read *· not applied* on 5 surfaces, and META.Stack Explorer reports the real `denominatorMode` instead of passing merely because the field exists | Requirement (2026-09-24) (review **R17**) | 🟡 `nest/src/modules/scoring/weight-strategy-inert.spec.ts` (6 tests, canaried both directions) — ⚠️ **NOT** in `prebuild` |

| 33 | **Design observations have their OWN register, and every row must name what would make us RECONSIDER** | Owner answered review row **R21** on 2026-09-24 with *"new register"* — choosing a dedicated register over a second section of the review queue, and accepting that observations should be able to **accumulate BEFORE they become decisions**. Trigger: **#990**, where a finding (*"the mechanism that locks in profit had no surface on the bot page"*) landed in a **code comment** — durable, and invisible to the one person who reconsiders designs. ⚠️ The field that makes this a register rather than a grievance log is **`reconsiderIf`**, **required on every row** and the direct analogue of a theory's falsification test; without it an observation is a preference. `evidence.basis` is graded exactly as in `theories.js` (`measurement` / `code-read` / `cited`) so a citation can never pass as a measurement. **Not** a defect report (that is an issue) and **not** a decision (that is the review queue) — it is a design tension held open on purpose. Readable at `/development/design-observations` | Requirement (2026-09-24) (review **R21**), **#990** | 🟡 `scripts/validate-design-observations.cjs` — canaried (`--self-test`: **8** rules each observed to fail on a broken copy, and the real register observed to pass) — ⚠️ **ON DEMAND, not in `prebuild`**, matching `validate-theories.cjs` |
| 260 | **THE EXECUTIONS TABLE NAMES ITS SYMBOL — AND THE DAY CAN BE READ AS ONE TABLE.** Owner 2026-11-23: the table needed the symbol, and it appeared to show executions for only one instrument because the prices looked alike. Measured: `DepthTrade` did not carry a symbol at all — `buildDetail()` mapped the tagged trades to `{entryDate, exitDate, prices, direction, pnl, exitReason, signal, kept}` and DROPPED `symbol`, even though the tag was there. So the panel was per (symbol, day) by construction and the instrument was left IMPLICIT; a reader seeing eight rows with prices near 1.30 had no way to tell one ticker from three. Fixes: `DepthTrade.symbol` is populated from the tagged trade; the executions table gains a **Symbol** column and falls back to the panel's own symbol when a row has none; the table is EXTRACTED to `ExecutionsTable` so the per-symbol panel and the merged view cannot drift; and the Bar-by-bar panel gains an **"All symbols"** chip per day that concatenates every selected symbol's executions with each row's own ticker. ⚠️ Bars and the ladder stay PER SYMBOL — a merged price axis across instruments is meaningless, and the merged view says so in place. Live 2026-11-23: `movers-winners-2026-11-19` returns **112 rows across 3 symbols** (CANG 30 · ELOG 32 · MVST 50), every one carrying its symbol. | 2026-11-23 | 🟡 partial — the data is verified live (112/112 rows carry a symbol, three symbols in one run) and the two views share one component, but **nothing fails if the symbol is dropped again**: `run-depth.spec.ts` builds its fixtures from a helper that never sets a symbol, so no test would notice. The next change to `buildDetail()` must add that assertion. |

| 261 | **"REFUSED" WAS ONE WORD DOING TWO JOBS — AND THE DAY NOW READS AS A ROW OF DECISIONS PER BAR.** Owner 2026-11-23: a row read `LONG refused` beside `Exit SL` and asked how a refused trade can exit. It cannot: `kept: false` means the **day list did not count** the trade, while the engine still opened it, walked it and stopped it out — often on a warm-up session the screen never covered. `buildDetail()` recorded only the boolean, so the table printed a verdict with no cause. `DepthTrade.selectionReason` now carries `before-cutoff` or `not-selected` (mapped from `applySelection()`'s own codes by object identity), the executions table says **which** refusal in words, shows the P/L of those rows (faded, titled "not in any total") instead of a dash, and a footer states the count. Second half of the request: a **Decisions** table, one row per bar — Time (with a pre-screen marker before the cutoff) · Score · Dir % · Opp % · Risk x · Signal class · Decision · **Why** — where Decision is `ENTER` / `ENTER + EXIT` / `EXIT <reason>` / `SIGNAL — NO TRADE` / `SKIP — UNDER BAR` / `NO DATA` and Why is the **highest-contributing directive's own score and reason string**, flagging a reading that was not counted in direction. ⚠️ **IT IS A RENDERER, NOT A SECOND SCORING PATH**: every column already existed in `detail.bars` (composite `score`, `signalPct`, `opportunityPct`, `riskMultiplier`, `signal`, `passedBar`, per-directive `score`/`maxScore`/`signalClass`/`reason`/`inSignal`), and the entry-gate verdict is the engine's own `passedBar`. No threshold is re-invented and no bar is re-scored. Live 2026-11-23 on `movers-winners-2026-11-19`: **67 bars per symbol, 67 with directive detail**, and of **112 trades 73 were not counted** — the hidden population that made the executions table look wrong. | 2026-11-23 | 🟡 partial — the payload is verified live (112 trades, 73 carrying a reason; 67/67 bars carrying directives and the score split), but **no test asserts either contract**: nothing fails if `selectionReason` is dropped again, and nothing fails if `passedBar`/`directives` stop being returned. The state-to-decision mapping is a pure function inside the component and is not extracted, so it cannot be unit-tested without a DOM. |



| 262 | **THE BEST RESULT PER TEST IS NOW A CORE RECORD, WITH THE SETTINGS THAT PRODUCED IT — AND IT IS DECIDED PER DAY, NOT ON THE TOTAL.** Owner 2026-11-23 required the best P/L per test to be kept long term: without the highest figure on record a tuning loop can improve on its own last attempt repeatedly and never regain new ground, so an improvement should back up the settings that produced it, and that must be a core mechanism for all backtesting rather than one page's feature. Every run was already stored (`backtest_selection`, `backtestrun`); what did not exist was the ANSWER, which is a query over all of them that nobody runs. So a new table `backtest_best` (modelled in `schema.prisma`, created with explicit `CREATE TABLE`, never `db push`) holds one row per `(scope, profileKey)` with the record's per-day and total P/L, trades, win rate, profit factor, the applied config JSON, the run it came from and `achievedAt`. `best-run.service.ts` is the single writer: movers runs record scope `movers-selection` from `SelectionRunService`, protocol runs record `protocol-backtest` from `BacktestService.run()`. ⚠️ **THE BASIS IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** `netPnl` alone must never decide a record: five days beats one every time, so a total-keyed record would simply track how long the window was — the same two-numbers-different-basis defect that has already produced three wrong comparisons in this repo. The record is replaced ONLY on net P/L **per measured day**, and the raw totals are stored beside it. A zero-day run is REFUSED rather than divided into an `Infinity` that nothing could ever beat. The `profileKey` deliberately EXCLUDES the day, so many days accumulate onto one record; it also excludes the semantic-version comparison, because a record set under older execution semantics is REPLACED rather than compared (`EXECUTION_SEMANTICS`). A write failure is REPORTED in `because` and never thrown — the run produced real numbers and must not be failed by the bookkeeping beside it. Surfaced as `GET /api/backtest/best` (an unknown scope is a 400, not an empty list) and a `BestRecordPanel` on the movers page that puts Best · This run · the record it beat in one table and lists which settings the record holds differently from those now in force. | 2026-11-23 | 🟢 enforced — `best-run.spec.ts` (**13/13**) canaries the design in both directions: the record is replaced by a better per-day figure and **NOT** replaced by a bigger TOTAL that came from more days (the load-bearing case); a zero-day run records nothing and says why; a record under older execution semantics is replaced rather than compared; a failed write is reported and does not throw; an unreadable record is treated as absent WITHOUT claiming a comparison; and the key treats a missing bot as its own case rather than as an empty string. `src/core/best-record.test.js` (**10/10**) canaries the panel's three states — an absent `best` claims nothing, an empty store IS a first run, and a setting unreadable on either side is `unknown`, never `same`. Live on 0.172.1: `movers-winners-2026-11-20` recorded **606.00 per day / 606.00 over 1 day / 18 trades / 72.2%** with its 16-row config; a repeat run returned `improved: false`, `"no improvement — 606.00 per day is not above the record 606.00"`, left `achievedAt` untouched and moved `attempts` to **2**. ⚠️ **NOT done:** there is no "apply the record's settings" action — the record is visible and reproducible, not a one-click restore; and the panel's wiring has no DOM test. |

| 263 | **THE PAGE NOW LISTS WHAT IT ALREADY KNOWS AND DOES NOT SHOW — WITH THIS RUN'S OWN VALUES, SO THE DECISION CAN BE MADE ON DATA.** Owner 2026-11-23 required a list of what the protocols and bots record that the backtesting page does not use, so the choice of what to surface could be made deliberately. Filed as **#1340** and then rendered in the app, because an issue body is not where a decision about a UI gets made: `UnusedDataPanel` puts one row per field with the VALUE that run produced — `fillGap.measured: 0`, `priorSessions: 5`, `sideSource: protocol` — beside a sentence on what it would tell a reader. The list of fields is a DECLARATION (`src/core/backtest-unused-fields.js`) because whether a field is used is a fact about the source, not about the payload; what keeps it from rotting is that every value is read live, so a field the run stops returning renders **"not returned by this run"** rather than a stale example or a blank. ⚠️ ONLY PATHS THE PAYLOAD ACTUALLY CARRIES: `runCounters`, `sharpeRatio` and `maxDrawdown` exist on the per-symbol `backtestrun`, not on a selection run, and listing them here would add a permanently-absent row to a table whose job is to show what is available. ⚠️ **THE MEASUREMENT FOUND A REAL GAP WHILE BEING WRITTEN:** `fillGap` is computed and RETURNED on every selection run and rendered by NO surface (0 occurrences across the page and its four panels), so the stop-fill assumption has been measurable and unseen since it was built. ⚠️ `admission` is `null` in the default independent mode and the panel says so rather than hiding the row — the mode is a fact about the run, not an absence. | 2026-11-23 | 🟢 enforced — `src/core/backtest-unused-fields.test.js` (**7/7**) canaries the states that would otherwise fabricate a value: a declared field the run does not return is absent with `formatValue` null; a NULL value counts as absent (a run with nothing is not a run of zeros); a **zero** value is PRESENT, which a falsy check gets wrong; a nested path is read through its own parent and never borrowed from a sibling; and `formatValue` never leaks `[object Object]`, summarising objects, empty objects, empty arrays and blank strings distinctly. `npm run test:backtest-unused-fields` and `npm run test:best-record` registered so both are reachable. Verified live on 0.172.1: the inventory resolves **6 of 7 present** against a real `movers-winners-2026-11-20` run. ⚠️ **NOT guarded:** nothing fails if the DECLARATION goes stale in the other direction — a field added to the payload and rendered by the page would stay listed here until someone removes it; the live value only catches a field that disappears. |

| 264 | **EVERY SECTION IS NOW A NUMBERED STEP IN AN ACCORDION, AND THE PAGE HAS AN EXTENSION-SHAPED SPLIT.** Owner 2026-11-23: each section and table gets its own accordion, the accordions are to be examined as steps of the process, and the page file should be considered for a split into a shared backtest library plus files belonging to this strategy, moving toward the strategy extension. `src/components/StepAccordion.jsx` is the shared primitive: a native `<details>`/`<summary>` (keyboard operable, screen-reader announced, and find-in-page opens a closed section — a `div` accordion silently hides text from Ctrl+F, the opposite of what an examination page needs). ⚠️ **A COLLAPSED STEP MUST STILL ANSWER "IS THIS WORTH OPENING?"** — a bare title makes an accordion WORSE than a flat page, so every step declares a `purpose` (the QUESTION it answers, not a description) and the page passes `meta` figures drawn from that step's own data, visible while closed: `$606.00 net P/L · 18 trades · 72.2% win rate`, `6 symbols · 112 executions`, `best per day · not beaten`. `src/features/movers-strategy/steps.js` is the ordered list — **the order is the pipeline's order** (what was asked for → what came back → is it a record → per day → per symbol → per bar → what we did not show → the settings behind it) — and it carries the stored-state namespace `movers-backtest-v1`, versioned so a change to the list cannot reuse stale keys. Open state persists per step in `localStorage`, guarded: storage can throw in private mode and a preference that cannot be saved must never break a page. `openSignal` is a monotonic COUNTER, not a boolean, because the summary's "Change settings" button must open a step whose stored state is already true — a boolean cannot express "open it again" and the reader would press a button that does nothing. **The split:** what a second strategy would want unchanged stays in `src/components/` + `src/core/` (`StepAccordion`, `StatCard`, `TradeConfigPanel`, `BestRecordPanel`, `UnusedDataPanel`, `ExecutionsTable`, `DecisionsTable`, `useSort`); `src/features/movers-strategy/` now holds the step list, `format.js` (the page's `fmtPct`/`fmtMoney`/`dayLabel`, MOVED not copied, so the page and its extracted steps share one copy) and the two result tables (`ResultByDayStep`, `ResultBySymbolStep`). | 2026-11-23 | 🟢 enforced — `src/features/movers-strategy/steps.test.js` (**6/6**, `npm run test:movers-steps`) canaries the four ways a step list rots silently: a missing `purpose` (a collapsed step nobody can judge), a DUPLICATED id (which would leak one step's stored open state into another, and collide as a DOM anchor), a GAP in the numbering (which reads as a step that failed to render), and a namespace that is not versioned. Build gates: `npm run build` ✅, `validate-css-tokens` ✅, `validate-css-literals` ✅, `validate-css-parses` ✅ (204 stylesheets), `validate-css-imports` ✅, `validate-table-enrolment` ✅ (72 table classes), `validate-page-chrome` ✅ (no new chrome), `validate-duplication` ✅ **reduced** in one pair. ⚠️ **NOT guarded:** nothing asserts the accordions actually render, or that a step's `meta` matches its body — this repo has no DOM test harness, so the wiring is verified by a clean build and a read of the JSX, not by an observation. ⚠️ **Deliberately not extracted:** the settings FORM and the summary block (264 and 210 lines) stay in the page — both are entangled with page state and extracting them in the same pass as the accordions would have mixed a layout change with a state-plumbing change. Tracked in **#1343**. |

Measured cause: the written standards produced conformity rather than a shared look, and no rule NAMED a treatment — two pages could both "follow the standards" and still look unrelated. Nine element-group pages, `ThemeLabSections`, `theme-lab.css` and `components/appearance/*` were deleted; `/development/theme` now renders `src/pages/ThemePage.jsx`, twenty NAMED specimens (Dotted Stat, Summary Strip, Dense Table, Spacious Table, Toolbar, Chip Bar, Colour Lists, Detail Rail, Theory Box, Status Pills, Form Sections, Signal Card, Compact Numbers, Trade Card, Alert Card, Priority Panel, Input States, Snapshot Cards) with `.thm-*` styles in `theme-page.css`. | 266 | **THE ACCORDION BODY OWNS THE COLOURED SURFACE, AND THE PAGE'S INNER PANEL CONTAINERS ARE REMOVED RATHER THAN OVERRIDDEN.** Owner 2026-11-23: the inside of each accordion needs a coloured background taken from the existing stylesheet, and the inner container that was already coloured must go. Measured cause: the page paints a tinted surface with `.mvp-panel:has(.mvp-scroll)` (`color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 9%, var(--panel))`, 26% accent border) — specificity `(0,2,0)`, the SAME as `.step-acc__body > .mvp-panel`, and `MoversStrategyBacktestPage.css` loads AFTER the component's stylesheet, so the neutralising rule lost the tie and the step rendered a tinted box inside an untinted one. So the fix is structural, not stronger CSS: the two `<section className="mvp-panel">` wrappers in the page (run summary, bar by bar) became plain `<div>`s (or nothing), and `.step-acc__body` now carries the SAME declaration the page already used — same `color-mix` percentages, same accent, with the top border tinted to 26% so the surface reads as one piece. ⚠️ **A REMOVED `<section aria-label>` IS A REMOVED LANDMARK**, so the body is now `role="group"` with `aria-labelledby` pointing at the step title: a screen-reader user entering the content is told which step they are in, which the visual reader gets from the summary immediately above. ⚠️ Component roots that own their own chrome (`.brp`, `.mvp-block`) are still neutralised for their border/background, but that is now a fallback rather than the mechanism. | 2026-11-23 | 🟡 partial — `validate-css-tokens` ✅ and `validate-css-literals` ✅ prove the tint uses tokens and no literal, and `validate-css-parses` ✅ proves the rules are well formed. **Nothing asserts the surface is PAINTED**: a future edit that re-adds an inner `.mvp-panel` would double-paint again and every gate would stay green, because the failure is a specificity tie that only CSS cascade order decides. The next change here should either drop the page's `:has()` rule (leaving one surface rule in the shared component) or add a DOM test — this repo has neither today. |


---

## How to use this file

Before changing anything in a listed area, answer:

1. **Which row does this touch?**
2. **Am I fixing a bug, or re-deciding the approach?**
   - A **bug fix** implements a settled decision correctly → go ahead.
   - A **re-decision** changes it → **say so explicitly first** (issue or handoff), with what
     changed that invalidates the original reasoning. The original is in the `Settled` column.
3. **Is it 🔴 unguarded?** Then you are very likely to be re-opening it by accident. Read the
   original issue before writing code.

### ⚠️ Before filing an issue: is it actually a bug?

**Added 2026-09-11 after #795 was filed `priority-high` and closed as NOT-A-BUG the same session.**

Ask **"what is the cheapest piece of evidence that could falsify this?"** For #795 that was the
bridge log — one file read — and it showed a restart at `20:52`, three minutes before the gap. The
*pattern* (29 symbols, simultaneous) was real; the inference from it was wrong.

| Symptom | Ask FIRST | Expected during development |
|---|---|---|
| **Bar gap in the DB** | Did a bridge/API restart just happen? (`scripts/mt5-bridge/bridge-broker*.log`, last lines) | **YES** — and it **self-heals** on the next refresh (design decision **row 11**) |
| Bars stale / `asOf` behind | Do bars **exist** in the missing window? | If **no bars exist** → data gap, not a scoring bug (the probe now reports this separately) |
| Feed reading `closed` | Is the market actually closed? | **YES** — a market closure and an outage look identical |

**A guard that fails on an expected condition gets switched off, which is worse than no guard because
it looks like coverage.** That applies to *issues* as much as to code: an over-filed non-bug spends
human attention and teaches distrust of the reporting.

### The check that catches most loops

```
node scripts/detect-circular-work.cjs            # all watched areas
node scripts/detect-circular-work.cjs risk-gate  # one area
node scripts/diff-seed-vs-db.cjs                 # seed vs DB drift
```

If `detect-circular-work` shows an area with both CLOSED and OPEN issues, you are probably in case
(b) — a re-opening — and should cite the earlier decision rather than re-derive it.

---

## Open reconciliations (documented contradictions — do not implement against these)

| Contradiction | Where | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Checklist says "flat constants removed"; `ENTRY_THRESHOLD = 40` is live | #638 closure vs `protocol-gating.service.ts` L324 | Documented here 2026-09-11. The constant is percentage-based now (not the old absolute bug), so the *claim* was loose rather than false — but the checklist did not verify it |
| "Duplicate directive" warning means two opposite things | `ProtocolGatingService` L671 | Resolved in #789: real double-counting vs a valid copy shadowing a **dead** one. Only the group **kind** distinguishes them |
| `signals-workbench` is `active: 1` with **0 groups** | `protocol` table + `protocols-seed-data.ts` L2450 | **RESOLVED 2026-09-11 — NOT a defect.** It is a *workbench* protocol populated by `SignalEnvelopeService.persistScores()` (`score-history.service.ts` L54), not scored through the gated pipeline. The `ProtocolHealthService` "no risk-gate group" warning is therefore expected, not a fault. It has no groups **in the seed either** — seed and DB agree |
| `060ed261-…` protocol whose **slug is a UUID**, 0 groups | `protocol` + seed L2439 | **RESOLVED 2026-09-11 — abandoned placeholder.** A real protocol row ("Conviction Dip Accumulation", created 2026-07-26) that was never configured: `active: 0`, **0 groups in BOTH the seed and the DB**, and nothing references it. Seed and DB agree, so there is **no drift**. Left in place deliberately — deleting a `protocol` row is a data decision, and it is harmless (`active: 0`) |

Swapping in `ThreadingHTTPServer` is **not** one line: the bridge holds **NO LOCK** anywhere, `MetaTrader5` is not thread-safe, and adding a lock around MT5 calls would RE-INTRODUCE the block for `/health` (which itself calls MT5). | 45 | **An exit directive that has no direction declares `exitScoreThreshold`; silence is NOT authority.** A directive whose `signalClass` is correctly `neutral` for both sides cannot argue an exit through direction, so it declares the score at which its OWN reason text starts recommending one. It deliberately does **not** take `directionBias`: the first design keyed on it (`neutral` ⇒ read the score instead of the class), and that would have **silently disabled `chandelier-exit`** — which declares neutral bias while emitting real `bullish`/`bearish` and arguing **6** exits on live data. `directive.interface.ts` already documented why: *"a `neutral` directionBias is valid for a dynamic two-sided directive."* ⚠️ **Not maths:** no threshold that affects a scored, tradeable symbol moves; the verdict is ADVISORY — it places nothing, moves no stop, closes nothing. Verified as a **bug fix restoring declared intent**, not as new behaviour to be compared. ⚠️ **Not a re-open of row 44:** that settled what a mechanism IS; this settles how one SPEAKS. | #1020, 2026-09-29 (primary) | 🟢 `nest/src/modules/scoring/exit-consensus.service.spec.ts` — 12 tests, canaried in BOTH directions: removing the directional path fails exactly 4, removing the threshold path exactly 3, no compile error, baseline 12/12. Live: `arguesExitVia` is on the wire. |

| 46 | **Exit timing is NOT the lever, and the exit verdict must not be BUILT INTO AN ACTOR — the loss MAGNITUDE is the target.** Three independent replays of the trades we already have all fail to reach break-even, so *"the app loses because it exits too late"* is refuted and **no acting-exit build is authorised on that premise**. | 47 | **A position is addressed by the bridge that HOLDS it (`brokerId`), never by a URL captured at start-up and never by `symbol_route`.** | `getUrl()` with **no argument** returns the **PRIMARY** broker, so one captured URL silently scopes everything to one of two accounts. | 48 | **MT5 session recovery is kept as TWO mechanisms — the bridge's own watchdog (A) and the API's observer (B) — and B must not be deleted as redundant.** A status field must not collapse two conditions that need opposite responses. | ⚠️ **A future reader WILL see two reconnection mechanisms and assume one is dead code.** They are not the same fix, and #992 says so in its own words. **A** recovers while the bridge can still *poll* MT5, and is **bounded on purpose**: `WATCHDOG_TERMINAL_DOWN_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 5`, after which it **stops escalating and never tries again** — because reconnecting a switched-off terminal forever hides the real remedy. **Once A stops, nothing ever tries again**; on 2026-09-25 that cost ~1 hour of trading while the bridge logged 4,325 lines and named no remedy. **B** is an observer outside that process, acting on the case A can still speak about and has given up on. ⚠️ **B existed nowhere until the SIGNAL existed:** `Mt5BrokerInfo.reachable` is `ok && connected`, so `reachable:false` meant BOTH "the bridge did not answer" (posting `/reconnect` is futile — start it) AND "the bridge answered and its session is gone" (posting `/reconnect` is exactly the remedy). Opposite conditions, one boolean. `answered` now separates them and `connectionState` / `remedy` are carried through. ⚠️ **`reachable` KEEPS ITS MEANING** — `AlertTriggerService`, `SystemHealthService` and `automation.controller` read it, and re-defining a field others use is how a fix becomes an outage. | 49 | **A bridge bound to an account it was NOT configured for REFUSES orders — it does not merely warn. | 56 | **The 205 duplicate daily bars are KEPT, and the follow-on is a READ-TIME exclusion — never a delete.** | Extends **R32 / #1049** (which settled *fix the writer, do not delete the rows*). Applied to BOTH the DB read and the MT5 fallback, because that fallback is a second import path that bypasses `bar-refresh.service.ts` entirely and whose `skipDuplicates` only dedupes on the (symbol, timeframe, timestamp) KEY — which a repeated OHLCV at a NEW timestamp does not collide with. | 🟡 **Partial** — `npm --prefix nest run audit:data-health` reports the count (a WARNING, on demand, not in `prebuild`). The chosen bot's **own** rules apply unchanged — nothing new is computed, only **who governs** changes, so this is **not a maths change**. `GuardedPosition.claimants` + `ownerVia: 'comment:contested'` make the choice readable. | **#1041** | 🟢 **ENFORCED, canaried BOTH directions.** `stop-guardian.service.spec.ts` — **40/40** (6 new). Planting the pre-#1041 condition (`contested.length > 99`) fails **exactly** the canary test (1 failed, 39 passed); restoring passes 40/40 with no marker left. Asserts: the tightest claimant wins; unknown `slPct` sorts last; ties break on name; "nobody claims it" and "several do" stay distinguishable; a contested position is **guarded, not refused**. ⚠️ **Unit-proven, NOT live-verified** — no contested position has recurred. ⚠️ **Deliberately unchanged:** `position-time-stop.service.ts` still refuses a contested comment (`comment_no_match_multi_strategy`), so a contested position the guardian now trails is **still not time-stopped** — a second behaviour change on the same ruling, owed its own decision. |
| 60 | **A PEAK is MONOTONIC and must be STORED, never reconstructed — and an unobserved peak is NULL, not zero.** | Requirement (2026-09-25): another later-afternoon to evening drift into the red after sitting with profit… it was steady and we bled that instead of slowly bringing trailing stop up. The give-back was real but **unreadable from the database** — it had to be RECONSTRUCTED from `marketbar` highs, because no maximum-favourable-excursion column existed anywhere, and the only peak in the code was an in-memory `Map` (`simTslWatermark`) explicitly declared ephemeral and gated behind `simTslEnabled`, which **0 of 39 bots had set**. There was nothing to measure against, so the issue could never be re-verified. Settled: `position.peakPrice` + `position.peakProfitPct` are written per refresh, and copied to `trade.peakPrice`/`trade.peakProfitPct` at close (the `position` row does not survive closure, so a peak kept only there is lost exactly when it matters). Three rules: **(1) MONOTONIC** — a lower later reading must never lower the peak; a peak that can go down is a last-price field with a misleading name. **(2) AN UNKNOWN MUST NOT ERASE A KNOWN PEAK** — `null`/`NaN` means "could not price it", not "the peak was lower"; the copy to `trade` is conditional for the same reason, so a repair pass cannot overwrite a live peak with nothing. **(3) NULL ≠ 0** — "no peak observed" and "the peak was break-even" are different facts. **Not a maths change:** nothing here sizes, gates or places anything. | **#998** | 🟡 **PARTIAL, and the gap is stated rather than implied.** The rule is **enforced**: `peak.spec.ts` **19/19**, canaried both directions by replacing `nextPeak`'s body with `return candidate` → 4 tests fail (the monotonic ones); restoring passes with no marker left. `db:check` ✅ and `db:schema-drift` ✅ (columns modelled, no drift). ⚠️ **THE FIRST PLACEMENT OF THE WRITER WAS WRONG AND IS THE REUSABLE LESSON.** It went in `PriceRefreshService.refreshPrices()`, which is reachable **only** from an HTTP endpoint and the import orchestrator — **nothing schedules it**. It was correct, tested, deployed, and **0 of 26 positions ever got a peak**, with no error and no log line: a **buried ability** (#1065). Fixed 2026-10-02 by moving the write to `PositionSyncService`, which already receives a **live broker price** for every open position on a cadence that actually runs — *"using what has already been imported"* (Requirement (2026-10-02)). **No second price fetch was added**, because a separate timer would have paid a provider call for a price the sync loop had already asked for, and given one field two sources that can disagree. ⚠️ **A weakness in the guard was found by canarying, not by review:** the "a lower price must not lower the peak" test asserted only **absence** of a write, so it also passed with the writer **deleted** — it could not tell *"considered and declined"* from *"the feature is gone"*. It now also asserts the **reader ran** (`select.peakPrice`), and removing the call fails **both** peak tests instead of one. The **decay** half of #998 is a maths change and is deliberately **not built**. |

| 61 | **The bar refresh stores ONLY bars the bridge SERVED. A synthetic bar is never written to `marketbar`.** | Found 2026-10-03 while answering an unrelated question. `doRefresh()` ended with a loop that called `getQuote()`, took `(bid+ask)/2`, floored the **local clock** to the current 5m bucket and INSERTed a bar: `provider 'mt5'`, `timeframe '5m'`, `o=h=l=c`, **`volume 1`**. **Measured: 1,172 rows across 52 symbols since Friday's close, 100% flat, 100% `volume <= 1`**, first `21:05` last `13:50` — while the bridge's own `/candles` returned **nothing after Fri 20:54**. **The proof:** `GET :5000/quote?symbol=XAUUSD` → `bid 4147.62 / ask 4147.97` → mid **4147.795**, the exact value in every one of those rows. 84 distinct closes (NZDUSD `0.561545` → US30 `51217`), so **not** cross-symbol contamination — a frozen quote per symbol. **Why it matters:** the bar is stamped at the CURRENT bucket, so `MAX(timestamp)` is always ~now and **a feed that died on Friday measures FRESH on Monday** — plausible values, no error. ⚠️ **It also countermanded a guard that was working:** the same run correctly reported `SKIPPED 209 stale bar set(s)` (#838) and then wrote fresh-looking rows for those same symbols, bypassing `isStaleSet`, `alignBarTimestamp` **and** `isBarFutureStamped`. **Two gates were rejected BY MEASUREMENT, not preference:** `quote.timestamp` cannot gate it (it is the bridge's **answer** time — `13:52:05.876Z` against a clock of `13:52:06.4` — so a frozen quote is indistinguishable from a live one), and *"only when the feed has a current bar"* is identical to deleting it. Deletion also removes a second harm: the loop ran LAST, overwriting a real forming bar's `close` with a quote **mid**. | **#1068** | 🟢 **ENFORCED, canaried BOTH directions.** `bar-refresh-no-synthetic.spec.ts` — **6/6**, and planting the synthetic row fails **3 of 6**. The spec carries a **positive control** so the detector is proven able to fail independently of the service. ⚠️ **The first canary was INERT and that is the reusable lesson:** it inlined the literals, so the `$executeRaw` mock recorded **one** value instead of nine and checked `undefined` against the signature — and the suite went GREEN, which would have read as *"the guard cannot fail"*. **Every field must be interpolated for a canary to land.** ⚠️ **The pre-existing suite could not have caught this:** its adapter mock omits `getQuote` and says so — *"No `getQuote` → the synthetic-quote loop is skipped, so it cannot add writes."* **A mock shaped so the defect cannot fire makes the suite indistinguishable from one that passes.** **Data repaired:** the 1,172 rows deleted; verified `0` remain and XAUUSD 5m newest is now `20:50 / close 4139.36 / volume 623` — byte-identical to the bridge. ⚠️ **55,518 rows share the signature BEFORE the close and are NOT deleted** — same writer almost certainly, but the market was open so "no bar can exist" does not apply. **Indication, not proof. Owner: #1068.** |

| 62 | **`apiactivitylog` gets a 30-day retention window — and it was the ONLY large table with none.** | Requirement 2026-10-03, given the 7/30/90 fork: nothing is reading 90 days and might not for a long time… 30 days by default is suitable. **Measured 2026-10-03:** the table was **1,332,329 rows / 249 MB** — the largest in the database — and the only one of the top ten with no declared window (the next undeclared was 18 MB, **14× smaller**). A retention system already existed for **10** tables and its prune was verified **working** (`directiveoutcome` sat at ~3.9 days against its declared 3-day window), so this was a missing entry, not a missing mechanism. `timestamp` is an intrinsic write-time column and is indexed. ⚠️ **The DEFAULT, not a ceiling** — `DATA_RETENTION_APIACTIVITY_DAYS` overrides it. ⚠️ **The project's second half is NOT built:** framing long-term storage as an **opt-in feature the user switches on for analysis** (*"the user has to initiate long term data storage"*) is a different size of change from a default bound, and only the bound was decided. ⚠️ **AND THE SAME TABLE EXPOSED A LARGER DEFECT — #1070 (with a correction on the issue):** `marketbar`'s 60-day bound **IS enforced per series** — measured 2026-10-03, every intraday timeframe trims at exactly 60 days. **Only `1d` reaches deeper (296 days), and by ACCIDENT:** the refresh upserts 200 bars per timeframe each cycle, so daily bars are re-written continuously, over-supplying what the prune removes. `btc-halving-cycle`, in the **ACTIVE** `btc-speed-v1`, declares **365** daily bars and we hold **206** — starved, silently. ⚠️ **My first version of this note said the bound was "not applied". It was wrong**, and the method is the lesson: I read the GLOBAL `MIN(timestamp)` (296d, set by `1d` alone) and inferred "nothing prunes". **A global aggregate hid a per-series rule.** A per-timeframe window is the shape that would make the daily depth deliberate rather than incidental. | **Requirement (2026-10-03)** | 🟡 **Config-guarded, not behaviour-guarded.** `nest/scripts/validate-retention-columns.cjs` passes **11/11** — intrinsic column, one owner, usable index — and it runs in `prebuild`, so a *mis-declared* bound fails the build. What it cannot check is that the prune **reaches** a 249 MB table in budget; that is unverified until the next natural prune cycle reports. |
| 63 | **`symbol.active` is the CORE-SET switch, and the bar refresh must honour it — EXCEPT for a symbol we hold or trade.** | Requirement 2026-10-03: reduce the app down to 40 - the ones already active for starters, and the ones that are already fully active, then any that are easily supported… I think 40 right now allows us erase a lot of redundant data. **Measured:** the union of every way a symbol can be *used* (has a bot ∪ assigned to a protocol ∪ routed ∪ the 5-pair FOREX_FLOOR) is **39 symbols** — so the project's core-40 **already existed, defined by usage**. **128** symbols held bars; **~89** were ingesting for no reason. ⚠️ **`symbol.active` was a buried ability on this path:** it gated the NEWS tier (`resolveAllActive`) and `getStarvedSymbols()` (`WHERE s.active = 1`), but **not** `bar-refresh`, whose tiers are positions + active-strategies + forex-floor + protocol-symbols — so setting it stopped news and left bars flowing. **Applied:** 260 → **57 active** (39 core + 18 held), **verified 0 held symbols deactivated**. ⚠️ **THE CARVE-OUT IS SAFETY-CRITICAL AND MUST NOT BE SIMPLIFIED AWAY:** a symbol with an open position or an active bot is fed **whatever its flag says** — starving a held position's feed stops the stop guardian pricing it, and the symptom would be *"the stop did not fire"* with nothing pointing back. The protected tier reuses the existing `'automation'` resolution rather than defining "a symbol we care about" a fourth time. **Fails OPEN** — if the active read throws, the full list survives, because a starved feed is worse than a needless fetch. **Reversible:** `logs/r37-apply-core-set.cjs --revert`. | **Requirement (2026-10-03)** | 🟢 **ENFORCED, canaried.** `bar-refresh-no-synthetic.spec.ts` — **9/9** in that suite (**31/31** across all three bar-refresh suites). Removing the carve-out fails exactly **1** test (the held-symbol case), with the canary confirmed present before the failure was trusted. Three cases: an inactive unprotected symbol is dropped; an **inactive HELD symbol is kept**; a failed active read **fails open**. ⚠️ **The sibling suite had to be fixed to make this real:** its mock omitted `symbol.findMany`, so the read threw and every test silently exercised the FALLBACK rather than the filter — the same "mock shaped so the code under test cannot run" defect that hid the synthetic-bar writer the same day. ⚠️ **Data removal NOT done:** the ~89 symbols' accumulated bars are still present; deleting them is a separate step, deliberately run only after ingestion was observed to stop — the #R32/#R39 lesson that deleting first races a writer that is still running. |

| 64 | **Retention has TWO CLASSES with OPPOSITE reasons: ACTIVITY is short, EVIDENCE is long. A single "retention policy" number cannot serve both.** | Requirement (2026-10-04), correcting my reading: *"When I said 60 days I meant general trading history and app activity — not strictly meaning everything or including bars for backtesting. I understand the benefit of storing a year or even more of bar data and potentially key news headlines, articles etc. For the purpose of backtesting relevant directives that read that."* **CLASS 1 — ACTIVITY AND TRADING HISTORY (short):** `directiveoutcome`, `score`, `score_snapshot`, `strategy_evaluation`, `decision_log`, `automation_event`, `portfolio_snapshot`, `order`, `bridge_event`, `apiactivitylog`. These record **what the app did**; nothing downstream reads them as input, so depth buys only diagnostics. **CLASS 2 — EVIDENCE A DIRECTIVE READS (long):** `marketbar` and news. ⚠️ **THE REASON IS OPPOSITE, not merely different:** a directive declares its required depth (`minBars`), so **a window here that is tighter than the deepest declaration STARVES that directive SILENTLY** — a short bar array is a legitimate state (a new symbol has no history), so nothing errors and the composite simply loses the vote. **THE RULE: a window in Class 2 must not be tighter than the deepest `minBars` of any directive that can run against it.** **APPLIED:** `marketbar` **60 → 365 days** — the 60 was MINE, not the project's; they had been asking about activity, and the binding declaration is `btc-halving-cycle` at **365 daily bars** in the **ACTIVE** `btc-speed-v1`, which 60 could never satisfy (#1070). ⚠️ **NEWS IS DELIBERATELY UNBOUNDED, and that IS the ask** — *"storing"* is the requirement, not *"pruning at N days"*, and a backtest cannot retrieve what was pruned because the source serves only recent items. Both bounds were attempted and **both were correctly rejected, for two different reasons** (see below). | **Requirement (2026-10-04)** | 🟢 **ENFORCED structurally.** `nest/scripts/validate-retention-columns.cjs` — **11/11**, exit 0, runs in `prebuild`. It enforces BOTH required properties: the column must be **intrinsic** (write-time, not source-supplied) AND the **leading column of an index**. ⚠️ **Two correct rejections, kept because the reasons outlast the attempt:** (1) `publishedAt` is when the ARTICLE was published, not when WE stored it — a two-year-old article fetched today would be deleted on arrival; (2) `newsarticle.importedAt` is the right COLUMN but not in the validator's name list, and **MEASURED — of the 6 columns named `importedAt`, only 3 are DB-populated; `watchlist` and two backups are app-written and nullable** — so adding the bare name would also license a bound on `watchlist.importedAt`, the exact defect the guard exists to prevent. **A name whitelist cannot express a per-column fact, so it was NOT widened.** |

| 65 | **`BarRefreshService.refresh()` is SINGLE-FLIGHT — a second caller JOINS the running cycle, it does not start a second.** | MEASURED 2026-10-02 in the running API: every bar fetch **and** upsert appeared **TWICE, interleaved, under ONE pid** (`MT5: fetching 200 candles for EURCAD (1m)` ×2, for every symbol and timeframe, MT5 and binance alike) and one automation tick took **24.9 minutes**. Two API instances were RULED OUT — one listener on :3201, one `dist/src/main.js`, and both copies carry the same `[Nest] 5588` prefix — as were two earlier hypotheses on the issue (ticks cannot overlap; `setIntervalMs()` clears before re-arming). **Cause: TWO INDEPENDENT DRIVERS sharing one method.** `start()` owns a 5-minute timer AND the automation loop calls `refresh()` once per tick; neither knew about the other and `refresh()` had no in-flight guard, so a slow cycle and the next driver's cycle ran concurrently over the same symbols and rows. **Fixed at the ONE function they share, NOT at the callers** — both drivers are wanted (the timer refreshes when no tick runs, the per-tick call refreshes during a tick). Reuses the pattern `symbols.service.ts` already established for the same shape rather than inventing a third. ⚠️ **JOINS, does not skip.** Returning `{ upserted: 0 }` early would tell a caller a refresh ran and wrote nothing — "wrote nothing" and "did not run" are different facts. | **#1067** | 🟢 **ENFORCED, canaried.** `bar-refresh-single-flight.spec.ts` — **3/3**, and **34/34** across all four bar-refresh suites. Disabling the guard fails exactly **1** test with `Expected 6, Received 12` — **the #1067 doubling reproduced as an assertion**, with the canary marker confirmed present before the failure was trusted. The spec asserts **concurrency** specifically: a sequential-calls assertion would pass against the broken code, so the first cycle is held open by a controlled gate and the second caller arrives inside it. It also pins that a NEW cycle still runs once the previous finishes, so the guard cannot latch and starve every later refresh. ⚠️ **The FIRST version of the spec blocked the first fetch in EVERY test**, so the sequential cases awaited a gate nothing released and timed out — the suite failed while the CODE was correct. A mock that changes global behaviour to set up one case makes every other case test the mock. |

## Settled 2026-10-05 — the architecture map STOPS AT D (Phase 4 of #1071)

| # | Decision | What it means | Settled | Guard status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 66 | **The architecture map STOPS AT D — the generated dataset IS the deliverable.** B renders it (Phase 3) and no fifth architecture surface is added. | Phase 4 scored the three approaches against the three questions the plan set. **C+D answers 3 of 3:** #1068's writers (**8**, including the raw-SQL one a Prisma-only scan cannot see); R37's no-consumer (`order` — 6 write sites, **0 readers**; plus **129** node-level candidates, 78 with no same-name call anywhere); #1069's duplicate maths (**43** exact groups, **17** divergent, **1** idiom group of 5 sites — one of which, `exit-consensus.service.ts:374`, the manual audit had **not** listed). **B answers 0 of 3** — its nodes declare *tables*, not writers; they say nothing about consumers; and a value that touches no table at all is invisible to it. ⚠️ **B IS NOT DELETED, and that is not the reading.** It is the only surface rendering **LIVE** health (ticks, provider status, breaker state), which a static dataset cannot. What this row forbids is a *second hand-maintained node list*. **Cost carried, measured:** the prebuild gate takes **6.8 s** against 0.2 s + 0.1 s for the two it sits beside; it stales on any change to what touches the eleven resources or any new ratio; the maths scan is **ratios-only**; and each node's **file list is still hand-written** — Phase 3 measured the *Data Pipeline* node declaring three files, **none of which writes a tracked resource**, while the pipeline plainly writes `marketbar` through `import-jobs.service.ts`, which the node never lists. | **#1071, Phase 4, 2026-10-05** | 🟡 **PARTIAL, and the gap is stated.** `generate-pipeline-map.cjs --check` in prebuild fails on a stale artefact, and `--self-test` canaries the three known defects in **23/23** cases. ⚠️ **Neither can detect the thing this row actually forbids:** a new hand-maintained surface fails no gate at all. |

## Settled 2026-10-07 — an A/B live pair needs TWO accounts, or it places NO orders (#1079)

| # | Decision | What it means | Settled | Guard status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 67 | **The live snapshot trial (`ab_test`) resolves TWO DISTINCT ACCOUNTS, one per arm, or it places NO orders.** The shared resolution is REMOVED, not defaulted around. | **The line that was there:** `const brokerId = (controlCfg.brokerId ?? variantCfg.brokerId ?? 'mt5') as string` — ONE account for BOTH arms, with a fallback chain ending in a literal. **Measured 2026-10-07** against the two live snapshots of run `b50727ee` (`SELECT JSON_EXTRACT(snapshot,'$.brokerId') FROM metastack_snapshot WHERE id IN ('038d897c…','3c299cec…')`): **both returned `'mt5'`.** ⚠️ **This is row 23's netting seen from the ORDER SIDE**, and it cites row 23: with one account behind both arms the broker nets them, so the arms are one position wearing two labels — the entry collision row 23 measured (three orders → one position of 0.04) and #1080 measured again in the books (~9 orders → one netted position). ⚠️ **This row does NOT re-open row 23 and does NOT make the live pair measurable** — row 26 stands unchanged: **the measurement is the BACKTEST**, and the live pair keeps its LOCK (row 25) as the **final confirmation step**. What this row adds is the precondition for that confirmation to be *safe*, not a route back to measuring live. ⚠️ **It is NOT a re-offer of the "hedging account" fix row 23 RETRACTED** — that was about MT5 *position-accounting type*, a different thing; this is two separate accounts, so the arms cannot net. **Requirement 2026-10-07** (`external-systems-are-data.instructions.md`): *"The pair must refuse to run when it cannot get two distinct, viable accounts — never fall back to one."* **Consequence today, stated plainly: the pair refuses, because there is no way to bind two distinct accounts** — `ab_test` has NO account column, `broker_config` is capability-only, and accounts live as `MT5_ACCOUNT_n` env vars (#1078). **The refusal is LOUD**: a `WARN` carrying the reason, throttled to once per hour per test (the check runs every tick; ~1,440 identical lines a day is a warn-storm), so a pair left misconfigured overnight is re-reported rather than swallowed. ⚠️ **What it does NOT fix, and those stay open on #1079:** the orders still bypass `dispatchTrade()`'s safety chain (cooldowns, duplicate guard, diversification, portfolio-risk gate — the gap tracked by **#1082**, the missing app-level order choke point), and they are still **not time-stoppable** (`position-time-stop.service.ts` matches the BOT NAME in the broker comment; `TCS AB-A …` matches no bot). Two accounts is a precondition, not the whole of #1079. ⚠️ **AND IT ENFORCES THE "DISTINCT" HALF OF THE PROJECT'S WORDING, NOT THE "VIABLE" HALF** — the check requires two present, different, non-blank account names; it does **not** yet verify that either can actually trade the pair's symbol. Stated because the project's own sentence says *"two distinct, viable accounts"*, and a reader would otherwise assume both halves are machine-checked. Distinctness is the half that fixes the measured netting; viability needs an account record (#1078) — the requirement has already measured that *"both current accounts … will not trade the same symbols on each"*, so it is a per-symbol question, not a name check. | **requirement 2026-10-07 → #1079** | 🟢 **ENFORCED, both ways.** `ab-arm-accounts.spec.ts` — **8 tests**, canaried in **4** directions with the tree verified byte-for-byte on restore: restoring the shared resolution fails the source guard · deleting the same-account refusal fails 2 tests · dropping the trim fails 2 (so `' mt5 '` cannot pass as a distinct account) · handing both sides account A fails the source guard. The source guard was **narrowed twice**, each time because it was observed firing on something it does not own — its own explanatory comment, then the BOT's unrelated `strategy.brokerId || 'mt5'` — since a guard that fires on unrelated code is allowlisted within a week. ⚠️ **The type does NOT enforce the call site:** `nest/tsconfig.json` sets `strictNullChecks: false`, which erases `null` and collapses the union, so `if (!accounts.ok)` was measured NOT to narrow (tsc: *"Property 'refusal' does not exist"*). The source check is what holds the call site. |

## Settled 2026-10-07 — a breached risk cap ANNOUNCES itself, and the two caps are announced DIFFERENTLY (#1086)

| # | Decision | What it means | Settled | Guard status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 68 | **A breached risk cap announces itself — and the two caps are announced DIFFERENTLY: `maxDailyLoss` is attended, `maxPortfolioRisk` is not.** | **The measurement.** `decision_log` 2026-10-07: `Daily loss $178.02 exceeds risk.maxDailyLoss ($100)` refused **440** orders across **13** symbols and `Portfolio at risk 17.7–18.3% exceeds risk.maxPortfolioRisk 15%` **~282** across **~10**. Executions that day: **7**. The app was locked out of new entries for most of the day and **nothing said so** — `risk.maxDailyLoss` had exactly two readers (the entry gate, and a health-page comparison of app-setting vs bridge-limit), and neither raised anything. This is `development-philosophy §7`: correct behaviour, invisible. **⚠️ NO THRESHOLD MOVED.** `100` and `0.15` are unchanged — a cap gates a trading decision, so changing one is *the maths* and needs a copied configuration and a comparison (`maths-change-requires-ab`). This row is **visibility only**. **The asymmetry is the decision, and it is not cosmetic:** `maxDailyLoss` is **SELF-BLOCKING** — you cannot trade out of it, because opening a position is the very thing it blocks; it clears only if a position **already open** closes green enough to bring the day back above the cap, or at local midnight. ⚠️ **This row first said "a LATCH — cannot clear until local midnight", and that was WRONG**: `dailyPnl` is recomputed from every trade closed today, so a green close raises it back above the cap and the gate reopens mid-day. Corrected 2026-10-07 after the brief asked what a latch is — *a claim in a row is still a claim, and this one was in an operator-facing alert*. `maxPortfolioRisk` is a **CAPACITY STATE** — it clears itself as positions close or their stops tighten, and a Windows toast for a condition that resolves on its own is how the toast channel gets ignored. So only the self-blocking cap enters `ATTENTION_KEYS`; both appear in the Alert Center. **It READS the recorded refusal rather than re-deriving the number** — the row already carries the cap, the figure and the accountable positions, and recomputing the day's P&L or the book's at-risk figure in the alerts module would be a second implementation of a money calculation. **It fires on a PERSISTED breach** (≥5 refusals in a 30-minute window), so a momentary over-cap on one order stays quiet. **⚠️ NOTHING ESCALATES, and that was considered rather than skipped:** auto-escalating to `emergencyStop()` would also halt the **Stop Guardian** (trailing-stop tightening on open positions) precisely when losses are running — and it still could not reach a path that bypasses the caps (**#1082**). ⚠️ **Two stated limits:** the check can only fire when something TRIED to trade, so it reports a *count of refusals* rather than claiming the cap is breached generally; and it surfaces in the Alert Center, the OS toast and stdout but **not** the canonical structured log, because `AlertTriggerService` writes through the NestJS logger. | **requirement 2026-10-07 → #1086** | 🟢 **ENFORCED, both ways.** `alert-risk-cap-breach.spec.ts` — **8 tests**; `npx jest src/modules/alerts` → **4 suites / 28 tests pass**. Canaried **5 ways, all 5 observed to fail, tree byte-for-byte on restore** (`logs/canary-1086-risk-alerts-v2.ps1`): dropping the below-the-bar guard · collapsing the two caps into one · removing the latch from `ATTENTION_KEYS` · windowing on `NOW()` instead of `UTC_TIMESTAMP()` · putting the count in the title. ⚠️ **The `UTC_TIMESTAMP()` and stable-title rules are guarded by SOURCE checks**, because both failure modes are SILENT — an hour-shifted window reports nothing at all, and a count in the title fractures one condition into a row per count (corrected for `symbol-silent` on 2026-09-23). ⚠️ **Both canaries in the first run were WRONG IN THE SCRIPT, not the guard** — PowerShell collapses `''` to one quote, and a title mutation injected a literal `${n}` in a single-quoted string — and the harness refused to report on an un-made edit rather than passing. |

---

## Settled 2026-10-08 — the deal lookup asks EVERY active broker, and MERGES (#1089)

| # | Decision | What it means | Settled | Guard status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 69 | **`getDeals()` fans out across EVERY active broker and MERGES the result — a broker that answers nothing is WARNED about, and finalisation is NOT deferred for it (yet).** | **The defect this settles.** `getDeals()` called `bridgeGet('/history')` with NO route → `route?.url ?? this.bridgeUrl` → `MT5_BRIDGE_URL` = `:5000` = broker 2. Unblock: review-queue **R48** + a repair re-run; tracked open in #1089. The run also exposed a tool defect (the pass stamped the RUN TIME over `closedAt` on rows it could not re-price — 50 rows, all restored; fixed + guarded) — **#1091**. | 2026-10-08 (#1089) | 🟢 `mt5-adapter-multibroker.spec.ts` — `getDeals` asks `:5000` **and** `:5001`, merges, warns on a silent broker, keeps the single-URL no-router path · canary `logs/canary-1089-getdeals-fanout.ps1` — mutated (single URL) = **2 failed**, restored = **8 passed**, byte-for-byte restore verified · **build-gate rule 5** in `nest/scripts/validate-single-bridge-position-io.cjs` (getDeals must delegate; the fan-out must enumerate `brokerIds` and pass a route; 5 self-test cases; tree canary mutated → exit 1, restored → exit 0) |

## 🔒 Settled subjects — naming one REQUIRES citing the row that settled it

> ⚠️ **Why this exists (2026-09-28).** An agent re-derived **row 23's** A/B netting finding as if it
> were new, wrote it into row 43, and asked the requirement a question row 23 records as *explicitly
> ended*. `detect-circular-work.cjs` reported **"No topic reached the 3-touch threshold"** — that
> detector tracks ISSUE re-opens, not an agent re-deriving a REGISTER entry — so **nothing caught
> it**. The answer existed, in this file, and reading it was optional.
>
> **The rule:** a row that names a subject below must ALSO cite the row that settled it. A citation
> costs four characters. Re-deciding by accident is the most expensive thing that happens here.
>
> ⚠️ **Terms are deliberately NARROW.** `merge` / `collision` / `snapshot trial` were measured and
> EXCLUDED: they legitimately appear in rows 24 and 26, which do not cite row 23, so including them
> would make this guard red from birth — and a guard red from birth gets ignored. The terms kept
> below were measured to hit ONLY the settling row and the row that already cites it.

| Subject | Terms (any match, case-insensitive) | Settled by |
|---|---|---|
| The A/B live pair cannot produce a result — MT5 nets | `netting`, `\bnets\b`, `hedging` | row 23 |
| The A/B row-shape / verdict menu | `(a)/(b)/(c)`, `a/b/c` | row 23 |
