| 72 | **`broker:unknown` on a CLOSED row is the ABSENCE of evidence, so a repair-path re-finalisation MAY overwrite it — every OTHER reason is never overwritten.** | **The defect this settles (#1093):** the #1089 repair staged rows by clearing the reason, but `finalizeClosedPosition` wrote `broker:unknown` BACK for any row it could not yet price — so a later recovery could never replace it, and 21 recoverable rows stayed unattributed. A mechanism that NAMED itself at close time (`broker:sl`, `user:manual`, `system:time-stop`) is strictly better evidence than anything derivable on a later tick, so those stay sacred. **INSTEAD OF** re-deriving freely on every repair tick. **REVERSE BY** deleting the `rederivable` term in `finalizeClosedPosition` (`const rederivable = opts.allowClosed && existingReason === CLOSE_REASONS.BROKER_UNKNOWN;`) — `closeReason` then keeps its existing value for every closed row, exactly as before. **Measured 2026-10-08:** with the rule + the #1093 fixes, the in-app recovery closed **34 of the 81** ticketed gaps (192 → 158 total; 111 more carry no ticket and can never be chased, and 47 sit beyond the Pepperstone terminal's ~3-day deal history). ⚠️ **The first version of this row recorded the final 13 as "deals with no mappable reason" — MEASURED FALSE the same day**: their closing deals carried reason 4 in the terminal, and the resolution never reached them because `getDeals`' `since` filter dropped the OPENING deal (`openedAt` is app-sighting time, later than the broker's fill — the order→position bridge existed only in that deal). Fixed with `DEAL_LOOKBACK_GRACE_MS` (ticketed rows only) plus permanent repair re-derivation diagnostics; all 13 then attributed. A note may state what was observed; the cause it first asserted had never been measured. | 🟢 **GUARDED.** `position-sync-ticketed-reap.spec.ts` pins both directions (⭐ re-derives `broker:unknown`; does NOT re-derive a named reason) and `logs/canary-1093-recovery.ps1` observes M1 fail on the mutation and pass on restore, byte-for-byte. The recovery path itself is pinned by `position-sync-exit-recovery.spec.ts` (the count means gap-closed, not finaliser-ran) and rule 5 of `validate-single-bridge-position-io.cjs` (#1093 extension). |

| 73 | **FX/CFD weekday closures come from a CURATED STATIC TABLE (`FX_HOLIDAYS` in `market-calendar.service.ts`), consulted by the WEEKLY-session path — universal closures only (25 Dec, 1 Jan), and the weekly path warns rather than silently ignoring anything else.** | **The defect this settles (#1088):** the weekly-session path had NO holiday axis, so **Christmas Day 2026 (a Friday) read as OPEN** for every instrument riding the FX venue — all index CFDs, metals and FX pairs (`venueForSymbol()`). Fail-OPEN: instruments wrongly admitted, beside #850's instruments wrongly refused. **Why a table and not the broker:** measured 2026-10-08, the MT5 Python binding exposes NO session/holiday API (`dir(mt5)` — the symbol family is only `symbol_info`, `symbol_info_tick`), so a broker-reported calendar cannot be read without inventing a probe; an `order_check` probe is heavy per gate evaluation and carries #824's filling-mode trap — recorded as a future option, not used. **INSTEAD OF** guessing family-specific dates (Thanksgiving for US500/US30 needs a per-family venue split) or declaring 24/31 Dec early closes the broker may or may not honour. **REVERSE BY** deleting `FX_HOLIDAYS` and the holiday branch in `getWeeklyStatus`. **Measured:** 25 Dec 2026 → closed, named, next open Sun 27 Dec 22:00Z; 24 Dec stays a full day (the declared early-close gap); the two venues provably read their own tables. | 🟢 **GUARDED.** 4 cases in `market-calendar.service.spec.ts` ('FX venue — weekday holidays'); `logs/canary-1088-fx-holidays.ps1` observes both halves fail independently (M1 table row, M2 weekly check — M2 alone also measured) and restores byte-for-byte. |

| 76 | **Per-symbol realised P&L is read from `PnlReport.bySymbol` (`GET /api/scoring/edge/pnl`) — NEVER from `/api/journal/stats`.** | **The defect this settles (#1098):** Signals Overview's "Past profit" ordering read `/api/journal/stats`, on the note that `journal.service` "already builds `bySymbol`". It builds the **shape** — over an **empty in-memory array** it never populates. Measured 2026-10-09: `totalTrades: 0`, `bySymbol: []` against **440 closed `trade` rows**, with `winRate` shipped as the literal string `"NaN%"` (from `0/0`). So the control was disabled and labelled "no data" **while the data existed**. **An empty AGGREGATE is not evidence that the SOURCE is empty** — the source must be read before absence is reported; this is the same shape as #758's observed-vs-configured denominator, and it is why the earlier 2026-10-08 note in that file ("the journal has no closed trades to summarise yet") was wrong. `PnlReport` is the canonical computation — quarantine-aware (excludes `pnlInvalid`, the #805 fabrication) with `reliability` bands — and answers with real rows: measured the same day, `XAUUSD` 54 trades, `winRate` 0.3519, `totalPnl` −1047.8657, `reliability: "moderate"`. **INSTEAD OF** wiring `JournalService` to Prisma, which would re-derive realised P&L and duplicate the `pnlInvalid` exclusion and the reliability bands — two copies drift and only one receives the next fix. **REVERSE BY** deleting the DECISION marker in `SignalsOverviewPage.jsx` and restoring the `/api/journal/stats` read with `queryKey: ['sig-journal-stats']`. ⚠️ **NOT fixed by this row, and the more serious half:** `journal.service` remains a stub that **fabricates** four statistics as if they described the account (`bestDay: 'Wednesday (71% win rate)'`, `worstDay: 'Friday (40% win rate)'`, `topStrength`, `topWeakness`), and `getEntries()` returns `[]` so the Trade Journal page is empty too — filed as **#1100**. | 🔴 **UNGUARDED.** Nothing fails if the stub is read again: no test pins the source, and the failure is silent (a plausible `0`, not an error). |

| 79 | **Runtime symbol registration is a RECORD OF USER DATA — written to the database, deliberately NOT seeded, and therefore lost on a reinstall. The SHIPPED catalogue is what gets seeded.** | **The matter this settles (#1098 item 2.6):** `POST /api/symbols` (→ `SymbolsService.create()`) writes a `symbol` row straight to the database, and `database-seed-discipline.instructions.md` states that a direct DB write which is not in a seeder is *a change to one machine*. That rule is about **config vanishing silently**; it does **not** mean this write belongs in a seed file. What a **user** registers is not the shipped catalogue, and putting user data into a repo seeder would make one user's registrations part of everyone's install — which is the opposite of the rule in `external-systems-are-data.instructions.md` (an external system is a record with a lifecycle, created from the UI). The two things are therefore different and both exist: **SHIPPED CATALOGUE = seeded** (`seed-symbol-onboarding.ts`; the broker's own catalogue is synced into `broker_symbol` — #958 measured 12,373 offered, 218 onboarded), **WHAT A USER REGISTERS = a record.** **INSTEAD OF** writing registrations into a seeder or adding a second import path. **REVERSE BY** adding an export/import route for registrations (export the rows, re-import after a reinstall) — a feature, and its own decision. **Measured/read 2026-10-09:** `SymbolsService.create()` writes `{ ticker, name, exchange, type, tags, aliases, active }` and **nothing else** — no `symbol_route`, no catalogue link; `symbol` holds 261 rows against `symbol_route` 12. ⚠️ **Not to be read as "a missing route blocks trading"** — routing is decided by what a broker CAN do (row 38), and that was NOT measured here. ⚠️ The consequence is made **visible** rather than silent: `RegisterSymbolOverlay` now states the registration is machine-local, and no longer claims a registered symbol "can be traded". | 🔴 **UNGUARDED.** No gate can see a missing seeder entry — the failure is the absence of a row in a file, which nothing asserts. |

| 77 | **atr-trailing-stop measures the stop distance in ATR UNITS (requirement \B\ on #1075): gaps = \(close − stop) / ATR\, graded at 2.0 ATR (strong, 75) / 0.5 ATR (moderate, 60) / below 0.5 ATR neutral (30). When BOTH stops are graded (range(20) < 6×ATR) the side with the LARGER gap wins; an exact tie resolves to the LONG side (stable sort, long pushed first).** | **The defect:** the previous thresholds graded a FRACTION of price (0.03 / 0.1 of \close\) against a distance bounded by the stop itself — for the long stop, \close − (hh20 − 3×ATR) ≤ 3×ATR\ while \close ≤ hh20\, so ANY positive grade required \ATR > 0.01×close\ (1% of price). Measured same day: real 5-minute ATR sits at 0.02–0.07% of price, so on a trending symbol the directive could only ever return neutral \Price near ATR trailing stop (up)\ — a direction computed and then discarded. The ATR-unit form is scale-free: a gap of 1.5×ATR means the same thing on XAUUSD and BTCUSD, and the guard rails are self-consistent (longStop = hh20 − 3×ATR and a 2×ATR strong band coincide exactly when close touches the trailing stop). MIN 25 bars; ATR ≤ 0 returns neutral with reason. **INSTEAD OF** option A — recalibrating the fraction thresholds (0.03→0.001 etc.), which keeps the price-relative denominator that cannot be compared across symbols. **REVERSE BY** restoring the fraction logic in #1075 (threshold constants + \/ lastClose\ divisor; the pre-change form is recorded in the issue). Canaries observed 2026-10-09: fraction-of-price divisor → **5 of 10** specs fail (fix-case, mirror, both overlap, plus the cross-suite mirror); ascending pick comparator → **exactly 1** (overlap-pick); both restored byte-for-byte (SHA256 match). | 2026-10-09 | 🟢 \tr-trailing-stop.directive.spec.ts\ + \stop-directives.spec.ts\ mirror (canaried both directions) |

| 84 | **META.Stack layer ownership (Requirement (2026-10-14)): a PROTOCOL finalises the score; a SIGNAL is a user tool for acting on it manually; a BOT automates opening/closing and applies RISK gates. A bot does not re-decide the opportunity, and no layer generates an additional score.** | **The matter this settles (owner's own words):** *"Bots are meant to accept that the opportunity has already been decided… It was never thought of as the bots having any bar they have to reach. It was framed as gates that need to be passed else a trade is blocked i.e. X percentage of the accounts funds are already in positions, and X amount of those positions are not locked into profit and X amount of loss potential exists."* And: *"never should it generate additional scores. Three defects in one expression, all served as numbers rather than errors: **(1)** the denominator was the peak *cumulative P&L* — £1,809.62, a fluke of one early winner — so a £161,068 fall was reported as **8,867.70%**, a figure that grows without bound as losses grow; **(2)** a `peak > 0` guard made a run that never goes positive report **exactly 0**, which is the "−0.00%" the requirement saw on screen (runs `e844b4cd` and `61ca9327` are exactly this shape — they now read **124.19%** and **127.14%** of capital); **(3)** trades were ordered by `entryDate` but stamped with `exitDate`, putting **117 of 1,027** points out of chronological order, so `peakEquity` and the peak/trough dates were computed on a zig-zag. The arithmetic moved to a pure function, `computeEquityPath()` in `nest/src/modules/backtest/equity-drawdown.ts`, because the three defects survived precisely because nothing could exercise them without a database. Same pass closed a related collision: `backtestrun.maxDrawdown` is WRITTEN from `min(returns10d)` — a ten-day forward return — and was being served under TWO labels on the same step ("Max Drawdown (10d)" and "Max Trade DD"), so run `7f00cfb8` showed −2.87 twice while its worst trade was **−$296.19**. The two tiles now read "Worst Return (10d)" and "Worst Trade", and those are three quantities with three names. | 🟢 **Enforced** for the arithmetic — `nest/src/modules/backtest/equity-drawdown.spec.ts`, 10 tests, canaried in **five** directions (peak>0 guard, summary denominator, point denominator, missing sort, zero capital) plus an unmodified control; every mutation was observed to fail before shipping. 🔴 **UNGUARDED** for the label split: nothing fails if a future reader maps `run.maxDrawdown` to a third name. |
| 103 | **A run comparison must name the settings that DIFFER, before it shows the results.** The diff is computed from stored columns, and `null` is never allowed to compare equal to a value. | Requirement 2026-10-31: *"this table also doesn't show any differences"* — and it could not. Four VIX 5m runs were listed at +141,219.98 / +134,925.50 / +129,840.82 / +76,617.26 with identical protocol, symbol, timeframe, trade count, win rate (68.64%) and profit factor (1.1722). Measured by diffing the rows (`logs/measure-run-config-diff.cjs`): of 18 stored settings only **`spread` and `slippage` differed**, and those were enough to swing the P&L by £64,600 on the *same trades*. `describeRunDifferences()` (`nest/src/modules/backtest/run-differences.ts`) now returns `changed[]` / `same[]` and `POST /api/backtest/runs/compare` carries it as `differences`; `RunSettingsDiff` renders it ABOVE the metrics table, because "what did I change" must be answered before the results are read or a difference gets credited to whatever the reader last remembers changing. ⚠️ `null` is `unrecorded` and is never equal to a value: a row that never stored a `spread` did not have zero spread, it has an unknown one, and marking it "same" would credit an unknown to both sides. | 🟢 **Enforced** — `run-differences.spec.ts`, 9 tests, canaried in BOTH directions (a dropped null so absences compare equal; an `if (false)` so nothing is ever "same") plus an unmodified control; every mutation was observed to fail. 🟡 **Partial** for the follow-on: the diff surfaces that `spread` differs, but not that the stored number meant a different thing before the 2026-10-29 cost-model fix (#1154) — that needs the row to carry what it CHARGED. |
| 104 | **A run declares WHICH SIDES it may open (`directionMode`: long / short / both), defaulting to `long` so no stored run changes meaning.** | Requirement 2026-10-31: *"I just want to be able to backtesting short strategies on the same symbol. It will be VIX for the purpose of development."* The engine could not do it at all: `backtest.service.ts` had `const isLong = true; // this loop only opens longs` beneath `if (signal === 'BUY' \|\| signal === 'STRONG_BUY')`, while every branch BELOW that line was already written for both sides — the capability existed everywhere except the one line that decides whether to enter. Measured evidence of the absence: `vix-spike-short-v1` scored 2 SELL signals over 23 bars and produced **0 trades**. Now the side comes from the signal class (`BUY`/`STRONG_BUY` long, `SELL`/`STRONG_SELL` short — enumerated, never `startsWith`, because `'STRONG_SELL'.startsWith('SELL') === false`), the bracket is mirrored (`stop` above and `target` below for a short), `applyExecutionCosts()` takes a `side` so a short is CHARGED rather than credited, `rawPnlPerShare` is signed by side, and `stopDistance` is side-aware so a short is not sized off the fallback. The mode is persisted (`backtestrun.directionMode`, added by ALTER TABLE, default `'long'`) and appears in `describeRunDifferences()` under `scope`, so a long-vs-short pair can never report "no setting differs". VERIFIED 2026-10-31: `vix-short-v1` in `short` mode produced 2 SHORT trades with entry 16.158 / stop 16.565 / target 15.915 — stop above, target below. | 🟢 **Enforced** for the cost sign — `execution-costs.spec.ts` asserts both sides charge the same round-trip percentage and that a losing short is charged, not credited, and the diff guard covers the long-vs-short pair. 🔴 **UNGUARDED** for the loop itself: the entry gate, the mirrored target test and the signed P&L have no unit test, because the whole loop sits inside `run()` behind Prisma. The canary that would catch a regression is the recorded QA entry (a short protocol must produce at least one `direction = 'SHORT'` trade), not an assertion.
| 105 | **Break-even win rate is MEASURED from the run's own trades (`p = avgLoss / (avgWin + avgLoss)`), never MODELLED from bracket levels plus a cost.** | The first version of the statistic solved `p = (stopPct + costPct) / (targetPct + stopPct)`, measuring stop/target distances from `entryPrice` — which is ALREADY `close × (1 + spread/2 + slippage)`. The cost was therefore inside both distances and then added again, overstating the requirement by **+13.1pp** on run `1bbf1860` (88.0% vs the true 74.9%) and **+8.9pp** on `568c5552` (57.7% vs 48.8%). It was found only by computing the same answer a second way and comparing; every test shipped with the first version passed, because they asserted the formula rather than the answer. `pnlPct` is net of spread, slippage AND commission, so the cost is already inside `avgWinPct`/`avgLossPct` and needs no input at all. ⚠️ The consequence for trust, not just for arithmetic: the page now prints avg win and avg loss, and the requirement is ONE DIVISION of the two — so a reader can check the tile by hand and no hidden constant can change it. A one-sided run (no wins, or no losses) returns `unavailable` rather than 0% or 100%, because "you cannot break even" is not provable from a sample with no wins. | 🟢 **Enforced** — `break-even.spec.ts`, 17 tests. The first case is a REGRESSION test pinning 74.9% against the old 88.0%, and another asserts the function takes NO cost parameter, so the model cannot creep back in. Canaried both directions (a null requirement returning 0; a zero gap counted as profitable) plus a control. |
| 106 | **Which side a signal opens is a PURE function (`decideEntry()`), enumerated over the four signal classes, and an unrecognised signal is refused rather than defaulted to long.** | Extracted 2026-10-31 from the trade loop so the newest money-path decision in the repo could be asserted at all — register row 104 recorded it as 🔴 UNGUARDED because it lived inline behind Prisma, a bar loader and a scoring call. The trap it pins is recorded in `meta-stack-contracts`: `'STRONG_SELL'.startsWith('SELL') === false`, so a prefix (or `includes`) test routes the STRONGEST sell signal down the LONG branch — a plausible backtest with the strongest signal inverted and no error. `side` (+1/−1) is derived in the same decision, never re-derived by a caller, because it multiplies BOTH the cost function and the P&L: a wrong sign books every short with a long's result. An unrecognised class (a typo, a lowercase `buy`, a renamed signal) returns `no-signal` rather than falling back to long, because "I do not recognise this" must never become "buy". | 🟢 **Enforced** — `entry-side.spec.ts`, 9 tests, canaried in THREE directions (prefix test instead of enumeration; unknown signal falling back to long; `directionMode` ignored) plus an unmodified control, every mutation observed to fail. |
| 107 | **Directives become PARAMETERISED per instance, and a protocol may hold more than one instance of the same directive — but only once instance identity and parameter storage exist.** Until then a second instance silently scores once. | Requirement 2026-11-01, after asking whether deeper configuration would raise scores: *"I can vision a more useful UI for configuring an indicator by simply having a dedicated chart per instance of indicator … we can also potentially run multiple configurations of one directive within a protocol."* MEASURED that day: **161** directive implementations across **112** files, **0** read any per-instance config (`DirectiveInput` has no such field), **110** named tuning constants plus **29** inline literal periods, **93** distinct score calibrations. (The first figure published here was **113**, which is the FILE count, and the constant count was **145** / then **1** — both measurement artefacts corrected on 2026-11-01; see #1166 for all three revisions of the extractor.) And the feature is structurally impossible today — `protocol-gating.service.ts:622-625` builds a `Set<string>` of directive ids so a duplicated id collapses to one execution, while `directives.service.ts:355` keys results by directive id so two instances could not be distinguished. `directive.config` (json, NOT NULL) looks like the parameter store and holds only `{description, directionBias, version}` on 109 of 109 rows; `protocol_directive_entry` has `maxScore` and `active` and no parameters. Order is fixed: **M1** research (#1166) → **M2** architecture (#1165) → **M4** UI (#1167), with **#605** (`rsi-divergence` parity) as the worked example. ⚠️ **CORRECTED AND EXTENDED 2026-11-01 (measured, not read):** the claim above that `protocol_directive_entry` has "no unique constraint" is **wrong** — `information_schema.STATISTICS` shows `protocol_directive_entry_groupId_directiveId_key` UNIQUE on (groupId, directiveId), enforced by the live database. Multi-instance is therefore blocked by the SCHEMA as well as by the scorer's `Set`, and unblocking it needs a deliberate drop of a live constraint plus the aggregation decision — not an id-keying change. **Landed in this row's name instead: per-ENTRY parameter storage.** `protocol_directive_entry.params json NULL` (`ALTER TABLE`, never `db push` — #769), read by `ProtocolDbService` into `DirectiveGroup.directiveParams`, passed by `ProtocolGatingService` into `DirectivesService.runAll(input, filter, paramsById)` and included in the tick-cache fingerprint, validated at the write boundary by `PATCH /api/scoring/protocols/:slug/directives/:directiveId` (unknown key or out-of-range value REFUSED, `paramsUsed` echoes the resolved values). `NULL` means "declared defaults", which is the pre-#1165 behaviour byte-for-byte. Verified on the running API 2026-11-01: `adx` in `eurusd-macro-v1` scored **80** (ADX 63.0) at defaults, **71** (ADX 42.8) at `adxPeriod: 7`, and **80** again after `params: null`; an unknown key and an out-of-range value were both refused with the known keys / allowed range named. | 🔴 **UNGUARDED for MULTI-INSTANCE, 🟢 ENFORCED for per-entry parameters.** The multi-instance guard does not exist and cannot until the aggregation rule is decided — a protocol holding one directive twice still scores it once, and the DB constraint prevents the second row anyway. Per-entry parameters are guarded by `nest/src/modules/directives/entry-params.spec.ts`, canaried three ways (removing the params hand-off fails HONOURED; removing the cache-key term fails CACHED CORRECTLY; restore verified by file hash) and by the write-boundary refusal, exercised live. |
| 108 | **A backtest result is only compared with another when the SETTINGS DIFF says they measure the same way — and a strategy more than ~15pp short of break-even is replaced, not tuned.** | Requirement 2026-11-01: *"We need to stop spending time testing the VIX protocol when we already know it loses so much money … If we can't instantly identify a clear fault then we should start over on a strategy."* MEASURED that day on the matched pair (same window, same 2%/4% bracket, nothing overridden): long `vix-spike-v1` 1,062 trades, win **24.3%**, needs **48.3%**; short `vix-short-v1` 1,425 trades, win **29.3%**, needs **50.1%**. Both ≈21–24pp short. The CLEAR FAULT was identifiable and is arithmetic: a nominal 4%:2% bracket needs 33.3% at zero cost and **49.5%** at the real 0.97% round trip, so **cost alone adds 16.2pp of required win rate** — on VIX the spread is ~24% of the target. At a 0.05% cost the same signals are ~4–5pp short. ⚠️ The comparison nearly went wrong: two VIX runs were briefly reported as a long-vs-short result while carrying **different brackets** (4% vs 1.5% target), and the short run's apparent 50.9% win rate was an artefact of the tighter target — caught by `describeRunDifferences()` and retracted (rows 103/105). | 🟡 **Partial** — `describeRunDifferences()` names differing settings before the metrics table and is canaried (#103), so the comparison trap is guarded. 🔴 **UNGUARDED** for the ≥15pp rule: it is a stated policy, not a gate. Nothing blocks a run being tuned rather than replaced. |
| 109 | **When directives gain per-instance configuration, THRESHOLDS are exposed before PERIODS.** A threshold fixes DORMANCY (a directive whose gate never trips contributes nothing to a composite, however well it scores); a period only changes WHICH opportunities are selected. Measured 2026-11-01 (`logs/measure-period-sensitivity.cjs`, #1166): on daily bars over 7-10 months MFI-oversold fired **0** events on both NVDA and SPY and RSI-oversold fired **0** on NVDA — gates calibrated for an instrument that oscillates around 50 do not trip on one that trends for seven months. Period value is uneven: recall across a 2x period change is **0.0%** for ADX (18 vs 40 events on VIX 1h, none coincident), **7.7%** for RSI-oversold, **45.8%** for CCI but **56.9%** for Stochastic and Williams %R — where the period is close to a tune and therefore should not gate the M2 architecture. | Requirement 2026-11-01 to work the directive area; measurement the same day. | 🔴 **UNGUARDED** — no parameter exists yet. The guard lands with #1165: an instance must be able to carry a threshold distinct from the source constant, asserted on a symbol/timeframe where the production gate produces zero events. |
| 110 | **A directive parameter is DECLARED in `params` and PROVEN by behaviour — a declaration alone is not evidence.** Every declared parameter must satisfy two machine-checked conditions: (1) BASELINE — supplying nothing reproduces the recorded production output for that directive, so parameterisation cannot change live scoring by accident; (2) LIVENESS — moving the parameter to its min or max actually MOVES the output on a committed fixture, so an inert control cannot reach a UI. The list of directives under test is DERIVED from `directives.module.ts`, never hand-written, because a hand-written list is itself a declaration that silently omits the next directive to gain a parameter. | Requirement 2026-11-01 to proceed on directives; the design is forced by **#761**, where a `directionBias` declaration was used to decide protocol capacity and was reverted after measurement showed declarations understate reality (`macd-crossover` declared neutral, emitted a direction 5,252 times). MEASURED the same day: capturing the contract as declaration-only was demonstrably insufficient — the NEUTRALITY comparison ("no params" vs "declared defaults") is near-tautological and passed while a default was deliberately moved 14 -> 21; only the recorded baseline caught it. First slice parameterised: `rsi-oversold`, `adx`, `mfi`, each verified byte-identical to `git show HEAD:` across five fixtures before its baseline was recorded. The write boundary is `POST /api/directives/:id/test`, which now accepts `params`, REFUSES an unknown key or an out-of-range value with the known keys named, and echoes the RESOLVED values as `paramsUsed` so a caller can see what actually ran rather than what it sent. A third guard, **SCORE BOUNDS**, sweeps every parameter at its min and max across every fixture and asserts the score stays in `0..maxScore` — added because parameterisation made a latent contract violation REACHABLE: `support-resistance-levels` returned **-14** on SPY 1h once `proximityPct` could be widened past the fixed 2% its unclamped expression had been safe against. Every declared parameter also carries a REQUIRED `chart` role (requirement 2026-11-01: charted indicators first; non-chartable knobs are marked `none` and surfaced as `needsDynamicVisual` by `GET /api/directives/:id/params`). Verified on the running API 2026-11-01: `rsi-oversold` on VIX 1h scored 11 at the default, then 7 / 10 / 15 at `oversoldLevel` 10 / 25 / 40, with `paramsUsed` reporting the resolved values each time. **EXTENDED 2026-11-01 (second landing, 11 directives):** `price-time-gap` (`gapLookback`, `minGapRatio`), `candle-pressure` (`lookback`, `deltaLevel`), `hammer-pattern` (`minWickRatio`, `maxUpperWickRatio`, `trendLookback`), `hurst-memory` (`trendLevel`, `reversionLevel`), `volume-confirmation` (`averagePeriod`, `surgeRatio`, `elevatedRatio`) — the FIVE rows that topped `audit-directive-depth` once registration was fixed (#1169), so the parameterisation programme stopped tuning code that could not run. ⚠️ **A THIRD CONDITION, learned here: a parameter must be PROVABLE BY A FIXTURE, so the fixture set is part of the contract.** Two whole classes were unprovable on the original five: `hammer-pattern` could never fire (every bar had identical geometry, so the upper wick was always 6× the body and the pattern was rejected before its thresholds were reached), and no series' last complete bar beat its own volume average, so every volume tier fell in the "normal" branch. FIXTURES gained `reversal-hammer` (3× lower wick, 0.1× upper wick, V-shaped run-up) and `mean-reverting` (R/S gives H = 0.338 against 0.99 for every directional series, plus varied candle geometry and one genuine 2.5× volume surge). Measured consequence: `trendLookback` first measured INERT because the V-shape crossed `priorTrend = 0` at exactly the 30-bar mark — a fixture has to clear a branch, not touch it. ⚠️ **A parameter that no fixture can distinguish is NOT declared**, even when it is the most natural knob on the surface: `candle-pressure`'s CLV band (0.6/0.4) gates the strong-pressure branches, which need 8+ bars of one-sided close location, and the best fixture gives 4 bull / 4 bear at 0.6 against 6 / 6 at 0.5 — both below the gate, so the score is identical either way. An inert control is worse than no control because a user tunes it and believes the result. **COMPLETE 2026-11-01 (third landing): 112 of 112 tuning constants across 144 directives now resolve from `params` — `node scripts/audit-directive-depth.cjs` reports 0 unturned.** Three shapes had to be decided rather than forced: (a) an ARRAY of periods is ONE knob, not several — `hurst-memory.RS_WINDOWS` exposes `longestWindow` (w/4, w/2, w) and `price-action.MA_PERIODS` exposes `longestMa` (w/10, w/4, w), because exposing three independent periods lets the set INVERT (fast slower than slow), which is not a tuning choice but a broken indicator; (b) a UNIT CONVERSION is a per-instrument knob and is exposed AS ITSELF rather than as a multiplier — `VIC_SCALE` (1e-8) became `vicScale` in both `price-time-gap` and `institutional-footprint`, because at 1e-8 the normalising sigmoid sits at ~0.12 and barely moves whatever the volume does, so the composite was inert in practice; (c) a knob whose data feed the harness never supplied is UNPROVABLE, and the fix is the harness, not the knob — `news-catalyst` abstained on every fixture until `input.news` was supplied (`newsFeed(asOf)`), and `social-buzz` threw inside its Prisma call until `socialMention.findFirst` was seeded, at which point `HIGH_BUZZ_THRESHOLD` (60 mentions against a 50 default) became provable. ⚠️ A THIRD harness defect found here, and it was silent: dependencies were supplied BY POSITION (`TEST_SAFE_SERVICES[i]`), so `social-buzz` received a `MarketSessionsService` where it expected a `PrismaService`; the loader now matches the CONSTRUCTOR PARAMETER'S DECLARED TYPE read from source, because `Cls.length` tells you how many dependencies there are and never what they are. | 🟢 **ENFORCED** — `nest/src/modules/directives/directive-parameters.spec.ts` runs inside `prebuild` via `npx jest`, and was canaried three ways: a moved default is caught by BASELINE, and both an ignored level read and an ignored period read are caught by LIVENESS; restore verified byte-for-byte. Re-canaried for the second landing: moving `hammer-pattern.minWickRatio` 2.0 → 2.5 fails BASELINE (1 failed / 81 passed) and making the directive ignore `trendLookback` fails LIVENESS (1 failed / 81 passed), with 82/82 green after restore. The five original fixture fingerprints were diffed across the six pre-existing directives after re-recording: **0 changed**, so the widened fixture set added evidence without moving production behaviour. |
| 111 | **A directive class in the library is either REGISTERED or DELETED — there is no third state.** Every `export class … implements IDirective` in `implementations/` must have a `register(this.x)` call in `directives.module.ts`, and `nest/scripts/validate-directive-registration.cjs` fails `prebuild` otherwise. Registration means the `register()` ACT, resolved through the constructor parameter to the DECLARED class name (so an aliased import resolves); it is NOT "the class name appears in the module", which matches the import line. A class that duplicates a live `id` is deleted, never registered — two instances of one signal inflate a composite denominator. Where two implementations of one indicator existed, the survivor is the one carrying a MEASURED `maxScore` ceiling (#1131) or a seed reference; the other file is removed. | Requirement 2026-11-01: *"Own the directive area only"*, with #1169 as the first slice. MEASURED the same day: **50 of 161 classes were reachable by nothing** — they compiled, passed conformance, and could never score. The cost was not dead code: `audit-directive-depth` ranked `price-time-gap` and `candle-pressure` TOP TWO by tunable headroom and neither could run, and `williams-r` had already been parameterised, given a BASELINE and named by `directive-groups.ts` while unreachable — so its parameters tuned nothing and a protocol silently scored one directive fewer than it declared. ⚠️ Two guard defects were found and fixed in the same pass, both the "reports on an artefact it is not testing" shape: `validate-directive-id-references.cjs` tested class-name presence (137 "registered" ids vs the runtime's 111, printing `✅ OK` on a broken tree), and every audit missed `MondayEffectDirective as MondayEffectRealDirective` — an ALIAS — reporting a live registered directive as unreachable, one step from a delete decision on working code. The rule now exists once, in `scripts/lib/directive-registry.cjs`, for all four consumers. Out of scope and still open: per-instance storage and multi-instance aggregation (#1165). | 🟢 **ENFORCED** — `nest/scripts/validate-directive-registration.cjs` runs in `prebuild`, and its `--self-test` canaries both directions plus the alias case (7/7). Canaried on the live tree 2026-11-01: removing one `register()` call made it exit **1** naming the class; restore verified byte-identical by file hash, exit **0**. |
| 112 | **A directive that cannot answer for a HISTORICAL bar must ABSTAIN (`unmeasured`), never score a current snapshot.** Lookahead has an exact definition — the data was observed AFTER the moment being evaluated — and both affected sources carry the timestamp needed to test it (`crypto-fear-greed` keeps `fetchedAt`; `companyMetric` has a `fetchedAt` column). The only tolerance is **one bar duration**, because a live bar is stamped at its OPEN and data legitimately arrives during it; a fixed tolerance would either flag every live bar or admit three-day-old data. Missing either timestamp is `unknown`, which ABSTAINS — "cannot prove it was available" must never be recorded as "it was available". | #1139 item 2, measured 2026-11-01. `crypto-fear-greed` fetched the index live with a 1h cache and never read `asOf`; `dilution-impact` read one current `companyMetric` row with no date predicate — so **every historical bar was scored against today's value** in both cases, plausibly and with no error. A protocol using either could not be judged by its backtest at all. | 🟢 **ENFORCED** — `lookahead.spec.ts` (13 tests) and `lookahead-directives.spec.ts` (7 tests) assert BOTH directions per directive: the historical bar is `unmeasured`, and LIVE still produces a real reading. Canaried by disabling each guard (`if (fidelity !== 'ok')` → `if (false)`), which failed the suite for both directives; restore verified byte-for-byte. |
| 113 | **Long and short are TWO ACTIVE STRATEGIES. One bot never trades both ways, and one protocol is never scored both ways.** The direction a protocol expresses is part of its identity, so a side-flip is a different strategy with its own protocol, its own bot and its own record — never a mode of one. Any surface offering "both", "either side" or "hedged" inside a single run is invalid by construction, whichever layer offers it. | Requirement, restated 2026-11-01 after finding a `directionMode: "both"` option in the Backtest Lab: *"this should not be possible by the rules of the apps architecture and design… The decision to not try to score both ways within a single protocol or trade both ways within a single bot was made ages ago: there needs to be two active strategies."* ⚠️ **The rule had never been written down** — a search of `docs/SETTLED-DECISIONS.md`, `docs/`, the roadmap data and the open issue tracker on 2026-11-01 found `trade both ways` / `both ways` / `long and short` / `hedged mode` mentioned **nowhere**, so an agent re-derived the forbidden shape from scratch and shipped it as a UI option. An unrecorded rule is indistinguishable from no rule. | 🟢 **ENFORCED** — `refuseUnrunnableDirection()` in `entry-side.ts` throws for `both` and `backtest.service.ts` calls it on every run, so the API answers 400 with the remedy; the Backtest Lab renders a DISABLED legacy option only when a stored run already used it. `entry-side.spec.ts` asserts both directions (refuses `both`, allows `long`/`short`, and classifies every `DirectionMode` so a future mode cannot slip in), canaried by removing the guard and by making it refuse everything. |
| 114 | **A backtest position must be AFFORDABLE, and an unaffordable one is REFUSED — never inflated to one unit.** Committed notional is capped at `accountSize x maxLeverage`, with `maxLeverage` defaulting to **1** so a run that says nothing cannot borrow. Every trade records `notional` and `notionalPct`, and skipped trades are COUNTED (`tradesRefusedUnaffordable`) with the arithmetic in the reason. | #1142, measured 2026-10-26 and confirmed 2026-11-01. `fixedPercent` sizes notional as `accountSize x riskPct / slPct`, so `Risk %` is a risk BUDGET and never a cap — at the defaults every trade commits 2x the account. A `if (positionSize < 1) positionSize = 1` floor then GUARANTEED one whole unit, so stored run `a6fcd0ee` (BTC 15m, 610 trades, accountSize $10,000) reported **+$138,058.50, a +1,380% return**, from an implied $77,600 notional per trade. That number was arithmetic from a floor, not an edge. | 🟢 **ENFORCED** — `applyAffordability()` (`affordable-size.ts`) is called on every sized trade in `backtest.service.ts`; 12 tests, canaried three ways (refusal disabled, cap removed, default leverage raised to 10 — each failed the suite, restore byte-for-byte). Verified on the running API: BTC 15m now gives 7 signals -> **7 refusals -> 0 trades, $0**, reason naming `one unit would be 80172.06 against an account of 10000.00 x 1`; VIX 1h gives 12 trades, 0 refusals, `avgNotionalFractionOfAccount 0.999` (capped) where it previously committed ~2x. |
| 115 | **A backtest window is chosen by a NAMED mechanism and the run reports which one, and `days` is a DATE RANGE ending at the newest bar — never a row limit.** Three sources, always reported as `windowSource`: `period:<name>` (a stored frozen window, authoritative), `days-range` (an explicit count measured back from the newest bar), or the **frozen development window by default** when neither is supplied. A run that measures an unstated window is not comparable with anything. | #1132, and a defect found while wiring it (2026-11-01). The day path ran `orderBy asc` + `take: days + 120`, which returns the **OLDEST** N bars: `days: 20` on VIX 1h measured **2026-08-14 to 08-24** - four days in August - while the last 20 days was 2026-10-22 to 10-30. Every day-count run had been measuring the beginning of the available history, silently, and its span depended on the timeframe. The default was then `days = 365`, so a caller who supplied neither got an invisible window too. | 🟢 **ENFORCED** — `daysRange.spec.ts` pins that the start is measured from the NEWEST BAR (not the clock) and spans exactly `days`; canaried by anchoring on `Date.now()`, which failed the suite, restore byte-for-byte. Verified live: default run reports `period:dev-window-v1` 2026-09-14 to 10-20 (155 trades); `days: 20` reports `days-range` 2026-10-16 to 10-30 (53 trades) where it previously reported four days in August. |

| 118 | **A position must be SIZED IN THE BROKER'S OWN UNIT, and "unknown granularity" is never a step of 1.** The smallest tradeable increment is `volumeStep × contractSize` from `broker_symbol`; every size is quantised DOWN to it, and the run reports the step it used (`unitStep`, `unitGranularity: 'broker' | 'assumed-whole'`). Whole units are a SHARE, not an instrument. | #1170, 2026-11-01. US30 at ≈$45k, 2% risk on a 1% stop, $10,000 account: the sizing model asked for **0.44 units**, `Math.floor()` upstream made it 0, and `applyAffordability()` refused it — measured **301 signals, 301 refusals, 0 trades**. After the change the same request produced **301 trades at 0.1 lots**, `avgNotionalFractionOfAccount` 0.514, and the bridge's own spec was the source (`broker 3 US30`: `volumeStep 0.1`, `contractSize 1`). The upstream `Math.floor` had to go with it: quantising twice is how the request became zero before anything knew what a broker accepts. ⚠️ **BTC is NOT fixed by this** — its route is `provider: binance`, so there is no MT5 spec to read and it still refuses; that is #1177 (its stored bars are a ~$34 Nasdaq ETF, a different defect). ⚠️ Every run stored before 2026-11-01 sized in whole units, so its money is not comparable with a run after it — `unitStep: NULL` is the marker. | 🟢 **Enforced for the rule** — `affordable-size.spec.ts` (19 cases, 7 new) pins step rounding DOWN, the minimum-lot refusal, the FP-dust boundary (0.1 × 3 must equal 0.3), the assumed-whole fallback and the unchanged whole-share case; `broker-symbol-discovery.spec.ts` fails if the sync stops writing `volumeStep`/`contractSize`; `run-differences-coverage.spec.ts` fails, naming `unitStep`, if the column is not diffed. 🟡 **Partial for the surface** — the Result step's "sized in 0.1-unit steps" sentence is served on both read paths and present in the bundle, but was not observed rendering because Lab step 2 is locked by an active probe; nothing fails if that sentence is later removed. |

The refusal is a `throw` before any bar is read, so a run cannot exist with an invented size. ⚠️ The SURFACE is verified by loading the Lab: the Sizing Model offers **Bot size (units)** and selecting it renders the size field whose placeholder reads "the bot's own size". |
| 120 | **A SEEDED PROTOCOL MUST BE SUPPORTED BY INSTALLED DIRECTIVE DATA — on BOTH install paths — and a tuned parameter is not real until the SEED can declare it.** (1) The install path (`POST /api/scoring/protocols`) REFUSES a `directiveIds` entry that no registered class answers to, naming every offending id; the seed path had been guarded since #1169 by `validate-directive-id-references.cjs`, but the API path could create exactly the entry that validator exists to prevent. | 122 | **Instances of one directive are AVERAGED into ONE directive slot — never summed, never keyed separately.** A protocol may hold several `protocol_directive_entry` rows for one `directiveId`, distinguished by `instanceLabel` (`''` = the primary, so the old `UNIQUE(groupId, directiveId)` survives as the special case `UNIQUE(groupId, directiveId, '')`). `DirectivesService.runAll` runs every instance and collapses them into ONE result keyed by DIRECTIVE ID: score = mean, confidence = mean, and `signalClass` only when every instance agrees — otherwise `neutral`, because a mixed reading is not a weaker direction and averaging a categorical has no meaning. `DirectiveGroup.directiveIds` stays de-duplicated so `capacityMax += maxScore` cannot run twice, which is what keeps `INV-STABLE-DENOMINATOR` true from the config side. An instance configured identically to the primary is REFUSED, because averaging makes a duplicate a silent halving of that directive's weight rather than a no-op. | Requirement 2026-11-01: *"Do 1165"* — run several configurations of one directive in one protocol and compare them on the same bars. MEASURED the same day: the issue's premise was wrong in one direction and right in another — `protocol_directive_entry` DID carry `UNIQUE(groupId, directiveId)` (information_schema, corrected in an issue comment), so a second row could not exist at all; and the scorer collapsed ids in a `Set`, so a repeated id would have been read twice and resolved to ONE result — scoring once and looking like it worked. Both were fixed. LIVE PROOF on `btc-speed-v1` / BTC: adding a `wide` instance (`bbPeriod` 60) to `bollinger-band-squeeze` moved the reading from **40** to **47** with reason `2 instances averaged: default=40, wide=53 → 47/90`, while `rawMax` stayed **1295** with one instance and **1295** with two — adding a comparison changed the view and not the protocol's configured capacity, which is the whole reason for averaging. Removing the named instance deleted only that row (the primary survived, score back to 40). Refusals verified live: empty label, unknown directive, directive not in the protocol, and an identical instance each return `success:false` with the reason. | 🟢 **ENFORCED** — `nest/src/modules/directives/directive-instances.spec.ts` (9 tests) runs in `prebuild` via `npx jest`. The schema half is canaried in SQL, not in a spec: two rows with distinct labels insert, a third with a duplicate label is refused `ERROR 1062` on the new unique key, and the table returned to 435 rows after cleanup. Seed declarations of instances are covered by `protocols-seed-integrity.spec.ts`, canaried with a bogus `directiveId` and an empty label. |

MEASURED and CONFIRMED that assumption, rather than assuming it: after the split, `protocol` = **50 rows / 15 active**, `trading_strategy` = **40 rows**, `protocol_directive_group` = 140, `protocol_directive_entry` = 435 — all unchanged; and `POST /api/trading/strategies/seed?dryRun=true` returned `{created:0, updated:0, unchanged:3, changes:[]}`, so the seeder touches only what it ships and proposes no deletion. ⚠️ Also measured: the split broke three guards that had encoded the old size — a rotted slug allowance, and two count-based floors (`> 10 keys`, `> 30 bots`) that turned a deliberate content change into a failed build. Both floors were replaced with a NAMED check, because a number cannot distinguish "deliberately small" from "the parse returned nothing". | 🟡 **PARTIAL** — `scripts/split-seed-packaging.cjs` (`--self-test` 9/9) enforces the two invariants that make a two-file split safe: it **refuses an empty keep-set**, and it **refuses a shipped bot whose protocol is not shipped**. `validate-strategy-library-links.cjs` now asserts the named `PACKAGED_SLUGS` present, and `validate-protocol-slugs.cjs` self-tests the baseline mechanism with a synthetic entry because the real allowance is empty. ⚠️ NOT guarded: a future agent adding a protocol back to the seed without adding its bot (or vice versa) is caught only by the second invariant at split time — nothing fails if the seed is edited by hand afterwards. Stated rather than implied. |

| 130 | **`priority` alone does not ORDER — a resolver that sorts on a non-unique column has no defined winner.** `symbol_protocol_assignment` is unique on `(symbol, protocolId)`, NOT on `(symbol, priority)`, so two protocols can claim one symbol at the same priority. Measured 2026-11-02: **EURUSD** (`probe-1dir-v1` + `forex-session-v1`) and **XAGUSD** (`probe-1dir-rsi` + `xau-speed-v1`, the latter INACTIVE), both tied at priority 0. `resolveProtocolForSymbol()` now orders by `[{ priority: 'asc' }, { protocolId: 'asc' }]`, so the pick is deterministic by construction. | 131 | **A fault found once must be swept across every protocol, not fixed where it was noticed — and the sweep is a tool, not a memory.** `npm --prefix nest run audit:protocol-faults` checks seven classes against every protocol at once: STALE-SCORE (active protocol whose assigned symbol stopped producing snapshots), NO-SNAPSHOTS, NO-DIRECTION-24H (20+ snapshots in 24h, not one directional), QUIET (writing score rows but no snapshot — the healthy-but-silent state, named so it is not mistaken for broken), DIRECTION-STARVED (config only: direction-capable capacity below the entry threshold, so it cannot signal in ANY market), AMBIGUOUS-PRIORITY, INACTIVE-WINNER, DEAD-FEEDER (a table an active protocol reads holds 0 rows), THIN-PROTOCOL. Every class cost real time to find once. It is **on demand**, not `prebuild`: all seven need live data, and a build must not fail because MySQL is down — the structural half of the same classes is already in `prebuild`. A changed protocol gets a **grace window** (`--grace-minutes`, default 30) so a just-activated protocol is not reported broken; `protocol.updatedAt` is a PROXY for "recently activated" and can only ever *suppress* a finding for that window, never invent one. First run found 9 findings, then **3 of them turned out to be the tool's own bug**: it counted `trading_signal IN ('BUY','SELL')` and so missed `STRONG_BUY`/`STRONG_SELL` entirely, reporting `xau-speed-m15` as "0 directional in 24h" when it had emitted **24 STRONG_BUY**. The STRONG_ labels are separate values, not prefixes — the same class the pipeline once shipped. `DIRECTIONAL_SIGNALS` is now one named constant used by every query and asserted by the self-test. The same lesson killed the original window-based NO-DIRECTION class: "never directional in this window" is not "cannot be directional". | 🟡 partial — `--self-test` (18 checks) proves every rule fires AND stays silent, and it caught a real field-name bug on first run (a healthy protocol was reported NO-DIRECTION); nothing runs it automatically, so a fault appears only when someone looks. | 2026-11-02 · #1181 |
| 132 | **A per-bar read of a table that cannot change between bars is a defect, not a cost — and the guard belongs on the RUN, not on the reader.** `computeRarity()` re-read the protocol's whole 365-day `score_snapshot` distribution on EVERY `scoreWithGating()` call, which is once per bar in a backtest. MEASURED 2026-11-02, `forex-session-v1`: 118,360 rows / **601 ms per read**, and a 548-bar USD1H run spent ~165 s of its 192 s inside 548 identical reads — 214 s total, **426 ms/bar**, against 46 ms/bar for a protocol running MORE directives. Fixed by memoising the history read per protocol for 60 s (`RARITY_HISTORY_TTL_MS`); result **7.5 s / 13.7 ms/bar** with every scored row, trade and stat field **byte-identical**. ⚠️ WHY THE RULE IS STATED THIS WAY: the maths was never wrong, so no test of the maths could catch it, and every unit test stubs the database — the defect WAS the database. Only counting queries (`SHOW GLOBAL STATUS` deltas) and sampling `information_schema.processlist` found it; `performance_schema` is denied to this MySQL user. ⚠️ Guarded by `INV-BACKTEST-BUDGET` on WALL TIME, deliberately a SYMPTOM guard: three plausible causes were investigated and REFUTED first (`Intl.DateTimeFormat` in `session-open-effect` = **0.4%** of run cost; all nine directives together = **143 ms per 648 bars**; `resolveSymbolId`/`loadTfBundle` were already cached), so a cause guard would have inherited a wrong theory. Residual tracked in **#1183** — the remaining 5m cost is per-bar WRITES to live tables. | 🟢 `INV-BACKTEST-BUDGET` (live) + `protocol-gating.rarity-cache.spec.ts` (canaried both directions) |
| 135 | **Protocol-symbol suitability is decided by four EXISTING gates in a fixed order, and the one that matters (separability) must be measured through the BACKTEST, not the live outcome tracker — because the tracker only holds days, not the six weeks a verdict needs.** The four gates are already built: (1) LEGAL — `GET /api/suitability/directive-instrument-audit` reports directive-vs-asset-class fit for 98/98 declared directives (95 of 238 placements currently fail it; `enforced: false` by design, because wiring it changes what a symbol scores). (2) SEPARABLE — `EdgeStatisticsService` (`GET /api/scoring/edge/report`) gives Wilson intervals + a binomial test, split long/short. (3) PROFITABLE — `GET /api/scoring/edge/pnl` plus a backtest A/B. (4) BOT DESIGN — backtest A/B. ⚠️ WHY GATE 2 CANNOT USE THE TRACKER: MEASURED 2026-11-02, `directiveoutcome` spans **2026-10-29 → 2026-11-02** — BTC 5 days, SPY/USDJPY/ETH/SOL 2, QQQ/NVDA/WTI 1. A direction split on 1-5 days cannot separate a directive from a lucky regime, and the endpoint says so in its own caveat. The usable source is `dev-window-v1` (42 days) through the backtest harness. ⚠️ The practical replacement for Gate 2 is the CEILING statistic: the maximum `signalPct` a protocol can even reach on a symbol, taken over the last 2 days (current config) rather than all-time (stale config — the all-time max for `spy-day-v1` was 92.9 recorded 2026-09-09, while the last 2 days read 28.7; comparing a fresh backtest against an all-time live max is NOT a comparison). That ceiling is what surfaced **#1184** — 1 directive averages 72 and reaches 100, 14 directives cannot exceed 14.9 — and it is why a `minComposite` of 10 is 36% of achievable for a 9-directive protocol, not 10% of 100. ⚠️ ALSO MEASURED AND FIXED in the same pass: the backtest called `scoreWithGating()` with FIVE arguments while live `speed-scan` always passed SIX, so every historical bar was scored with `input.timeframe === undefined`; verified behaviour-neutral on the USDJPY baseline (548 scored / 200 entrySignals / 316 trades / tradePF 3.47 / +9,728.72, all identical). | 🟡 partial — the `timeframe` argument is now aligned and `scoreSeries` can be requested in full (`fullScoreSeries`, guarded by `score-series-cap.spec.ts`, canaried both ways), but nothing yet forces Gate 1-4 to be run in order and the ceiling statistic has no tool |

| 136 | **A paired bot's SHORT leg must be ASSIGNED to the bot's symbol; the bot's `symbol` column does NOT imply it, because scoring and routing read different tables.** A bot carries the instrument and subscribes to two protocols (`strategy_protocol`, with `role`), and that drives ROUTING. | 137 | **A backtest is a SIMULATION and must write nothing to the live tables; the guard is on the COUNTERS, not the code path.** `scoreWithGating()` persists at the end of every evaluation, and a backtest calls it once per bar, so one 548-bar run added **+320 to `score_snapshot`, +320 to `Score` and +320 to `protocol.adaptiveTargetSamples`** (measured 2026-11-02). Refused by an `opts.simulation` flag gated at the top of `storeSnapshot()` — the SINGLE write point — which is the same argument `active = 0` uses for the #803 kill switch: gate the class at one place rather than each caller remembering. Nothing is lost by refusing: the run's own `scoreSeries` carries every bar it scored, because the live tables are a HISTORY of what the market did and a simulated bar is not that. ⚠️ TWO CORRECTNESS COSTS, which is why this is not tidiness: `computeRarity()` percentiles a live composite against the protocol's last 365 days of `score_snapshot`, so a run pollutes the distribution the next run is measured against; and `adaptiveTargetMax` is MONOTONIC, so a historical bar above the stored peak permanently raises the LIVE protocol's target. ⚠️ MEASURED AND REFUTED (2026-11-03): the issue also predicted this was "the binding cost of a 5m run". It is not — a 5m XAGUSD run measures **106–112 s warm either way**, because `storeSnapshot()` is fire-and-forget and the writes were never on the scoring loop's critical path. Removing them removes DB churn and a correctness defect, NOT wall time. | 2026-11-03 | 🟢 enforced — `INV-BACKTEST-NO-WRITES` reads the four counters either side of the run `INV-BACKTEST-BUDGET` already times (no extra backtest), and `protocol-gating.simulation-no-write.spec.ts` canaries the gate (removing it fails 2 of 5 tests) plus the opposite direction (a live write must still happen). |

Fixed three ways: (1) `resolveConst()` evaluates sums/differences and nested references; (2) resolution is CLASS-SCOPED first — `mt5-indicators.directives.ts` declares **17 separate const MIN**, one per class, so a file-wide first-match gives every class the wrong number; (3) an unresolvable threshold is REPORTED as NOT judged, and an unjudgeable id is excluded from the shrink-the-baseline list so the ratchet cannot shrink on a class nobody looked at. `minBars` now derives from `MIN_BARS` so a literal can never disagree again. | 🟢 enforced — 9-case `--self-test` covers the SUM case, the difference case, class-scoping and unresolvable recording; live ratchet is 40 baselined / 9 reported unjudgeable | 2026-11-03 · #1181 |

| 139 | **A protocol's `focusRank` is PRESENTATION-ONLY, is seeded, and a LONG leg and its SHORT leg share one rank.** The requirement directive, 2026-11-03: Please add a value to our chosen focus protocols to indicate some form of priority status … I want our focus ones to be more prominent in much of our other work and when I do manual testing. `protocol.focusRank INT NULL` — `1` is the first thing to look at, `NULL` means "not a protocol under active work". ⚠️ **Nothing in scoring, gating, sizing or execution reads it, and nothing should**: a rank that changed what a protocol SCORES would make the number depend on the operator's attention. It is converged in BOTH the upsert's `create` and `update` clauses, so a re-seed can repair it. ⚠️ A tie is fine HERE — the derived SHORT leg inherits its parent's rank so a pair never straddles two list positions — because the consumer is a sorted LIST with a `slug` tiebreak, unlike `symbol_protocol_assignment.priority`, where a tie meant an arbitrary winner (#1181). Ranks are assigned by MEASURED evidence, not enthusiasm: `forex-session-v1` 1 (the only arm with a positive baseline — 316 trades, 72%, trade PF 3.47, +9,728.72), `xau-speed-v1` 2 (carries the live XAGUSD position that moved into profit), `momentum-stock-v1` 3 (NVDA baseline −2,878). | 2026-11-03 | 🟢 enforced — `protocols-focus-rank.spec.ts` (5 tests in the `prebuild` suite): ranks unique among longs, contiguous from 1, positive integers, and every short leg carries its parent's rank. | 140 | **The trend READING is built as an OBSERVATION and must never feed a score — and that isolation is enforced by a build gate, not a comment.** Requirement (2026-11-03): explain to me how other bots and AI trading agents determine trend, just like a trader might draw a predicted trend on a chart … is this app doing that and is it even possible? then *"yes use the research you just explained to me and do this as an experimental feature."* Built at `/development/trend-reading` + `GET /api/trend/read`, computing (1) TREND STATE from two INDEPENDENT families — R² of a least-squares fit ("how line-like") and ADX ("how much directional movement") — reported separately with a `familiesAgree` flag rather than blended, because they regularly disagree (measured 2026-11-03: SPY r² 0.31/ADX 27.7 and TSLA r² 0.49/ADX 15.7 both disagreed); (2) A TREND LINE as the fit plus a ±2σ residual channel; (3) NON-REPAINTING confirmed pivots; (4) an INVALIDATION level. ⚠️ **It does NOT forecast.** A trendline's predictive content is small and its invalidation level is the half that protects a position, so `projection` is labelled an extension of the fit, drawn dashed, and gated on R². ⚠️ **The isolation is the load-bearing part, not a style preference**: a reading that moved a score would be a maths change and would need a copied configuration plus a monitored A/B, so `nest/src/modules/trend/**` is imported by nothing in scoring/gating/sizing/execution, and `trend-read-observation-only.spec.ts` fails the build if that changes. ⚠️ That guard's FIRST VERSION PASSED WHILE SCANNING ZERO FILES (it joined `__dirname` with `src/modules/...` from inside `src/modules/trend`, resolving to `src/src/modules/...`); canarying it exposed this, and it now asserts a minimum file count so **a scan that inspects nothing FAILS**. ⚠️ The reading is `status: testing`, not `supported` — the 12-symbol distribution (ranging 5 · trending-down 3 · trending-up 2 · indeterminate 2) shows the classifier does not label everything a trend, which is NOT the same as showing a human agrees. Only the requirement can settle that. | 2026-11-03 | 🟢 enforced — `trend-read.math.spec.ts` (23 tests: direction both ways, flat/noise NOT called a trend, `null` below the bar floor, invalidation on the correct side, determinism) + `trend-read-observation-only.spec.ts` (4 tests, canaried: inserting the import into `protocol-gating.service.ts` fails it and names the file) |

| 141 | **A ONE-SIDED DIRECTIVE HAS TWO DISTINCT FAILURES, and only one is "dead capacity".** Measured 2026-11-03 while building the `mfi` mirror. | 143 | **The sim-TSL and exhaustion OBSERVERS are ARMED on the three focus bots while those bots remain INACTIVE — arming is configuration, activation stays the project's.** Measured 2026-11-03 before this: `simTslEnabled = 0` on **all 41 bots** and `sim_tsl_event` had **0 rows ever**, so the decision moment (a position runs into profit, then gives it back) was never recorded anywhere — while the 30-day exit arithmetic says it is the whole problem: avg win **+4.50** against avg loss **−15.27** needs a **77.2%** win rate to break even, and the realised rate is **29.5%** with **21** stop-outs against **1** take-profit. ⚠️ **Arming an observer is NOT a trailing stop.** `recordSimTslEvent()` writes `sim_tsl_event` with `action: 'recorded'` hardcoded and calls a single `prisma.create`; it places nothing and moves nothing, verified by reading the recorder. Acting on the moment remains a MATHS change needing a copied configuration and an A/B, so it is deliberately not done. ⚠️ WHY ARM BEFORE ACTIVATION: the measurement has to exist before the exit rule can be judged, and arming changes no behaviour while `active = 0` — so the project's activation decision is not pre-empted. ⚠️ THE THRESHOLDS ARE DERIVED, NOT GUESSED: `activationPct` ≈ ⅓ of the bot's own `tpPct` (the trade has made a third of its target) and `distancePct` ≈ ⅓ of its `slPct` (give back a third of the stop), giving XAGUSD 0.5/0.25, USDJPY 1.0/0.5, NVDA 2.0/1.0. ⚠️ The values are in `nest/src/modules/trading/strategy-seed-data.ts` and converge in BOTH the `create` and `update` paths — the first attempt put them in `create` only, and the round-trip test caught it (`unchanged: 3` with the DB still at 0, i.e. a seeder that can insert but never repair). | 2026-11-03 | 🟢 enforced — `strategy-seed-convergence.spec.ts` (17 tests) covers converge-on-drift and leave-alone-when-absent; canaried: removing the convergence block fails the "can never repair" test. The observer's record-only property is a code fact, not guarded — stated rather than implied. |
| 144 | **"Contrast" is the term for backtest comparison; "Live A/B" stays reserved for the forward, parallel-account method. One declared difference in both, but they run on different clocks.** Requirement (2026-11-03): can we create a term/name for that form of testing. We have the longterm A/B testing approach that requires live market data but the comparison approach as part of backtesting is different and I want to avoid confusion when discussing it. The terms: **Backtest** = one configuration over one historical window; **Contrast** = two backtests over the SAME bars differing in EXACTLY ONE declared input, yielding a verdict (sides are **baseline** / **variant**); **Live A/B** = two accounts trading forward in parallel, one declared difference (sides are **arm A** / **arm B**). ⚠️ **The rule is what makes the word worth having, and it is symmetric**: 0 differing inputs is not a contrast (nothing to learn — it is a reproducibility check), 1 is a contrast (the outcome difference is attributable), and 2+ is **confounded** (neither input can be credited) — the same one-declared-difference rule as the live A/B pair LOCK (#963), copied deliberately so the two methods cannot drift apart. ⚠️ **"Arm" now MEANS live** — one word that tells the reader which clock is running. ⚠️ Rejected: "Backtest A/B" (the exact confusion being removed), "Comparison" (kept as the plain-English word inside the definition, not as the term), "Twin run" (implies identical; a contrast is deliberately not), "Delta run" (collides with P&L delta), "Shadow run" (collides with parallel-live), and "Counterfactual" (already means the narrower thing, correctly, on the backtest UI). ⚠️ A contrast is NOT evidence about the future: it cannot see a regime absent from its window, which is the gap the Live A/B exists to close — a contrast narrows the search, live trading confirms it. | 2026-11-03 | 🔴 UNGUARDED — the vocabulary is declared in `docs/GLOSSARY.md` and the one-difference rule is stated, but nothing yet FAILS when two runs differing in three inputs are described as a contrast. `POST /api/backtest/runs/compare` reports outcome metrics and comparability warnings, not the count of differing INPUTS, so the rule cannot be enforced until the runs persist enough of their request to diff. Stated rather than implied. |
| 145 | **A DRY RUN THAT MUTATES IS WORSE THAN NO DRY RUN: it reports the change and causes it.** Measured 2026-11-03 by triggering it — `POST /api/trading/strategies/seed?dryRun=true` returned `created: 3` AND inserted them, because every write on the update branch of `seedDefaults()` was guarded by `dryRun` while the whole create branch was not. The trigger is ordinary and not exotic: the seeder matches bots by NAME, so RENAMING a seeded bot makes it look absent, and a dry run then reports a create and performs it. Result: 58 bots where 55 were expected, the three originals alive alongside their renames, and — the part that matters — the duplicates were created with `active: true` from the seed, i.e. three extra ACTIVE bots that could place duplicate orders on a shared netting account. ⚠️ NOT re-opening row 23: that is one A/B pair's two arms netting against each other, a limit of the measurement; this is two separate BOTS holding the same symbol, a duplication defect. Different cause, different fix. The guard is at the branch head because the scoring-strategy create and the subscription create are on the same path. ⚠️ Name-keyed seeding remains a hazard: `trading_strategy` has no `slug` column, so a rename is indistinguishable from a delete unless the seed is renamed in the SAME change (it was, in all three sources: `strategy-seed-data.ts` plus the two scripts that look bots up by name). | 🟢 enforced — `strategy-seed-convergence.spec.ts` asserts a dry run creates NO bot and NO subscription while still reporting `created: 1`, AND that the same input without `dryRun` does create. Canaried: deleting the guard fails the dry-run test (1 failed / 18 passed), restoring it passes — verified byte-identical restore by file hash. | 2026-11-03 |
| 146 | **A COMPLETE STRATEGY IS A THEORY AND A TRACK RECORD — and `theory` is seeded CONDITIONALLY, unlike `focusRank`.** The requirement is for a word for *"factual behaviour and evidence output by strategy"* and approved **Track record** plus four theory kinds (`asymmetry` / `participation` / `regime` / `control`, where `control` is a first-class kind meaning "no thesis"). Schema: `strategy_track_record` (one row per observation: one sentence capped at 300 chars, `numbers` JSON, `source` named) plus `trading_strategy.theory` + `.theoryKind`, rendered together on the bot page because neither half survives alone. ⚠️ **Two failures learned the hard way the same day.** (1) `protocol.theory` was **DB-only** — the seeder READ it (protocol-db.service.ts lines 474/584/831) and never WROTE it, so 9 protocols' prose had no path through a clean install; seeded now. (2) The first version wrote `theory: config.theory ?? null` in the upsert `update` clause, and **one re-seed nulled `forex-session-v1` (735 chars) and `xau-speed-v1` (773 chars)** — `?? null` coerces "not declared" into "write NULL", and those two carry their theory in the database with no seed entry. Nothing errored; two live Theories panels simply went blank. Text recovered from `scripts/db/protocol-theory-seed.sql`, which held the original. The rule: `?? null` is the #833 convergence rule for a column the seed OWNS; `theory` is not owned here, so DECLARED converges and OMITTED is left alone (Prisma `undefined`). | 2026-11-03 | 🟢 | `scripts/canary-689-theory-seed.cjs` — canaried BOTH directions: nulls the six seed-owned theories and asserts each returns from a re-seed (RESTORE), then writes a sentinel into `gold-macro-v1` (a protocol the seed does not own), re-seeds, and asserts it is untouched (PRESERVE), then restores byte-for-byte. The PRESERVE direction is the one that was broken. |
| 147 | **A BOUND ON RETRIES MUST NOT BE A LATCH: after the bound the watchdog must still RETRY, just slowly.** The MT5 bridge watchdog allowed 5 reconnect attempts while `terminal-down`, then `continue`d forever. That could never clear itself, because the counter is reset ONLY by observing a healthy poll and `terminal-down` clears ONLY via a reconnect — which the latch forbade. **Measured 2026-11-03:** both bridges hit 5/5 at 12:28 with BOTH TERMINALS OPEN and algo trading enabled, backed off to 1800s, and stayed disconnected for ~11 hours (`connectionErrors` 7,490 and 32,251, `connectionStateDetail: terminal_info() is None`). `POST /reconnect` restored each on the FIRST call (accounts 10011309743 £660.65 and 62149650 £945.83). The 2026-09-27 decision that introduced the bound was right that a stale IPC link is recoverable; it was wrong that a bound is self-clearing. Past the bound the attempt cadence is now governed by `next_attempt_at` (already capped at `MT5_WATCHDOG_MAX_BACKOFF_SECONDS` = 30 min), so escalation still stops while recovery stays possible. ⚠️ The bound remains a real anti-thrash guard against a genuinely-closed terminal — this removes the deadlock, not the limit. | 🟢 enforced — `test_connection_watchdog.py` (19 tests). Canaried both ways: reintroducing `if attempts >= MAX: continue` fails `test_terminal_down_keeps_retrying_past_the_bound`, and restoring passes with the file verified byte-identical by hash. `test_terminal_down_never_escalates_past_the_bound` pins that the counter still saturates, so "stop trying" cannot come back disguised as "stop escalating". | 2026-11-03 |

| 151 | **A FAILED FEED MUST NOT RENDER AS AN EMPTY RESULT — INV-NO-EMPTY-SWALLOW.** A `queryFn` ending `.catch(() => ({ x: [] }))` returns the exact shape of a successful empty answer, so three things follow silently: the page states a fact it never read ("No open positions", "0 entries", "nothing at risk", "No active alerts"), react-query records a SUCCESS so `isError`/retry never engage, and anything derived from the value is destroyed (in #782 an empty list PRUNED the user's stored protocol selection on one transient 502). ⚠️ `.catch(() => null)` is ALLOWED — null is the absence of an answer and every caller can tell it from an empty array. ⚠️ `isError` alone is also not enough: measured 2026-11-06 against a URL returning 404, react-query reported `status: pending · isError: false · failureCount: 1 · isFetching: false` — the RETRY WAIT — with `data` undefined, so an `isError`-only check leaves the false zero on screen for seconds. `feedState()` therefore separates loading / ready / error / unknown. 'sell' : 'buy'`). `validatePreTrade()` **passed** — position exists, quantity ≤ held — and the request then went out positive, i.e. a **BUY** of the stock the operator asked to sell: worst case they end up holding more, having paid for it. `accountType` was destructured and dropped, so an ISA sale had no route at all. **Fixed and OBSERVED:** selling 0.1 `AIRp_EQ` on the practice account returned `quantity: -0.1`, `"side":"SELL"` and moved the position 0.5972579 → 0.4972579. | 173 | **A BACKUP NOBODY RESTORED IS A FILE, NOT A BACKUP — and every archive this project had was UNRESTORABLE.** Requirement (2026-11-13): we do not want to give my bots away, there should be an automatic backup of the database happening to reduce the impact of hard drive failure. Mitigated without admin: `tcs-launch.ps1` now prints a warning when the newest archive is >36 h old (canaried in all three states: fresh / 96 h stale / empty). 🔴 Drill runtime **~8.5 min** (516 s import) — too slow for nightly, right for weekly. |

Editing one constant therefore changed the score of every protocol carrying that directive, wrote no audit row and changed no version anybody reads — measured the same day, adding two parameters to `ema-structure` changed the scoring input of **11 protocols** (one resolving 79 symbols) and nothing failed. `docs/directive-versions.json` now records `id -> {version, sourceHash}`, and `prebuild` FAILS when a source hash moves without a version bump. **Comments are stripped before hashing and string literals are NOT**, because a docblock has never affected a score (and `ema-structure` gained a multi-paragraph one in the very change that motivated this) while a `reason` string IS returned through the API. Whitespace is collapsed. A version bump with no source change is allowed: the version is a claim the author makes, and it is required only when behaviour actually moved. ⚠️ This pins the RELATIONSHIP between a directive and its version — it does NOT pin a protocol's behaviour, which is sections 2-4. | 2026-11-14, #1274 | 🟢 **ENFORCED for the announcement** — `scripts/validate-directive-versions.cjs` runs in `prebuild` (before `rimraf dist`); 13 self-test cases; canaried four ways on the real `ema-structure.directive.ts` (code change without a bump -> exit 1 naming the directive; with a bump -> not silent; comment-only -> exit 0; restore verified byte-identical); `directive-version-guard-wiring.spec.ts` fails if the guard is unwired (2 failures when removed from `prebuild`). 🔴 **UNGUARDED for protocol behaviour** — a developer can still bump 2.1.0 -> 2.1.1 and change scoring for the whole fleet in one commit; it is now announced, not prevented (#1274 §2-4). |
| 180 | **A PROTOCOL'S "NOTHING IS HAPPENING" STATE CARRIES A NON-ZERO SCORE — so an entry bar at or below it trades noise on every bar, and the only guard that existed checked the CEILING.** Measured 2026-11-14 (#1275): `forex-session-v1-long` produced **11,945** readings in 7 days with **16 distinct values**, of which **84.6% were the identical 8.391608%** — that is `raw 60 of 715 capacity`, not zero. The floor is protocol-specific, so no single bar can be right for all: `xau-speed-v1-long` 0.00% (68.6% of 8,459 bars), `momentum-stock-v1-long` 0.00% (30.4%), `forex-session-v1-long` 8.40% (47.1% of 548, only 23 distinct values). **Six of 64 active bots sit at or below their own protocol's floor** — Probe RSI Oversold Bot bar **0** vs floor 57.14, Gold Short M15 Bot 10 vs 25.05, EURUSD MTF Weighted Bot 9 vs 23.53, Earnings Momentum NVDA 10 vs 12.20, US SPY Speed 9 vs 11.51, Gold Position 4H 8 vs 9.89 — and **29 more could not be judged** (no readings in the window, which is not a pass). This is the mechanism behind the "cliff" seen the same day: on forex/USDJPY 1h an entry bar of **8.0** gave 803 trades and **−£1,002** while **8.4** gave 159 and **+£66** — 8.4 IS the floor value. ⚠️ **THE ROOT CAUSE IS AN ASYMMETRIC GUARD:** `assertCeilingAllowsActivation()` (`trading-strategy.service.ts:1864`) refuses a bar ABOVE the measured ceiling, and **nothing** checks a bar BELOW the floor — so a bot can be comfortably under the ceiling and still be trading the no-signal state. Worst exposure is the only profitable arm: forex is the 1-of-3 that profits (tradePF 3.47, net +9,728.72) and **12 of its 13 session bots run a bar of 9% against a 8.40% floor — a 0.60pp margin**. **Built:** `baseline-three-protocols.cjs` now prints the floor, its share, the distinct-value count and a per-bot verdict from the same run (no extra backtest). **REVERSE BY** removing the `noiseFloor()` read — no scoring behaviour changed. | Primary, 2026-11-14 (#1275) | 🟡 the measurement is repeatable (`node scripts/baseline-three-protocols.cjs`) but nothing FAILS yet — whether a below-floor bar should refuse activation is a trading decision and is left open on the issue |
| 181 | **A GUARD AT ONE END OF A RANGE IS NOT A RANGE CHECK — the entry bar was validated against the ceiling and nothing checked the floor, so 21 of 64 active bots traded the protocol's no-signal state.** Measured 2026-11-14 (#1275): `assertCeilingAllowsActivation()` (`trading-strategy.service.ts:1864`) refuses a bar ABOVE the protocol's measured ceiling, added after a bot that could never fire (#1051/#1048).
