| 183 | **A MINIFIER THAT SAYS THE CSS IS INVALID IS RIGHT — so `cssMinify` is `lightningcss`, and a failure there means the CSS is broken, not that the tool is too strict.** | `vite.config.js` carried `cssMinify: false, // lightningcss is too strict`, which silenced the only thing in the build that was noticing. Measured 2026-11-15: **20 stylesheets in `src/` were genuinely invalid**, and a browser silently DROPS an invalid rule and renders the rest with no error — so invalid CSS is indistinguishable from styling nobody wanted. Cause traced to commit `ac63fa7f` (2026-10-15), which replaced whole rule blocks with `}undefined { … }` across **53 CSS files**; `.cm-table`, `.tp-comparison-table`, `.sig-loop-table`, `.uir-table`, `.rll-table`, `.pc-table` and `.tp-config-table` were still rendered by their pages while their rules no longer existed, so tables silently lost `min-width`, `border-collapse`, sticky headers and cell padding for a month. ⚠️ The primary corruption is **syntactically VALID CSS** (`undefined` is a legal type selector), so a parse gate alone cannot see it — hence two checks. Rules recovered from git (`ac63fa7f^`), not guessed. Side effect: `metastack-cockpit.css` prose containing `src/**/*.jsx` had been terminating its CSS comment early, which also made `validate-css-literals` count issue reference `#862` as a hardcoded colour (129 → 128 keys). Build output went 1,386 KB → 858 KB (-38%). | 2026-11-15 | 🟢 enforced — `scripts/validate-css-parses.cjs` runs in `prebuild`; C1 parses every stylesheet with the production minifier, C2 strips comments then rejects `undefined` as a selector or property; 11-case self-test covering both checks in both directions, including that prose mentioning "undefined" must PASS. `npm run test:css-parses`. |
| 184 | **A GUARD THAT CANNOT PASS IS NOT A GUARD, BUT A NUMBER INVENTED TO MAKE IT PASS IS NOT A BUDGET — so bundle size is one ABSOLUTE check plus a RATCHET.** | `check:bundle` compared the entry chunk against hardcoded `MAX_JS_BYTES = 280000` / `MAX_CSS_BYTES = 12000` and failed on **every** run since 2026-09-23 (measured then: JS 3,697,654 B, CSS 1,255,365 B — 13x and 105x over; the entry chunk is 4,879,567 B today, 17x). A light that is always red is indistinguishable from no light, and the next real regression hides in the noise. Raising the constant to today's size would have made the number meaningless, so the budgets were replaced by two different questions. **C1 PWA cliff (absolute, a FACT):** `workbox.maximumFileSizeToCacheInBytes` is read from `vite.config.js` at run time and never duplicated — exceeding it makes `vite-plugin-pwa` FAIL the build. Measured 2026-11-15: largest asset 4,879,567 B against a 5,242,880 B cap = **354.8 KB (7.4%) headroom**, and the config already notes the cap was raised from 4 MB because that line "was repeatedly exceeded". Warns at 90%. **C2 ratchet:** entry JS, entry CSS, total `dist/` and PWA precache against `scripts/bundle-baseline.json` — size may go DOWN freely, never UP without a deliberate, reviewable re-freeze. ⚠️ No honest absolute target exists while `view-routes.jsx` statically imports **153** pages with zero `React.lazy` (#1280) — the ratchet stops growth being an accident until that lands. Three exit codes, because "cannot judge" is not "failed": 0 pass, 1 violation, **2 no `dist/` or unreadable cap**. Now runs as `postbuild`, so it is no longer opt-in. | 2026-11-15 | 🟢 enforced — `scripts/check-bundle-budget.mjs`, 14-case `--self-test` (`npm run test:bundle`), canaried all three ways: ratchet growth → 1, 6 MB asset over the cap → 1, `dist/` absent → **2**. |
| 185 | **A PAGE THAT IS IMPOSSIBLE TO LOSE IS A PAGE ON THE CRITICAL PATH — so every route is `React.lazy`.** | `src/core/view-routes.jsx` statically imported **153** page components, and `App.jsx` renders `VIEW_COMPONENTS[secondaryView]`, so **every page in the app sat in the entry chunk**: 4.65 MB of JS a user must download before first paint, including ~150 pages they may never open. Measured 2026-11-15, that chunk was **354.8 KB (7.4%)** from `workbox.maximumFileSizeToCacheInBytes`, i.e. a `npm run build` FAILURE, so the size was not merely slow — it was a latent breaker. Converting the 153 imports to `lazy(() => import(...))` plus one `<Suspense>` boundary at the single render site cut **entry JS 4,765 KB → 692 KB (−85.5%)** and **entry CSS 858 KB → 158 KB (−81.6%)**, moving the largest asset from 93.1% to **15.9%** of the PWA cap (headroom 355 KB → 4,306 KB). Total `dist/` GREW 1.0% (113 → 452 files) — that is the honest cost of splitting, and the C2 ratchet reports it rather than hiding it. ⚠️ The binding risk is NOT the JS split but the **CSS** split: page stylesheets are global (per-page prefixes, not `*.module.css`), so a class defined in page A but used by page B would render unstyled until A loads. Verified in a browser on 4 routes (capability-map, backtest-strategies, positions-monitor, ui-review) that each page's own deferred CSS applies — including the restored `.cm-table`, `.pm-pos-ticket` and the `@media (max-width: 900px)` `.uir-table` rule. | 2026-11-15 | 🟡 partial — the *outcome* is guarded (`check-bundle-budget.mjs` C2 fails the build when entry JS grows) and 3,204 tests pass, but nothing fails if a NEW page is added with a plain `import` instead of `lazy`; it would only surface as a ratchet breach once a build grew the entry chunk. |
| 186 | **"NOT SCORED" AND "NOT MEASURED" ARE OPPOSITE FINDINGS — so a mover's treatment is six states and never collapses to two.** Requirement (2026-11-15) (#1281): *"I would like to see a list of all the winners and losers and how TCS is treating them in terms of scoring, trading, monitoring and decisions made."* Every part of that answer already existed in a different place — `/api/market-data/top-movers` had the moves, `/lab/hot-stock-picks` the ranked scores, `/development/blocked-signals` the refusals — and nobody could see them for ONE symbol. `GET /api/scoring/movers-treatment` now answers it in four queries regardless of universe size (the existing `getTopMovers()` runs one query per active symbol — 214 — which is fine for a widget and not for a page). ⚠️ **The states are ordered, and the order is the decision:** `traded` → `queued` → `refused` → `scored` → `unscored` → `no-data`. A recorded refusal OUTRANKS a score, because reporting only the score hides the refusal behind a number; a pending queue row outranks a refusal because it is newer work. `no-data` is **cannot judge**, not a 0% move — a symbol with no bars has not "stayed flat". `unscored` (bars but no score) is a **coverage gap**, not a decision. Live measurement 2026-11-15: 47 of 47 symbols comparable on 5m, verdicts 5 traded · 11 scored-with-no-subscriber · 4 not-scored — i.e. the majority of measured movers were scored and had **nothing subscribed to act**, which is information no single existing page carried. **Built:** `movers-treatment.service.ts` (pure `classifyTreatment` / `hotCandidates` / `verdictSentence`), `movers-treatment.controller.ts`, `MoversTreatmentPage.jsx` at `/development/movers-treatment`, and the FIRST real reader of `hotStockEnabled` + `hotStockDetectionMode`. **REVERSE BY** removing the route — nothing that scores or trades reads this. | Primary, 2026-11-15 (#1281) | 🟡 partial — `movers-treatment.spec.ts` 14/14 pins the ORDER of the six states and the detection modes (canaried: deleting the refusal branch fails exactly 1 test, restore verified byte-identical), but nothing fails if a future reader collapses two states into one colour. The `hotStock*` sizing/hold columns remain unread and `bot-rules.spec.ts` still refuses to let them say otherwise. |
| 187 | **A DISTRIBUTION CANNOT TELL YOU WHETHER ITS OWN INPUTS ARE REAL — so the momentum-ladder thesis is UNTESTED, not refuted, and no ladder is built until the bars are.** Ran the Phase 0 measurement the plan required: of our symbols hitting +25% intraday, how many reach +50% / +100%? On 5m over 100 sessions × 50 symbols = **2,735 symbol-days** (≥20 bars): run-up ≥+10% **42**, ≥+25% **8**, ≥+50% **0**, ≥+100% **0**; run-down ≥-10% **52**, ≤-25% **7**, ≤-50% **0**. Extremes **+45.92%** / **−43.81%**. The arithmetic is correct and the conclusion it invites — "the upper rungs do not occur" — would have been a claim about the MARKET drawn from a claim about the DATA. ⚠️ **Every extreme case inspected bar-by-bar turned out to be a corrupt bar:** METC +45.92% = session opens 10.41 then freezes at **10.08 V=1**, while its `1d` bar reads O 14.13 H 14.26; QNTM +42.27% = frozen at **2.6850 V=1** all session vs `1d` O 3.66; VIX +43.49% = 5m at **17.36** vs `1d` O 24.47; SOUN −43.81% = stale prefix at **11.1050 V=1** then real trading near 6.25. Two distinct defects behind it: **(a)** `WTI` carries **two providers on one `symbol` row** ~25× apart (`mt5` 2.82–4.28 on every timeframe, `yahoo` 88.28–104.12 on 4h/1d); **(b)** **stale-fill bars** — 5m has **54,012 of 476,662 frozen O=H=L=C (11.3%)** across **1,294 of 2,735 symbol-days (47%)**, and on NVDA `frozen` ≈ `volume ≤ 1` in every weekday bucket with **Saturday 115/115 and Sunday 275/275**. Net: **455 of 2,229 symbol-days (20.4%)** have a 5m first-open disagreeing with that day's `1d` open by >2%. ⇒ **Decision: do not build the ladder yet, and do not report "the strategy doesn't work".** The step that caught the error was opening ONE day bar-by-bar; a distribution cannot audit itself. **REVERSE BY** re-running the Phase 0 measurement once #1282 lands — if the upper rungs are still empty on CLEAN bars, that IS a refutation and the strategy should be dropped. | Primary, 2026-11-15 (#1282) | 🔴 UNGUARDED — nothing fails if an agent builds the ladder or reports the thesis as refuted tomorrow. #1282 is the work that would let it be re-measured; there is no test that can distinguish a real +45% day from a stale-fill one today, which is precisely the defect. |

| 190 | **A READY-MADE MOVERS FEED IS END-OF-DAY, NOT REAL-TIME — so it builds an OVERNIGHT WATCHLIST and cannot be the intraday discovery source.** Requirement (2026-11-16): we would ideally use a ready made source of top movers… It doesn't make sense to only process our registered symbols. | 191 | **A GUARD THAT READS ONE DIRECTIVE PER FILE MISSES A THIRD OF THE LIBRARY — and an audit that reads only UPPER_SNAKE module constants misses every inline literal.** Two false-cleans, measured 2026-11-16 (#1284) while parameterising the two directives a momentum strategy needs. **(1)** `validate-directive-versions.cjs` — the guard behind `INV-DIRECTIVE-VERSION-ANNOUNCED` — recorded **93 of 144** directives and printed `✅ OK`. **51 (35%) were unguarded**, all in five files: `momentum-and-macro.directives.ts` **30**, `mt5-indicators.directives.ts` **17**, `surge.directive.ts` 2, `mtf-confluence.directive.ts` 1, `vwap-confluence.directive.ts` 1. Cause was two bugs: the glob was `*.directive.ts` so **`*.directives.ts` (plural) was never opened** (47 directives invisible in their entirety), and `fingerprint()` read only the **FIRST** `readonly id` per file. **Fixed** by `directivesInFile()` — segmenting per `export class … implements IDirective` and hashing each segment, scanning both suffixes, with the segment starting at the PREVIOUS class's closing brace so a module-level `const X_PARAMS` default is inside the hash. `fingerprint()` deleted rather than left as a second implementation. Now `✅ OK — 144 directive(s)`; canary changes `MOMENTUM_ROC_PARAMS.strongPct.default` 5→6 with no bump and fails naming `momentum-roc` (`1.0.0/… -> 1.0.0/…`), restore byte-identical. **(2)** `audit-directive-depth.cjs` prints `113 knobs, 113 exposed, 0 unturned`, which is TRUE and NARROW — it counts module-level UPPER_SNAKE names matching `/(PERIOD|LOOKBACK|THRESHOLD|…)/`, so a lowercase function-scoped `const period = 20` (`donchian-breakout`), an inline argument `roc(closes, 12)` and an inline literal `current > 5` (`momentum-roc`) all reported `knobs=0, unexposed=0` **and reported the same before and after parameterisation** — it cannot tell the two states apart. ⇒ **`0 unturned` must never be read as "parameterisation is complete"**, and the work must not be driven from that number alone. Both directives are now parameterised (`donchian-breakout` period 3-120 default 20 → v1.2.0; `momentum-roc` period 2-60 default 12 + `strongPct` 0.5-50 default 5, symmetric so the strong-negative branch uses `-strongPct` → v1.1.0), with `MIN` rebased to `period+5` / `period+3` which equals the old hardcoded 25/15 at default params so **no protocol changes behaviour**. ⚠️ The first `--write` baselined all 144 including the 51 previously invisible — whether any of those changed without a bump historically is **unverifiable and recorded as such**, not assumed clean. **REVERSE BY** restoring `fingerprint()` and the single suffix — which would re-open the 51. | Primary, 2026-11-16 (#1284) | 🟢 the version gate now covers 144/144 and its canary fires on a parameter default; `directive-parameters.spec.ts` **463/463** proves baseline + liveness for **68** directives (was 66). 🔴 **The audit-width gap remains open** — no test fails if a new directive ships with an inline literal, because nothing can see one |
| 192 | **A PROTOCOL CREATED THROUGH THE API IS PERFECTLY HEALTHY AND SCORES NOTHING — because scoring resolves symbols from a 54-row table that NO API WRITES.** Measured 2026-11-16 (#1285). Built the momentum-candidate protocol (`POST /api/scoring/protocols` → 9 directives across 3 groups, `maxPossibleScore` **845**) and its bot (`POST /api/trading/strategies` → magic 234195), then found the run wrote **0** snapshots for it while writing **56 for XAUUSD in the same pass**. Cause: `getSymbolsForProtocol()` reads `symbol_protocol_assignment`, the single writer of which is **`nest/prisma/seed-symbol-onboarding.ts`** — there is **no controller** that touches the table — so an API-created protocol is `active=1`, appears correct in every UI, and can never score. This is the same defect the paired-legs block in that seeder already records (*"an active protocol resolving to no symbols is indistinguishable in the UI from a quiet market"*), and what `INV-NO-SYMBOL-GUESSING` reports as "WIRED TO NOTHING". **Fixed** by declaring `{ symbol: 'MSTR', protocolSlug: 'momentum-candidate-v1-long' }` in `ASSIGNMENTS`, and the **round-trip was proved** rather than assumed: delete the row → `0` → re-seed → restored with the same UUID `2767cc7b-…` and priority 0. ⚠️ **Two further facts, both load-bearing.** **(a)** `trading_strategy.symbol` is `varchar(20) NOT NULL` and `signal-queue.service.ts` filters `if (sub.strategySymbol && sub.strategySymbol !== rec.symbol) continue;` — so an **empty** symbol would act as a WILDCARD over every symbol its protocol scores. That is the only existing route to market-wide discovery, **0 of 75 bots use it**, and it is an accident of `&&` rather than a designed feature, so it was NOT used: relying on it would be an unvalidated behaviour on a money path. **(b)** For the strategy that means **one bot = one symbol**; discovery needs either that wildcard decision or a code change. ⚠️ **And the bar had to be measured, not chosen:** a 20-day 5m backtest of MSTR gave **839 scored bars, floor 16.9%** (modal no-signal value, 10% of bars) and **ceiling 51.7%**, so the legal band is `16.9 < bar <= 51.7`. The first placeholder of **50 passed 1 bar of 839 (0.1%)** — a bot that can never fire — and **30 passes 21%**, so 30 is what the bot carries. **REVERSE BY** deleting the assignment row and the two API objects; nothing in scoring changed. | Primary, 2026-11-16 (#1285) | 🟡 partial — the backtest proves the protocol computes (`opportunityMax` 845 matches the group sums, 1,085 rows) but **nothing fails when a protocol has no assignment**, which is the gap that let this happen. Live scoring is additionally blocked by stale stock bars (4,125 min) |
| 193 | **A BOT KNOB SET ON 42 OF 64 BOTS AND READ BY NOTHING IS NOT A SETTING, IT IS A SILENT DIFFERENCE IN WHAT WAS MEASURED - `maxHoldHours` is now honoured.** | `trading_strategy.maxHoldHours` was set on **42 of 64 active bots** and referenced **zero** times by `backtest.service.ts`, so a run held every trade until its stop, its target or the end of the data while the live path closed it at `entry + limit` (`position-time-stop.service.ts`, `SYSTEM_TIME_STOP`). Measured on the existing population: MSTR 5m, 560 trades, **longest hold 4,045 min (67 h)** on a 5m chart. Now honoured via `max-hold.ts` + `mergeBotConfig`, with a new exit reason `TIME` (deliberately not `EOD`: end-of-data means the sample ran out, a time stop means the BOT decided). ⚠️ The ORDER is the decision: the time stop is tested AFTER the stop and target, and closes at the bar close. A stop is a level the bar genuinely traded through; `TIME` is a decision to leave at the close, so testing `TIME` first would invent an exit at the close while ignoring a level the market traded through — worst across a market gap, where the resting stop is what would really have filled on reopen. A/B on identical parameters, `maxHoldHours` the only difference: **3 TIME exits (two at exactly 240 min, one at 1,060 min across a weekend gap) vs 0** with the knob off. NOTE: `cooldownMinutes` (set on 76 of 76 bots) is deliberately NOT implemented — each simulated trade is independent, so a per-trade limit is unambiguous, but "no new entry within N minutes of the last close" across OVERLAPPING hypothetical trades has more than one defensible reading and needs a modelling decision. | 2026-11-16 | 🟢 enforced in direction, pinned in the other - `nest/src/modules/backtest/max-hold.spec.ts` (9 cases: the inclusive boundary, zero/negative/NaN/null all meaning NO limit, and a reversed series) plus `settings-inventory.spec.ts`, which now pins `maxHoldHours` in the honoured set so removing the wire fails the build. Canaried: `>=` to `>` fails 3 cases, dropping the `limit <= 0` guard fails the no-limit case, both restored byte-identical. |
| 194 | **MOVING THE RULE CORPUS OUT OF A PUBLIC REPO IS CORRECT — AND EVERY CONSUMER MUST RESOLVE IT FROM EITHER LOCATION, OR THE BUILD DIES ON THE MOVE RATHER THAN ON THE CODE.** Commit `bdfde054` (Requirement (2026-11-16)) moved all **19** `*.instructions.md` files out of `.github/instructions/` into the docs store at `agent-ops/instructions`, replacing them with a published `.github/copilot-instructions.md`. **The reasoning was right and measured**: `.github/` is an ordinary tracked folder, git ignores nothing by default, so 3,776 lines of internal technique — thresholds, fleet reasoning, strategy hints — had been public since they were added. ⚠️ **But the commit updated 2 of the 3 consumers.** `validate-agent-docs.cjs` and `validate-output-style-sync.cjs` gained a resolver; `nest/scripts/validate-pipeline-contracts.cjs` did not, and it is the one that OWNS `meta-stack-contracts.instructions.md`. It kept reading the old repo path, so the **entire backend build went red** with `no invariants found in meta-stack-contracts.instructions.md` — a message that names a missing document and therefore points at the wrong thing, since the document was not missing, it had moved. **Fixed** by adopting the same resolver: `[repo path, docs store].find(exists)`, `CONTRACTS_DOC = null` when neither holds it, and **wiring checks SKIPPED LOUDLY** (exit 0, and the skip says *"This is NOT a pass: invariant <-> guard <-> QA wiring was NOT checked"*). ⚠️ **The skip is deliberate, not laziness**: `G:` is a Google Drive mount that is regularly unmounted on this machine, and a gate that goes red because a network drive is offline teaches everyone to ignore it — worse than not having it. That reasoning is the project's own, from the same commit. Canaried in both directions: breaking the docs-store path prints the skip and exits **0**; restoring prints `✅ OK — 36 invariants` and the file restores byte-identical. **REVERSE BY** restoring `.github/instructions/` — which would re-publish the corpus, so it is not a reversal anyone should take. | Primary, 2026-11-16 (`bdfde054`) | 🟡 partial — the three consumers that were KNOWN are now aligned (`validate-agent-docs` exit 0, `validate-pipeline-contracts` exit 0), but `validate-output-style-sync` still exits **2 (UNVERIFIED)** for an unrelated reason — its SOURCE file is a VS Code prompt path that no longer exists, tracked as REVIEW-QUEUE **R56**. Nothing enumerates "who reads the corpus", so a fourth consumer would be found the same way this one was: by a red build |
| 195 | **TWO INDEXES ON THE SAME COLUMNS IS NOT REDUNDANCY, IT IS WRITE COST ON EVERY INSERT - the duplicate `marketbar` index is dropped (203 MB).** | `marketbar` carried TWO indexes on the identical column list - `MarketBar_symbolId_timeframe_timestamp_idx` (non-unique) and `..._key` (UNIQUE) - which is how Prisma created them: both an `@@index` and an `@@unique` were declared on the same three columns. An index on (a,b,c) answers `(a)`, `(a,b)` and `(a,b,c)`, and the UNIQUE copy also enforces the constraint, so the non-unique copy could never be chosen: measured `EXPLAIN` on the live table (1,790,688 rows) picked `..._key` for the hot bar read and listed the other only as an unused `possible_key`. It was pure write amplification on the hottest ingestion path. REMOVED from `schema.prisma`, regenerated into `schema-init.sql` (one line), and dropped live with `ALTER TABLE marketbar DROP INDEX`. Measured after `ANALYZE TABLE` (the data-dictionary estimate does NOT update on a drop, which is worth knowing): **index 881.8 MB -> 678.9 MB, a 203 MB reduction**, and that is DESPITE the table growing from 1,790,688 to 1,922,346 rows during the test. NOTE: this is a bug-shaped artefact, not a tuning choice. | 2026-11-17 | 🟢 enforced - `gen-schema-init.cjs --check` (in `prebuild`) fails if the regenerated DDL and `schema.prisma` diverge, and the `@@index` line is gone from the source with a comment saying why re-adding it is wrong. Behaviour verified after the drop: `EXPLAIN` still uses the UNIQUE index for all three prefix shapes (symbolId+timeframe+timestamp range, symbolId alone, symbolId+timeframe), 619 bars were written in the following 10 minutes, and `/api/market-data/freshness` (214 ms) and `/api/market-data/candles` (253 ms, real bars) both returned 200. |
| 196 | **THE HOLE THAT SWALLOWS A BRACKET IS USUALLY THE ONE RIGHT AFTER ENTRY - and `spansGap` was skipping exactly that pair, so the trades most in need of the flag reported clean.** `spansGap` marks a trade whose stop/target was never actually monitored because the bar series had a hole (the machine sleeps). Its own comment claimed "a hole > 1h", but the test was `stepMin > barMinutes * 12 && stepMin > 60`, and it sat behind `if (future > day + 1)` — which is FALSE on the trade loop's first iteration, where `future === day + 1`. So the pair `(bars[day], bars[day + 1])`, ENTRY BAR -> FIRST FORWARD BAR, was never measured. That is the worst omission available: an entry placed on the last bar before the machine sleeps is unmonitored for the entire hole, and it was reported `spansGap = 0`. Fix is to DELETE the guard, not widen it — on the first iteration `bars[future - 1]` IS the entry bar, which is what the flag means. The threshold moved to `bar-gap.ts` so it has ONE definition, and its doc now states the rule accurately: both `barMinutes * 12` AND `> 60` min, which coincide only on a 5m chart (on 1h bars the floor is 12 HOURS, not one hour as the old comment implied). A/B on an identical window, the guard the only difference: 571 trades both sides, `totalPnl` **-23334.98 both sides** (so this is classification, not P&L), flagged **94 -> 99**, exactly **5 flipped clean -> flagged, 0 regressions**. The 5 are all the same shape — entry **21:00**, exit **14:30** next session, `barsHeld = 1` — the overnight hold where the exit reason is a guess. Why it matters beyond tidiness: gap-crossing trades average **154.41%** MFE against **1.05%** for clean ones, so every misclassified trade pollutes the population the excursion analysis averages over. | 2026-11-17 (#1286) | 🟢 enforced in both directions - `bar-gap.spec.ts` (16 cases: the exclusive boundary at exactly 60 min and exactly 12 bars, the two rules being an AND rather than an OR, and non-finite input meaning NOT a hole) plus `isBarHole` wired into the loop. Four canaries observed failing then restored byte-identical: inclusive `>=` (2 fail), dropping the 60-min floor (1 fail), dropping the 12-bar rule (2 fail), non-finite returning TRUE (4 fail). ⚠️ The GUARD itself is not unit-reachable - it lives in the loop - so it is covered by the A/B above, recorded in the issue. |
| 197 | **A DATA SET IS ONE DECISION, SO THE LIBRARY GROUPS BY SET — AND `?? {}` IS NOT A SAFE DEFAULT FOR A NUMBER YOU THEN DEREFERENCE.** Requirement (2026-11-17): Do we have a UI that acts as a library for data sets… It would be good to be able to name them: make their period clear but also make the key reasons for the set being stored clear. The infrastructure already existed — `backtest_period` has `name` + `note`, `PATCH`/`DELETE /backtest/periods/:id` exist, and `/lab/backtest-data` was the library — but **the list was flat, one row per symbol**. Measured: `dev-window-v1` **27 rows**, the two extreme-mover sets **10 each**, so **48 rows rendered as 20 identical names repeating one paragraph twice over**, and the UI offered only *Re-import* — a note could be written at capture and never corrected. **Built:** `src/core/backtest/period-sets.js` groups rows by **`name + timeframe + from + to`** — ⚠️ **timeframe AND window are in the key deliberately**: `dev-window-v1` exists at 5m/15m/1h/1d for one calendar window and those are different data, so merging them would let a 5m bar count be read as describing the 1d window; and two captures can share a name over different windows. Grouping now yields **7 sets from 48 rows** on live data. Each set shows its name, its **window**, its symbol count / bars / runs, its **reason** (the note, clamped to 3 lines), a health label, and an *Edit name & note* that `PATCH`es **every member** (a set is one reason, so editing one row would leave nine stale copies — `noteVariants` counts that drift and is what caught a real em-dash/hyphen divergence I had introduced). Delete is two-step and deliberately does **not** pass `cascade`, so the server's refusal while runs reference a set is shown rather than forced. ⚠️ **The reason `ready` ≠ `partial`:** a set where 6 of 10 symbols were captured looks complete from its name and its bar count. Live proof: all four `dev-window-v1` sets render **partly captured · 0 bars** — they were never imported. ⚠️ **AND A CRASH FIX:** `BacktestDataPage` had `const summary = data.summary ?? {}` then called `summary.totalBars.toLocaleString()`. When the payload lost `summary` — a proxy 502 while the backend restarts is enough — the page threw `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'toLocaleString')` and the **whole page, library included, vanished behind the error boundary**. An empty object is not a safe default for a field that is then dereferenced: it converts "no data" into a crash that takes the rest of the page with it. `?? {}` was replaced with explicit numeric zeros. ⚠️ **Sets are NOT seeded and will not be** — owner: *"users will need to work to establish their own testing"*; verified no `backtestPeriod.create` exists outside the service. **REVERSE BY** deleting `period-sets.js` and restoring the flat table; no stored data changed. | Primary, 2026-11-17 (#1291) | 🟢 `period-sets.test.js` **9/9** (`node --test`) pins the four-key grouping, the timeframe split, the first-non-empty-note rule, `null` vs `0` bar counts, and `partial` never reading as `ready`. Live: 48 rows → 7 sets, both mover sets `ready`, four `dev-window-v1` sets correctly `partly captured`. `PATCH` and `DELETE` each exercised against the running API and restored. |
| 198 | **DELETING DUPLICATES WITHOUT GUARDING THE WRITER IS A CLEANUP THAT UNDOES ITSELF - the two news pipelines stored 17,245 of the same story twice, and the writer is now the thing that keeps it clean.** `newsarticle` (hourly `@Cron` via `NewsImportService`, read by 9 modules, owns the `NewsSource` taxonomy plus `category`/`impact`) and `news_item` (three research ingesters, read only by `screener` as a fallback) both hold market news. Measured: of **28,350 `news_item` rows, 17,245 carried a URL already in `newsarticle`** - 61% of the smaller table and 42% of the larger, written within the same hour. ⚠️ **ALL 17,245 were `fh:finnhub`**, so the duplication was one poller, not a design. ⚠️ **The obvious fix is wrong**: each ingester dedupes on `sourceHash` (namespaced `rss:` / `fh:` / `av:`), which only ever compares against `news_item` itself, and the RSS scraper re-polls every **120 s** while Finnhub re-polls every **300 s** - so a `DELETE` alone is undone within minutes. The **writer** had to be guarded first: `isAlreadyInCanonicalNews()` asks whether `newsarticle` already holds the URL, and all three ingesters call it before `create`. Only then were the 17,245 rows removed (**28,350 -> 11,105**, the 11,105 genuinely unique stories kept - nothing was lost). Exact-match on `url`, deliberately: trimming, case-folding or stripping query strings would each WIDEN "duplicate" beyond the set actually measured, and a false positive silently drops a story that was never a duplicate. ⚠️ **An empty URL and a FAILED probe both mean "insert"**, uniquely here: the two failures are not symmetric - a duplicate is recoverable and named by the gate, a story never stored is gone. | 2026-11-17 (#1290) | 🟢 enforced - `scripts/validate-news-dedupe.cjs` fails when any `news_item.url` appears in `newsarticle.url`, and is wired into `npm --prefix nest run db:check` (not `prebuild`, because it queries MySQL and must not turn a database outage into a build failure). Canaried in BOTH directions: before the cleanup it exited **1** naming 17,245 rows and `fh:finnhub` as the sole offender; after, it exits **0**. `news-dedupe.spec.ts` 8/8 pins exact-match pass-through, empty/whitespace/null meaning insert, and fail-open on a probe error. The COMPILED guard was also exercised against the live database: a real `newsarticle` URL -> `true` (skip), an unseen URL -> `false` (insert), `'` -> `false`. ⚠️ NOT isolated: the pollers wrote 0 rows in the first cycle after the guard went live, which is consistent with the guard AND with the category allowlist filtering everything - so the guard's effectiveness is proved by the live-DB probe above, not by that observation. |

| 200 | **A TRAILING STOP THE UI OFFERS AND THE ENGINE IGNORES IS NOT A SETTING, IT IS A SILENT DIFFERENCE IN WHAT WAS MEASURED - the bot's simulated trailing stop is now wired.** `trading_strategy` carries `simTslEnabled` / `simTslActivationPct` / `simTslDistancePct`; **3 of 64 active bots** have them on (NVDA 2/1, USDJPY 1/0.5, XAGUSD 0.5/0.25) and the UI edits all three. `settings-inventory.ts` recorded the group as `applied: 'ignored' - "Zero references."`, and `git grep` confirms the engine never read them. | 204 | **A DATA MIGRATION THAT IS NOT IDEMPOTENT CANNOT BE RESUMED, AND ONE PARSED FROM TAB-SEPARATED TEXT CANNOT BE TRUSTED - the news stories were moved with a mapper, not a copy-paste.** #1290 measured 17,245 URLs in BOTH news paths; the duplication itself was already removed by guarding the writers (`news-dedupe.ts`). What remained was STRUCTURAL: 11,339 stories existing only in `news_item`, reachable by ONE consumer through a fallback nobody would think to look for. `scripts/migrate-news-to-newsarticle.cjs` moves them: ADDITIVE ONLY, `INSERT IGNORE` on `newsarticle.url` (UNIQUE) so an interrupted run resumes by re-running, ⚠️ **I FIRST WROTE THAT IT WOULD STORE "ONE ROW PER SYMBOL", AND THAT WAS FALSE** - `newsarticle.url` is **UNIQUE**, so a second row for the same story is discarded by `INSERT IGNORE`. Measured: **102 of 10,396** attributed stories carry more than one ticker and every one kept only its FIRST (`NVDA,DIA` -> `NVDA`). The rows were built per symbol, the insert silently kept one, and the run reported success - the "plausible output, no error" shape this repo keeps finding, written by me while documenting it. The mapper now returns ONE row and REPORTS the dropped secondary tickers (currently **107**), so the limitation is a number in the output rather than a sentence in a comment. Storing secondary tickers needs a `newsarticle_symbol` join table, which is a schema change and belongs in its own issue. Measured result: **10,453 rows added, 10,396 of 10,396 attributed URLs now present, 0 missing**; 993 skipped because they carry no symbol and `newsarticle.symbol` is NOT NULL, which is a decision rather than an oversight - an unattributed story could never match a ticker query anyway. ⚠️ **TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY RUNNING IT, both the repository's signature shape**: (1) the first version parsed `mysql --batch` output as TSV, and `summary` is TEXT containing NEWLINES, so 490 rows read as "no url" and 123 as "no title" against a SQL count of ZERO empty urls - plausible output, no error, wrong underneath. Replaced with `JSON_ARRAYAGG` + parse from the first `[`, because the batch HEADER echoes the whole multi-line statement so counting lines is unsafe. (2) `--apply` with 200-row chunks died on E2BIG (`spawn ... pid 0`), because `mysql-cli` passes SQL as a command-line ARGUMENT and Windows caps that near 32 KB; now 40 rows per statement. ⚠️ **AND A DRY RUN WROTE**: the first version created 2 `NewsSource` rows before checking `--apply`. Harmless (same shape `news-import.service.ts:344` creates on demand) but wrong - a command reporting "nothing written" while writing is the defect these scripts exist to prevent, so creation is now gated and the dry run substitutes a placeholder id. | 2026-11-18 (#1290) | 🟡 partial - `--self-test` **14/14** (one-row-per-symbol expansion, severity/impact bands, null sentiment 0 rather than directional, and four skip reasons that must never vanish silently). **Behaviour gate:** 10,396/10,396 attributed URLs present in `newsarticle`; `screener` still answers NVDA/TSLA/META with 5 stories each; `build:api` exit 0. 🔴 **NOT DONE - the writers still write `news_item`**, so the split is still live for NEW stories and the `screener` fallback cannot be removed yet. Rewiring the three ingesters onto `newsarticle` is the remaining land, and doing it in the other order would have hidden every newly-ingested story from the one consumer. |
| 205 | **CONVERGENCE HAD TO LAND IN ONE ORDER AND ONLY ONE - migrate the DATA, then move the WRITERS, then the READER, and the gate had to be INVERTED halfway.** #1290 final: the three research ingesters now write `newsarticle` through ONE shared `news-writer.ts` instead of three near-identical `store()` methods that differed only in a URL prefix. ⚠️ **THE ORDER WAS LOAD-BEARING**: the ingesters were guarded to write only what `newsarticle` did not already hold, so every `news_item` row was by definition a story the canonical table lacked. Removing `screener`'s fallback before rewiring the writers would have hidden every freshly-ingested story from its only consumer; rewiring first made the fallback provably dead. Both were done in that order and `screener` still answers NVDA/TSLA/META. ⚠️ **THE GATE HAD TO BE INVERTED, NOT DELETED.** `validate-news-dedupe.cjs` asserted ZERO overlap, which was right while the tables duplicated each other and becomes BACKWARDS after migration - overlap is now the success condition. A gate left checking the old direction fails forever after a successful fix, which is exactly how a real gate gets allowlisted and stops being read. It now asserts the SUBSET: every ATTRIBUTABLE `news_item` story must be present in `newsarticle`. ⚠️ And it separates **992 unattributable** rows (no symbol; `newsarticle.symbol` is NOT NULL so they can never migrate, and a ticker query could not match them anyway) from failures - counting them as unmigrated would have kept the gate red permanently. ⚠️ **`news-dedupe.ts` WAS DELETED, not kept**: with one write path the guard it enforced is structurally impossible, and its only remaining importer was its own spec. ⚠️ That deletion then failed `validate-repo-hygiene` with **exit 2 (CANNOT JUDGE)**, because the credential scan reads `git ls-files` and the deleted paths were still in the index - it refused to pass over files it could not open rather than reporting a clean scan, which is the behaviour this repo asks for. Staging the deletions fixed it. | 2026-11-18 (#1290) | 🟢 enforced - `news-writer.spec.ts` **21/21** (source taxonomy incl. an unknown prefix returning null, impact bands with NaN low, sentiment accepting both a word and a clamped float, and six refusals each naming its reason). **Behaviour gate against the LIVE database with the COMPILED writer:** first call `{"status":"written","symbol":"NVDA","secondarySymbolsDropped":1}`, second `{"status":"duplicate"}`, stored row `sentiment 0.42 / impact high`, **0 rows in `news_item`**, synthetic row deleted after. Gate now reports `10397 also in newsarticle · 0 attributable-but-unmigrated · 992 unattributable`, exit 0. Suites **33 / 479 tests pass**; `build:api` exit 0. ⚠️ The pollers wrote nothing in a 4-minute window - nothing new arrived - so the LIVE ingest path was not observed; the writer was exercised directly instead, and that gap is stated rather than papered over. |
| 206 | **A BLOCK OF EXPLANATORY PROSE ON A PAGE IS A STATUS CARD THAT HAS NOT BEEN ASKED FOR YET — and a status card must reveal the same detail on hover, on FOCUS and on click, never on hover alone.** Owner review of `/development/movers-treatment`, 2026-11-18: *"I do not like screen clutter - large blocks of information need to be displayed through the users desire to see it"* … *"which basically constitutes a status (do not need all the text displayed until the user hovers over the status)"* … *"Maybe a small icon in the top right corner of cards that have additional information would help users realise that there is more to read."* Four elements were converted, not deleted: the visible `<h2>` became `h1.sr-only` (the nav and breadcrumb already name the page; *"Every page MUST have exactly one <h1>"* still holds) with the description behind `PageInfoPopover`; the three-paragraph INACTIVE strategy panel became one `StatusCard`; the seven flat tallies became `StatusCard`s carrying each state's definition quoted from `classifyTreatment()`; and the hot-stock paragraph became two **derived** cards. **WHAT:** new `src/components/StatusCard.jsx` + `status-card.css`, documented on `/appearance/surfaces` as the fourth display pattern beside `InfoNote`, `NoticeBlock` and `PageInfoPopover`. **WHY NOT HOVER-ONLY:** `accessibility-policy` forbids it (*"No hover-only interactions"*), and most of this app is used on a phone; hover and focus reveal, click pins, and `aria-expanded` reports `pinned \|\| hovered` — the rendered truth, not the clicked one — because announcing "collapsed" while the detail is on screen is the same defect as a status field reporting a state it is not in. **WHY THE DETAIL IS AN OVERLAY:** `page-info-popover.css` records that a native `[popover]` lands at the wrong coordinates in this app because `motion` wrappers set `transform` and become the containing block for `position: fixed`; this component positions against its own `position: relative` card and cannot inherit that trap. **ALSO:** the hot-stock readiness count moved from hand-written prose to `src/core/hot-stock-readiness.js`, reading `enforcement` from `GET /api/trading/strategies/bot-rules` — the prose happened to be right, but a hand-typed count goes stale silently the day a fifth setting is added. **AND THE CUE IS A CHEVRON, NOT AN ⓘ — the requirement rejected the first build's corner ⓘ on sight:** *"I don't like the little 'i' in the corner of the cards. Look for something that has been a standard approach in other designs online."* The convention was wrong, not merely the taste: ⓘ means "information ABOUT this thing" and is what `PageInfoPopover` uses on a heading, whereas these cards **expand** — and every mainstream system signals that with a chevron (Material 3 expansion panels, Apple disclosure groups, the Bootstrap and WAI-ARIA accordion patterns; NN/g separates chevron = "expands in place" from arrow = "goes somewhere else"). It is drawn with two borders rather than a typed glyph so no platform font can change it, it sits on the right edge in a reserved lane so a wrapped label cannot run under it, and it rotates 180° when open. It started at 5×5 with 1.5px borders in `--text-faint` and **could not be seen at all** — a check of the built stylesheet at real size showed no chevron on any card — so it is now ~10px visual in `--text-dim`, because a cue nobody can see is the same as no cue. Its borders are declared as LONGHANDS with a `currentColor` fallback for a measured reason: `border-right: 2px solid var(--text-dim)` is a shorthand, and a failed `var()` invalidates the WHOLE declaration so the border resets to `style: none` and paints nothing — observed while rendering the built CSS without the theme adapter, where the cue silently vanished. `--text-dim` is injected at runtime by `src/theme/adapters/vanilla.js` and is not declared in CSS, so that is a real first-paint state, not a hypothetical. The card also lifts its border and background on hover/focus, because a 5px glyph is not the whole affordance — the surface has to respond before the reader has to find it. **REVERSE BY** deleting `StatusCard.jsx`/`status-card.css`/`hot-stock-readiness.js` and restoring the `<h2>` plus the three text blocks; no stored data changed. | Primary, 2026-11-18 (#1296) | 🟢 `src/core/hot-stock-readiness.test.js` **6/6** (`node --test`), canaried both ways — inverting `!== 'none'` to `=== 'none'` fails 3 of 6, and the restore was verified byte-for-byte. CSS guarded by `validate-css-tokens`, `validate-css-parses`, `validate-no-notice-bar`, `validate-css-imports`. ⚠️ The **hover/focus/click reveal itself is UNGUARDED** — this repo has no React test harness, so the interaction is covered by `qa-status-card-001` (a procedure someone runs) and not by a gate. |

| 208 | **A DECLARED RELATION THAT CAN NEVER RESOLVE IS WORSE THAN NO RELATION - `score.strategyId` holds a PROTOCOL SLUG, and the false FK is now gone.** Measured 2026-11-18 (#1288): of **2,740** rows written in one hour, **2,740 matched `protocol.slug`** and **0 matched `scoring_strategy.id`** (which are UUIDs). `schema.prisma` nonetheless declared `strategy Strategy @relation(fields: [strategyId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)` - false for every row - and the live table has **NO FK constraint at all** (`information_schema.KEY_COLUMN_USAGE` returns nothing for `score`), so nothing enforced it and nothing complained. ⚠️ The cost is not a crash: an `include: { strategy: true }` would return `null` for every row with no error, which reads as "this score has no strategy" - the same shape as `score_snapshot.protocolId` (a slug in a column named for an id) and `trading_strategy.protocolId` (slug-or-UUID). Removed from BOTH sides (`Score.strategy` and `Strategy.scores`) with the real contents documented on the column, and `tsc` after `prisma generate` exited **0**, which is the proof nothing depended on it - a compile error would also have been a valid answer, and better than a silent null. ⚠️ **REVERSE BY renaming the column to `protocolId` and pointing it at `protocol.slug`**, which is what it contains; that touches the 8 modules reading `score`, so it is a separate change. ⚠️ **AND THE AUDIT'S "score + score_snapshot is doubled data" WAS TOO STRONG**: they are written together (~1:1 rows per symbol+minute) but are NOT redundant - `score` holds the per-directive `breakdown` json that `score_snapshot` has no column for, and covers **98 distinct protocols against 68**, so it is not derivable from the snapshot. | 209 | **TWO TABLES HOLDING DIFFERENT POPULATIONS ON PURPOSE IS NOT DUPLICATION - `score` and `score_snapshot` must NOT be converged, and I twice called a documented design a defect.** Measured 2026-11-18 (#1288). `score` covers **98** distinct protocol slugs against `score_snapshot`'s **68**, and in the last 24h **6 protocols scored but never snapshotted** - which I first reported as a "gap" and then as a "live divergence". ⚠️ **Both were wrong.** All **606 of 606** of those rows have `value = 0`: every one is a NO_READING row, and `protocol-gating.service.ts` already documents, ~40 lines above the write, why they must never reach the snapshot: `score_snapshot` is the RARITY distribution, and `composite: 0` every two minutes "would fill that distribution with zeros, LOWER every percentile, and silently reclassify every live score as rarer than it is. Rarity drives position sizing bands, so polluting it is a money-path change." ⇒ `score` is the HISTORY table and records the empty reading; `score_snapshot` is the RARITY distribution and must stay free of zeros. The 6 protocols are not invisible to gating - they have no reading to gate, and their `symbol_protocol_assignment` count is 0, which is consistent. ⚠️ **The remaining differences are narrow and do not justify convergence**: `score` holds `breakdown` json with no equivalent column, it carries a documented **9-day hole (#1117)** whose cause is unproven, and **nothing on a money path reads it** - the gate reads `score_snapshot`. Retiring it would save write volume on a table only diagnostics and the backtest archive read: a cost/benefit question, not a correctness one, and not worth a change across 8 readers without a measured benefit. | 2026-11-18 (#1288) | 🟢 enforced as a DECISION TO LEAVE IT - no code change, and the reason is recorded at the site (`protocol-gating.service.ts` skipRaritySnapshot block) and here, so the next audit does not re-open it. ⚠️ **THE LESSON IS THE THIRD INSTANCE THIS SESSION**: I called a documented, deliberate design a defect twice in ONE comment. Measuring before asserting is not sufficient when the claim is about INTENT - the intent has to be read from the comments at the site, not inferred from the numbers. |
| 210 | **A DISCOVERY FEED MAY RECORD A SYMBOL BUT NEVER ACTIVATE IT — and the vocabulary it writes must be the COLUMN'S, because a case difference is invisible to SQL and not to JavaScript.** Requirement (2026-11-18) (#1299): *"We cannot activate every symbol that appears in top losers or top winners else by the end of a month we'll have hundreds of active symbols being monitored."* The arithmetic makes it a hard rule: the feed offers **~95 gainers and ~174 losers per fetch**, and `symbol.active` is what the scoring and monitoring paths read, so activating on sight converts discovery into an unbounded workload and into a universe indistinguishable from one the requirement settled on. `SymbolDiscoveryService.recordDiscovered()` is therefore INSERT-ONLY — new rows get `active = false`, an existing dormant row is left alone (a re-discovery is not an event), and an existing `active = 1` row is **never touched**, because activation was a decision someone made and discovery has no standing to reverse it. ⚠️ The third row is the one guarded: a naive `upsert` with `update: { active: false }` would DEACTIVATE an owner-activated symbol every time it appeared in a movers list — worse than activating one, because it removes a symbol from monitoring while looking like a successful write. The spec asserts `update`/`upsert` are never called. ⚠️ **AND THE WRITE ITSELF WAS WRONG FOR A DAY, IN A WAY NO SQL CHECK COULD SEE.** `symbolType()` returned UPPERCASE, so `symbol.type` held `STOCK` ×120 / `ETF` ×11 against an established lowercase vocabulary (`stock` 233 · `forex` 15 · `etf` 2 · …) — the only uppercase writer among four. The column collates **`utf8mb4_unicode_ci`**, so `WHERE type = 'stock'` matched all 353 and every `GROUP BY`, every Prisma `equals` and every diagnostic answered correctly, while two case-SENSITIVE comparisons in JavaScript disagreed: `symbols.service.ts`'s `isEquity` answered `false`, **skipping the whole 5-adapter profile chain** and telling the UI *"A STOCK instrument has no company profile"* — a fabricated fact — and `import-jobs.service.ts` skipped the Yahoo `.L`/`.CO` retry, returning **no bars** for a foreign-listed discovery. `suitability.service.ts` was already safe (it lowercases both sides), so the scoring gate was never affected, and that precision matters. The mapping is now a DICTIONARY onto the app's vocabulary rather than a lowercased copy of the feed's (`EQUITY -> stock`, `CRYPTOCURRENCY -> crypto`, `CURRENCY -> forex`, `FUTURE -> commodity`, absent -> `unknown`) — ⚠️ **not cosmetic**, because suitability compares a symbol's type to a protocol's declared `symbolTypes` by exact lowercased equality, so `cryptocurrency` would be REFUSED by a protocol declaring `crypto`; 131 rows were normalised (`stock=353` … 0 not lowercase), both consumers now lowercase (matching suitability's existing pattern, so a future writer cannot misclassify), and the spec asserts the WHOLE map rather than one branch. | 2026-11-18/19 (#1299) | 🟢 enforced — three ways. (1) `symbol-discovery.service.spec.ts` asserts `update`/`upsert` are never called, so "never activate" is a build gate via `nest` `prebuild`'s jest run. (2) The same spec asserts every mapping and passthrough is LOWERCASE and that the mappings land in the real vocabulary; canaried by mutating `equity: 'stock'` -> `equity: 'STOCK'` (**3 failed -> 26 passed**, restore verified byte-identical). (3) Both consumers lowercase what they read, so the class is closed rather than the instance. ⚠️ **The live canary mattered more than the unit one**: with `BP.type = 'STOCK'` on disk the endpoint still returned `profileStatus="ok"` from finnhub, and the restore was verified by hex (`73746F636B`) rather than by eye. ⚠️ **NOT guarded, and said out loud:** provenance is not recorded — `tags` is a curated vocabulary and discovery writes only its five factual columns — so **we cannot, after the fact, say which rows came from the feed.** `active = 0` is the marker that matters (it is exactly the set not being monitored), and the panel reports `created`/`existing` per fetch, but the gap is real and is recorded in the issue rather than left to be discovered. |
| 211 | **ONE ROUTE, TWO HANDLERS — and the loser looked like the data source.** `GET /api/database/table-stats` was declared twice: `database/database.controller.ts` and `modules/system/database-stats.controller.ts`, both `@Controller('database') @Get('table-stats')`, with DIFFERENT shapes (array of `TableStat` vs a record keyed by Prisma model). Nest lets the first module in `AppModule.imports` win, and `DatabaseModule` is registered before `SystemModule`, so the system-module handler was unreachable for its whole life — while holding a 26-model hardcoded list that reads like this endpoint's source. **DELETED, not left in place:** an edit to an unreachable handler looks exactly like a fix and changes nothing. ⚠️ **THE PAGE WAS WRITTEN FOR THE LOSER**, and its shape mismatch was silent: `tableStats.data?.tables['Symbol']` on an ARRAY is `undefined`, so the **Last Insert column rendered `—` for every table** on a page whose whole purpose is to report it — measured: the API returned 130 rows carrying `lastInsert` (`alert` = 2026-11-17T19:40:07Z) and the page discarded all of them. ⚠️ **THE PAGE ALSO NAMED SEVEN TABLES THAT DO NOT EXIST** (`DataSourcesMeta`, `CacheMeta`, `StrategyMeta`, `WatchlistMeta`, `CronMeta`, `ProcessMeta`, `NewsMeta`), each chip-labelled `installed`/`present` with a phpMyAdmin link that 404s — six of the seven are the unused `…Meta` tables dropped in #1288, and the page had simply never been told. Removed, and a table the stats feed does not know now renders **`not in db`** rather than as a working table, so the next drop shows as a visible gap. ⚠️ **`SHOW TABLE STATUS.Rows` IS AN INNODB ESTIMATE, NOT A COUNT** — measured on this database: `newsarticle` **34,821 vs 52,048 (−33%)**, `score` 12,482 vs 19,103 (−35%), `position` **25 vs 11 (+127%)**, `marketbar` ~0% (large table, sampled more). Presenting that as "Rows" would have repeated the exact defect being fixed, so the endpoint gained an opt-in `?tables=a,b` that issues real `COUNT(*)` and returns `rowCountSource: 'exact' | 'estimate'` per row — **~0.4–0.5 s for the page's 19 tables against ~2.9 s for all 130**, which is why it is narrowed rather than always-on. ⚠️ **AND THREE LITERALS WENT**: `SHOW TABLE STATUS FROM \`tradingcs\`` (this deployment's schema name — on any other install it throws, and the throw was caught and returned as an empty table list), `db=tradingcs` in the phpMyAdmin link (the API now reports `SELECT DATABASE()`), and a footer that **instructed the reader to run `npx prisma db push`** — the one command this repo bans, because it drops every table with no model, 13 of them here. **REVERSE BY** restoring the footer text, dropping the `?tables=` parameter and reading `Rows` again; no stored data changed. | Primary, 2026-11-18/19 (#1292) | 🟢 enforced — `database.controller.spec.ts` **8/8**, covering the three silent-wrongness paths a `?tables=` value introduces: an unknown name must be REFUSED and named (a skipped name renders as an empty table), the allowlist must be the schema's own table list and not a regex (which would have admitted `information_schema`/`fake_table`), and duplicates must collapse (MySQL rejects a repeated column alias — a 500 naming none of the cause). Canaried by mutator, all three observed failing and each restore verified by sha256: no dedupe → duplicate test fails; `if (false as boolean)` on the refusal → refusal tests fail; regex allowlist → the well-formed-name test fails. ⚠️ The first canary run reported **CANARY INVALID** because a multi-line anchor used `\n` against a CRLF file — the anchor landed zero times, which is the trap this register warns about, and it was caught by requiring `hits === 1` rather than trusting the pass. Live: `?tables=…` → **19/19 exact** (`position` 11, matching `COUNT(*)`), no param → 130 rows all `estimate`, `?tables=Score,NotARealTable` → **400** `not a table in this database: notarealtable`. Page rendered in the browser: **`Position 11`** (estimate had said 25), `Score 17,013`, header now reads **Last Write** with a tooltip saying it is `Update_time` and may be NULL. ⚠️ NOT exercised live: the throw-on-unreadable path — the mechanism it replaces was measured instead (`SHOW TABLE STATUS FROM \`no_such_database\`` → `ERROR 1049`), and the page's half is covered by `feed-state.test.js`, which runs in `prebuild`. |
| 212 | **A FEATURE THAT GROUPS RECORDS MUST NOT INVENT THE KEY IT GROUPS BY — and the guard it needs can be one whose removal is INVISIBLE without a warning.** #1295 auto-files unnamed backtest runs into a bucket per protocol. `protocolId` arrives from the caller verbatim, and one caller (June 2026, before protocols were minted per strategy) sent the literal `'default'`: the bucket was created with `protocolId='default'`, which resolves to nothing. `validate-protocol-refs.cjs` failed on it — correctly, because a bucket keyed on a non-existent protocol is unreachable by every protocol-filtered report and its label reads as though a protocol called "default" is real. ⚠️ **THE 11 RUNS ARE NOT THE DEFECT AND WERE NOT TOUCHED** — real measurements from 2026-06/08 whose own `protocolId` is that placeholder; the defect was PROMOTING an unchecked condition (`backtestrun`) into a CHECKED one (`backtest_experiment`). They were unfiled and the bucket deleted, restoring exactly the state they were in before the feature existed; all 11 rows are still stored. The bucket is now created only when the protocol RESOLVES, keyed on its SLUG (#800's canonical form, so a UUID caller files into the same bucket rather than minting a second one for the same protocol), and the lookup sits INSIDE the `try` because filing is bookkeeping and must never be why a completed backtest is lost. ⚠️ **THE FIRST TEST FOR THIS WAS A FALSE PASS, AND THE CANARY IS WHAT FOUND IT.** With the existence check deleted, `protocol.slug` throws on null, `catch` swallows it, and the function returns `null` — identical outcome, suite still GREEN. The guard now also emits a `logger.warn` naming the value, which is the only part of it a test can observe, and the assertion moved onto that warning. **REVERSE BY** restoring `protocolId` verbatim and dropping the warning; no run rows changed. | Primary, 2026-11-18 (#1295/#1292) | 🟢 enforced — `backtest-experiment.spec.ts` **15/15** (4 new: refusal for an unknown protocol, filing for a real one, a UUID caller storing the SLUG, and a lookup that throws leaving the run unfiled). | 214 | **A BACKTEST'S SIDE COMES FROM THE PROTOCOL WHEN THE CALLER DOES NOT SAY — because the old `'long'` default returned a COMPLETE RESULT WITH ZERO TRADES for every short protocol.** Measured 2026-11-19 (#1300). `run()` destructured `directionMode = 'long'`, so `momentum-stock-v1-short` backtested without an explicit direction ran with the wrong side and `decideEntry()` — which is correct, and enumerates the four signal classes rather than prefix-matching — returned `blockedBy: 'direction-mode'` for every `SELL`/`STRONG_SELL`. The fix is `directionFromProtocol()`, a pure function (extracted for the same reason `entry-side.ts` was: the decision sat behind Prisma and a bar loader with no assertion), and it is **derivation, not coercion** — a `SHORT_ONLY` protocol run long blocks 100% of its own signals, so there is no configuration in which the old default measured anything, and an EXPLICIT `directionMode` still wins untouched so a deliberate contrarian run works and records what was asked for. ⚠️ **`BOTH` is deliberately NOT mapped to `'both'`** — that mode is refused on the run path (`refuseUnrunnableDirection`), so mapping it here would turn a refusal into a stored, unrunnable run. ⚠️ An unreadable protocol falls back to `'long'` **with a warning logged**, because `'long'` is the historical default kept for compatibility, not a safe inference — "cannot judge" is reported rather than rendered as a decision. ⚠️ The **bot path already knew**: `backtest.service.ts` documents this exact defect at L1115 and derives the direction for a named `legProtocolId`, so the plain `protocolId`-with-no-bot path was the hole and the fix belongs in `run()` where both paths meet. | 2026-11-19 (#1300) | 🟢 enforced — `direction-from-protocol.spec.ts` pins five branches: SHORT_ONLY→short (the regression itself), LONG_ONLY→long, explicit wins (including a contrarian `'long'` on a SHORT_ONLY protocol), `BOTH`/`LONG_SHORT` never deriving `'both'`, and absent→long. Canaried by mutating `return 'short'` → `return 'long'` for the SHORT_ONLY branch: **1 failed → 5 passed**, restore verified byte-identical. ⚠️ **The live proof is the point, not the unit test**: the identical `POST /api/backtest/run` that returned `signals=0 trades=0` before the build returns `signals=173 trades=173 netPnl=-5132.2709` after it, matching the explicit-`directionMode: 'short'` run to the penny. ⚠️ **And the near-miss is recorded**: I first reported this as evidence that the SHORT SIDE WAS UNWIRED (#770) and posted that to the epic, because my "isolated" comparison varied in a second way I had not checked — one run carried the field and one did not. | 215 | **AN ADVISORY FLAG ON A MONEY PATH IS A FLAG THAT GETS SKIPPED — the movers payload now carries a DECISION (`entryAllowed`), not just the observations `stale` and `delayed`.** Measured 2026-11-19 (#1283). | 219 | **A PLAN TO CHANGE A PROTOCOL BELONGS IN A TABLE WITH A LIFECYCLE, NOT IN PROSE — and a PLACEHOLDER for an unbuilt directive must never be a registered directive.** Requirement (2026-11-18) (#1311): *"Create a new procedure for suggested/queued changes to make to a protocol: might require a new database table… Eventually we will automate backtesting and A/B comparison to try changes automatically."* `protocol_change_suggestion` holds `scope` (protocol/bot/strategy) + `protocolSlug` + `botId` + `kind` + `targetKey` + `currentValue` + `suggestedValue` + `rationale` + `status` (queued→accepted→applied, or rejected/blocked) + `priority` + `issueUrl`. ⚠️ **PROSE CANNOT HOLD A LIFECYCLE**, which is why a list of intended changes in a document decays into a wish list nobody can act on, audit, or automate — the exact next step the requirement named. ⚠️ **`protocolSlug`, not a UUID FK**: `backtest/run` REJECTS a protocol UUID with *"not found or has no directive groups"* and wants the slug, and `protocol_directive_change.protocolSlug` already uses it, so a UUID here would be a third convention for one identity. ⚠️ **`currentValue = null` IS NOT ZERO** — it means "this does not exist yet", and a missing directive and one scoring 0 are different facts; | 223 | **AN ACCOUNT ID MUST NAME THE ACCOUNT — and when a persisted key is renamed, the OLD key is READ as a fallback, never rewritten on a guess.** `GET /api/portfolio/accounts` returned six MT5 rows for three logins because the id was `mt5:<login>` and one login is configured on BOTH bridges (measured 2026-11-19: `mt5:10011309743`, `mt5:62149650`, `mt5:52918025` each twice, with different `connected` values). Id is now `mt5:<brokerId>:<login>`; the page builds the same key (`BrokersPage.jsx`), and its old comment — *"the same login on two bridges is ONE account, so both rows honestly agree"* — was the wrong model written down, which is why it survived. ⚠️ **A ROW WITH NO `brokerId` KEEPS THE LEGACY ID** rather than becoming `mt5:?:<login>`: an id that cannot name its bridge must not be handed a unique-looking one. ⚠️ **THE MIGRATION IS A READ FALLBACK, NOT A REWRITE, AND THAT IS THE POINT:** one legacy row controlled BOTH bridges at once, so copying it to two per-bridge keys is a guess about intent — right today, wrong the moment they diverge. The fallback needs no guess; the first write to the new key supersedes it, and the legacy row is left in place because deleting it is unrecoverable and read-only-when-absent stops being true after one toggle. | 2026-11-19 (#1205) | 🟢 enforced — `portfolio-accounts-identity.spec.ts` 5 cases (unique ids for a login on two bridges; same-bridge rows still distinct; legacy toggle reaches BOTH per-bridge keys; a per-bridge write supersedes the legacy one and the two may then disagree; a row without `brokerId` keeps the legacy id). Canaried both ways: reverting the id shape fails **4**, removing the legacy fallback fails **2**, restore 12/12. Verified live: 6 rows, `duplicate ids: NONE`, each labelled `· bridge 2` / `· bridge 3`; Screen Options rendered all six and the console had **zero** duplicate-key warnings. ⚠️ I did not re-run the old build to watch the warning appear — the "before" is the issue's record plus my own API measurement of three duplicated ids. |
| 224 | **A TICK THAT MEASURED NOTHING HAS NO CONFIDENCE, AND A PROTOCOL THAT CANNOT BE RESOLVED IS AN ERROR RATHER THAN A DEFAULT.** `GET /api/signals/envelope/{GER40,XAUUSD,EURUSD}?protocol=euro-stoxx-v1-long` returned `NO_DATA / composite 0 / confidence 35 / groups 3` for all three (#1267, measured 2026-11-19). Three unrelated symbols agreeing on 35 is arithmetic over an absence: `computeConfidence()` starts at a base of **50** and subtracts `min(\|signalPct − 65\| × 0.3, 15)`; with `signalPct: 0` that is 35. ⚠️ **The scorer already answered correctly** — `protocol-gating.service.ts` returns `confidence: 0` on its NO_DATA path and `enrichEnvelope()` OVERWROTE it, so the fix was to stop replacing a right answer, not to invent a new one. Separately, `getCached() ?? findBySlug()` left `protocol` undefined when both missed, and the code below then substituted `selectBaseTimeframe(undefined)` and `{ '1d': 200 }` for the named protocol's cadence and bar requirements — a different cadence reads a different bar array and scores differently, so the same question had two answers depending on cache temperature with nothing saying which. It now refuses with `UNKNOWN_PROTOCOL`. ⚠️ **Three states stay distinct:** `NO_DATA` (nothing directional this tick), `emptyEnvelope` (nothing measured at all), `UNKNOWN_PROTOCOL` (the question could not be asked). ⚠️ `composite 50` in the issue is the `emptyEnvelope` placeholder, documented at #1236 — NOT this defect, and the two are now separable. | 2026-11-19 (#1267) | 🟢 enforced — `signal-envelope-unread-and-unresolved.spec.ts` 4 cases, each canaried in BOTH directions: unread keeps `confidence: 0` (revert fails 1, and `not.toBe(35)` is the assertion that would have caught the original); a READ tick still gets a computed confidence (so the rule is not blanket-zero); an unresolved protocol refuses and **`scoreWithGating` is never called** (a returned score would look identical either way); a protocol resolved from a COLD cache still scores. Verified live: `confidence 35 → 0` on all three symbols, and `no-such-protocol-v1` now answers `UNKNOWN_PROTOCOL`. ⚠️ #1267 stays OPEN — its cold→warm transition is still unexplained and the on-demand-vs-ladder agreement guard is not built. |
| 225 | **A BOT'S BLOCKING RULE IS A QUEUED ROW THAT NAMES ITS ENFORCEMENT — NOT ITS VALUE. And a bot defined by a DAILY LIST must never be committed to a public repo.** Requirement (2026-11-19) (#1315): *"register a new bot (or two if a short and long bot is required) and its configuration (rules in use) as standard, and register placeholder changes…"* Two bots registered — `Movers V1 Long Bot` / `Movers V1 Short Bot` — both `active=0` + `mode=manual`, because the traded universe is a daily list (#1314) and `trading_strategy.symbol` is `NOT NULL`, so each carries a real ticker as a PLACEHOLDER and says so in its own `theory` prose. ⚠️ **`currentValue` on those 14 queued `bot-rule` rows holds the ENFORCEMENT, not the value** — every blocked column (`shortProtocolId`, `maxPositions`, `simTslEnabled`, `exhaustionObserveEnabled`, `hotStockEnabled`) exists and has a value; what blocks the strategy is that it `display-only`/`legacy-only` and therefore **cannot change a trade**. Writing the value would make a blocked rule read as a working one, which is precisely the lie `bot-rules.ts` was written to stop. ⚠️ **TWO BOTS, NOT ONE WITH BOTH LEGS**: `shortProtocolId` is `display-only` and 0 of 76 bots use it, so a single bot holding long and short would be a display claim with no enforcement. ⚠️ **AND THE NUMBERS ARE NOT IN THE REPO** — register row 177 owns it (*"a bot's numbers ARE the strategy"*, repo is PUBLIC), so they were created by a local script and captured into the GITIGNORED `strategy-seed-generated.local.ts` by `generate-strategy-seed.cjs` (verified present, `git status` clean), with a comment in `strategy-seed-data.ts` saying DO NOT add them to the tracked array. ⚠️ **The roadmap is now IN the code, at the sites where the wrong move would be made**: `bot-rules.ts` carries the 4-step sequence (protocols → bots+queue → non-fixed symbols → backtesting) and the rule that **the queue is the spec** — do not implement a `display-only` rule quietly, accept the row and record the A/B; `exit-consensus.service.ts` records that the "directive as verdict" design EXISTS with NO ACTOR (its only consumer is its read controller) and that directives must expose `{score, value, window}` before the Stop Guardian can stop recomputing deceleration. | 2026-11-19 (#1315) | 🟡 partial — the REGISTRATION is verified live (`GET /api/suggestions?botId=…` → `queued=7` per bot with exactly the seven blockers; browser: 36 queue rows, 14 bot-scoped; `generate-strategy-seed` → 75 bots, 3 skipped; typecheck + frontend build clean). ⚠️ **NOT guarded: nothing fails if a future agent activates these bots before #1314 exists** — they would then be dispatched on the placeholder ticker `AAPL` under `mode=manual` (so no automatic dispatch, which is the only reason this is partial rather than a live hazard). ⚠️ Also unguarded: nothing enforces that the three roadmap comments stay accurate as steps complete; they are prose, and prose does not fail a build. Stated rather than implied. |
| 226 | **A FAULT IS AN INSTANCE WITH AN OWNER, NOT A PHRASE — and the system that records them is bounded by a GUARDRAIL, because a taxonomy is cheap to add to and expensive to maintain.** Requirement (2026-11-19), accepting the primary agent's recommendation: persistent faults must be **regularly visitable**, the pathways they sit on should be **diagrammed from data**, and each must **link to its GitHub issue**. ACCEPTED: a named fault with an instance, a recurrence count, an owner and a generated view. REJECTED: a hand-drawn "every eventuality" diagram, and a registry nothing visits. ⚠️ **MEASURED, AND IT IS WHY THIS IS A RULING RATHER THAN A PLAN:** `ProviderDegradationService` keeps provider state in `new Map()` and **no `provider_degradation` table exists** — so the fault state `/api/brokers/status` reads is LOST ON RESTART; `decision_log.reason` is free text, so no decision reason can be counted; 57 `TCS-*` notice codes are UI copy with no history; and `alert.link` (varchar 500) exists while **nothing writes it**. ⇒ the parts exist but share no join key, which is why faults feel unvisited: there is no noun to visit. ⚠️ **BUILD ORDER IS DELIBERATELY INVERTED FROM THE PROPOSAL** — table + `faults.record(code, evidence)` writers, then a screen carrying a **"not visited in N days"** counter, and only then a **generated** diagram. A diagram is the most impressive artefact and the least useful without a registry behind it, and it is the one that rots. ⚠️ **THE CADENCE IS LOAD-BEARING AND NO BUILD CAN ENFORCE IT** — `REVIEW-QUEUE.md` exists because lists rot when nobody is prompted, so the counter must sit on a page already opened, never on a new page nobody opens. | 2026-11-19 (owner; no issue — the reasoning is the artefact) | 🟡 **partial, and the split is stated:** the REASONING is enforced — `scripts/validate-fault-taxonomy.cjs` runs in `prebuild` and fails on G1 (a `Fault` model missing `code, firstSeen, lastSeen, count, evidence, issueUrl, status`) and G3 (a hand-drawn fault diagram in `docs/` without a `GENERATED` marker). Self-test 9/9 both directions, and all three rules were canaried on the REAL tree, not only fixtures: a prose-only `Fault` appended to `schema.prisma` → exit 1 naming all seven missing fields (schema restored HASH-IDENTICAL); `.fault.deleteMany(` added to `alert-trigger.service.ts` → exit 1; `docs/FAULT-CANARY.md` containing a mermaid block → exit 1. 🔴 **NOT guarded, and no build can guard it:** nothing enforces the cadence, and nothing checks that the registry is ever built. The guard makes the WRONG shapes fail; it cannot make the right one appear. |
| 227 | **CLOSING AN ISSUE MAY RETIRE A FAULT CODE — IT MAY NOT DELETE ONE. The trigger was the right instinct; the verb was not.** Requirement (2026-11-19): proposed that closing an issue could be one of several triggers that also removes tracking and fault-taxonomy data. The trigger is accepted and correct — an issue close is exactly when a fault's ownership ends. The deletion is refused, because **the whole value of the taxonomy is recurrence**: a code removed when its issue closes destroys the evidence that it ever happened, so the next occurrence is indistinguishable from a new fault, and the count that made the registry worth building resets to zero. Retirement is therefore a `status` change (`active → retired`) and **the row stays**, so "this came back" remains an answerable question. ⚠️ **THE SAME RULE APPLIES TO THE EVIDENCE, NOT JUST THE CODE:** `firstSeen`, `count` and `evidence` are the record; pruning them to keep the table small is deletion wearing a different name. ⚠️ **AND A RETIRED CODE MUST NOT BE REUSED** — re-instating the same string for a different fault would silently merge two histories, which is worse than having none. ⚠️ Deletion of this data is permitted only by an explicit owner instruction naming the codes, never by an automated trigger. | 2026-11-19 (owner + primary) | 🟢 enforced for the mechanical half — `scripts/validate-fault-taxonomy.cjs` **G2** fails the build on any `fault.delete`, `fault.deleteMany` or `DELETE FROM fault` in `nest/src`, so the only removal path a future agent can reach is a status update. Canaried on the real tree: `.fault.deleteMany({ where: {} })` appended to `alert-trigger.service.ts` → exit 1 naming the call, restore → exit 0; the self-test additionally proves a `status: 'retired'` update PASSES (so the rule cannot be satisfied by banning the model entirely). 🔴 **NOT guarded:** nothing checks that a retirement was justified by quiet recurrence, and nothing prevents a retired code being reused — both are review-time judgements, and this row is what the reviewer cites. |
| 228 | **A RETIRED FAULT THAT IS OBSERVED AGAIN IS REPORTED, NOT REOPENED — a machine must not reverse a retirement decision silently.** `FaultsService.record()` writes `title`, `area`, `severity`, `lastSeen` and `count: { increment: 1 }` and deliberately OMITS `status`, so a recurrence moves the evidence while the human decision stands; the response carries `recurredAfterRetirement: true` and a warning is logged. Row 227 settled that a row is retired rather than deleted; it did not say what happens when the retired thing comes back, and that is what this row settles. ⚠️ **AUTO-REOPEN WAS REJECTED ON AUDITABILITY, NOT ON TASTE:** a status flipped back by a machine leaves no record of who decided the fix had failed, so the next reader cannot tell a fault that never stopped from one deliberately retired and returned. Reporting preserves both facts at once, and the same-millisecond case is handled honestly — `recurredSinceReview` treats an unordered pair as `false` rather than inventing a recurrence. | 2026-11-19 (primary; no issue — the reasoning is the artefact) | 🟢 enforced — `faults.service.spec.ts` asserts a retired row keeps `status: retired`, reaches `count: 2`, and reports `recurredAfterRetirement: true`; canaried by adding `status: 'open'` to the update map, which fails exactly that case while the other eight stay green, and the file was restored hash-identical. ⚠️ NOT guarded: nothing stops a future second write path from setting `status` directly — there is one write path today and that is the only reason the guard holds. |
| 229 | **A BOT'S SYMBOL SCOPE IS A DISCRIMINANT (`fixed` \| `feed`) — AND AN UNKNOWN VALUE REFUSES RATHER THAN BEING READ AS EITHER.** | A bot trades one instrument (`scopeMode='fixed'`, `symbol`) or whatever is on today's movers list (`scopeMode='feed'`, `feedList`, `symbol` becomes a PLACEHOLDER routing ignores). One pure authority, `feed-scope.ts`, answers "may this bot trade this symbol?" for `syncQueues()`. Every unknown REFUSES with a code: unrecognised `scopeMode`, missing feed, `entryAllowed=false`, NULL `feedList`, empty list, off-list ticker. `stale`/`delayed` are NOT re-read — `entryAllowed` already folds them, and two sources for one fact eventually disagree. Scope joins the strategy IDENTITY hash (`buildTreatment`), because two bots that may trade different instruments are not the same strategy. | 2026-11-19 (#1314) | 🟢 enforced — `feed-scope.spec.ts` (17 cases, canaried: disabling list membership fails 4), `signal-queue-feed-scope.spec.ts` (9 cases, canaried: disabling the refusal fails 5), and `strategy-key-coverage.spec.ts` (#1224) fails the build if either column is left unclassified. |

Every unknown REFUSES with a code: unrecognised `scopeMode`, missing feed, `entryAllowed=false`, NULL `feedList`, empty list, off-list ticker. `stale`/`delayed` are NOT re-read — `entryAllowed` already folds them, and two sources for one fact eventually disagree. Scope is part of the strategy IDENTITY hash (`buildTreatment`), because two bots that may trade different instruments are not the same strategy. Settled 2026-11-19 (#1314).
