| 230 | **DOCUMENTATION MUST NOT DESCRIBE THE REQUIREMENT AS A PERSON — the de-personalisation stands, the grammar is guarded.** Direction, 2026-11-19: a code comment reading *"the owner asked for ALL strategies to be listed here"* was rejected — a person does not narrate themselves as a third party. A global sweep then replaced "owner" with "requirement" across 401 files, which kept the intent and broke the sentences — a requirement approving a notice, a requirement reading a percentage off a card, a requirement owning a machine, and a commit-cadence line with a requirement doing the reading. **Those verbs need a person**, so an agent reading them cannot tell who decided — strictly worse than the word it replaced, because "the owner said X" is actionable and "the requirement said X" names nobody. The canonical form is `Requirement (2026-09-23): <the rule>` — a dated label, which no reader can mistake for a speaker. 225 replacements across 3 reviewed passes, plus one TABLE CELL restored (`\| tf \| Requirement \|` → `\| tf \| owner \|` in the ownership-axes measurement table, where the column names an owning MODULE and "Requirement" made a table of owners meaningless). ⚠️ **TECHNICAL "owner" IS A DIFFERENT WORD AND WAS NOT SWEPT** — `owning module`, `RAW_MANAGED_TABLES.owner`, position owner: verified intact, because a blank ban on the word would break the code's own vocabulary. ⚠️ The RULE CORPUS (`.github/instructions/`, `~/.copilot/instructions/`) still says "the owner" deliberately: there it defines a role ("the owner must approve X") and reads correctly — that vocabulary is a separate decision. | 2026-11-19 (direction; no issue — the reasoning is the artefact) | 🟢 enforced — `scripts/validate-phrasing.cjs` in root `prebuild` fails on P1 (a person-verb with a non-person subject), P2 (`Requirement, YYYY-MM-DD` as a speaker) and P3 (the possessive, including the escaped-apostrophe form that only exists inside a single-quoted JS string and which two scripted passes could not see). Self-test **10/10** proving each rule fires on its defect AND stays silent on the corrected form; canaried on the REAL tree — injected a person-verb sentence into a tracked doc → exit 1 naming file and line, restored → exit 0. It found **12 survivors** on its first run that three greps and two scripts had missed. ⚠️ NOT guarded, and stated: no check can see a replacement that broke a sentence ACROSS A LINE WRAP (four did — `no UI score was given for / the page a UI score`); every word is real, so they were found by reading each replacement in context. That rule is recorded in the guard's own docblock. |

| 231 | **A MOVERS DAY LIST DEFINES EXACTLY ONE MEASURABLE DAY — THE DATE IN ITS OWN NAME — and a leg/list side mismatch REFUSES rather than being resolved.** A stored `movers-{winners|losers}-YYYY-MM-DD` group is a screen of ONE session plus warm-up sessions for directive history; it is not a window to measure across. `parseMoversDatasetName()` is the one reader of that convention, used by BOTH the dataset list endpoint and the run, so the page cannot offer a dataset the run would refuse. `selectAnswerDay()` keeps exactly the named day, and REFUSES when the window does not contain it or the day could not be ranked — never falling back to the last day that happens to have bars. The side is taken from the protocol's own `directionMode` (Long → winners, Short → losers) and a list of the other end is a 400, because ranking the gainers while filing the run under the SHORT leg measures a different strategy under the wrong name. | 2026-11-19 (#1319) | 🟢 enforced — `movers-dataset.spec.ts` (12 cases: the name parser in both directions incl. a non-existent calendar date, plus every side-resolution branch and both conflict paths) and the `selectAnswerDay` block in `selection-ranking.spec.ts` (5 cases, canaried twice: the whole-window result really does hold several rankable days, and a missing date must not fall back to a present one). Exercised live 2026-11-19: `movers-winners-2026-11-11` + `movers-v1-long` returned ONE day and 3 selections where the previous build returned 5 rows and 15; `movers-losers-2026-11-10` + `movers-v1-long` returned 400 naming the conflict; `dev-window-v1` returned 400 naming the naming convention. 🔴 **NOT guarded, and stated on every result:** nothing can check that a stored candidate LIST was free of hindsight — the lists were built from close-to-close moves, so membership knows the outcome even though the ranking does not. That is reported as a caveat on each run and is the open work in the follow-up issue. |

| 232 | **A DERIVED TIMELINE LABELS RECORDED FACTS AND SAYS WHERE ONE IS MISSING — it never invents the reason a signal produced no trade.** The movers depth view puts every bar of one session on one axis: price, change against the previous session's close, both scores, the risk multiplier, the signal class, the bar-gate verdict, and the trade (if any) that sits on it. Each bar's STATE is derived, and the derivation is stated: a bar-gate failure outranks an actionable signal (the bar never reached the decision path), a `BUY` on a SHORT leg is not actionable, and a bar that both closed and opened a position says both. A bar that cleared both gates and produced no trade is labelled `signal-not-traded` **with the words "the engine does not record the per-bar reason"**, because it does not: `decision_log` is written by the live loop only (47,011 rows) and `decision_trail` has never been written (0 rows), so a backtest keeps trades and scores and nothing between them. | 2026-11-20 (#1319 follow-up; gap tracked in #1323) | 🟢 enforced — `run-depth.spec.ts` (14 cases) canaries the three ways the label can lie: a gated bar with a STRONG_BUY signal must report `bar-gate` (not "signalled, not traded"), a `BUY` bar on a SHORT run must report `quiet`, and no ladder rung may exceed its own denominator — the last one caught a live defect where `entries` counted the whole session against the post-cutoff bar count and printed **53 of 44**. ⚠️ The underlying per-bar REASON remains unrecorded; that is #1323, and this row is what stops the label being upgraded into a guess before it exists. |

| 233 | **A BACKTEST MUST RESOLVE A BOT, AND EVERY TRADE-MANAGEMENT NUMBER MUST DECLARE ITS SOURCE — `bot`, `this run`, or `engine default` — plus whether the engine applies it at all.** Measured 2026-11-20: the selection run passed neither `botId` nor an explicit bracket, so the engine used its own literals (`slPct 1`, `tpPct 1`, `minComposite 0`, no hold) while `Movers V1 Long Bot` is configured **2.5% / 6% / entry bar 30 / max hold 4h**. The result — 44 trades, TP 16 · SL 27 · EOD 1, net **−1,538.47** — was read as the strategy's configuration and reported as "a 1%/1% bracket is the problem". Re-run with the bot resolved by the SAME code, same dataset and same cutoff: **29 trades, TP 10 · SL 19, net +767.64**. The conclusion was an artefact of the wrong configuration, not a property of the strategy. `resolveBotForRun()` now auto-resolves (primary leg beats `shortProtocolId`, active beats inactive) unless `botId: null` is passed deliberately, and `buildTradeConfig()` returns one row per setting with `source` and `applied`. ⚠️ **`applied: false` IS THE SECOND HALF OF THE ANSWER AND IS NOT COSMETIC:** the engine reads no `cooldownMinutes`, no `maxPositions`, no `sessionWindow`, and sizes differently from the live loop, so those rows say the result is silent about them — which is why 29 trades on one session is still not the bot's trade count (#1325). | 2026-11-20 (#1319 follow-up; cooldown gap in #1325) | 🟢 enforced — `trade-config.spec.ts` (7 cases) canaries both failure directions: with `bot: null` every number must read `engine-default` (the exact defect that produced the wrong conclusion) and a field the engine ignores must read `applied: false` with its reason. Exercised live: the same request produced `resolvedBy: auto`, `bot: Movers V1 Long Bot (INACTIVE, manual)`, and the run's own `bracketNominalSource` agreed. 🔴 **NOT guarded:** nothing can detect that a HUMAN read a result as the strategy's configuration — the guard makes the provenance visible, it cannot make a reader use it. |
| 234 | **THE PIPELINE STAGE LIST IS A CHECKLIST FOR A BESPOKE PAGE, NOT A LAYOUT ENGINE.** A strategy page is easy to make incomplete: it shows the stages its author thought of. `/development/pipeline-graph-lab` already declares the canonical stages, so `src/core/pipeline-phases.js` requires every stage to be either mapped to the section that shows it or declared absent **with a reason** — a stage nobody remembered is then a build failure, not a gap a reader infers as "fine". ⚠️ **DELIBERATELY NOT WIRED INTO THE COCKPIT.** The graph lab's node text is illustrative and measurably stale ("99 Directives · 32 Protocols" against 60 registered; "3 Bots" against 64 active strategies), and the cockpit's seven columns are hardcoded panels in a 27KB page, not a column spec — so "use the cockpit to render every phase" is a build, not an existing capability. The reusable seam being built is the PHASE LIST, which a future cockpit board can consume once a column spec exists. ⚠️ And a card is the wrong shape for the depth view: two linked tables plus a per-directive decomposition is a table, not a panel. | 2026-11-20 (primary; reasoning is the artefact) | 🟢 enforced for the mechanical half — `src/core/pipeline-phases.test.js` (6 cases) fails on a phase with no section and no stated reason, on a phase claiming both, on a duplicate id, and — the canary that matters — on a phase naming a DOM id that nothing renders, which is what catches a section rename leaving coverage looking complete. 🔴 **NOT guarded:** nothing checks that a phase's `provides` sentence is TRUE of its section, and nothing prevents a future page inventing its own phase vocabulary instead of importing this list. |

| 235 | **A BESPOKE PAGE HAS ONE FORM CONTAINER THAT FOLDS AWAY, AND ONE PANEL PER PIPELINE STAGE — explanation text is a CONTROL (`WhyPopover`), never a paragraph between sections.** The reader's measured faults on `/lab/movers-strategy-backtest` (2026-11-20) were: form controls scattered down the page; a stage STRIP that explained stages instead of showing them; a "None" button that cleared the day list, which is the one thing the page exists to vary; and prose dumped between containers ("really dull… this text dump"). The form now holds every input, collapses to a one-line summary on run, and is reopened by clicking that summary; results are separate panels, one per stage; `src/components/WhyPopover.jsx` holds every explanation behind a focusable control with `aria-expanded`, closing on outside click and Escape. ⚠️ The strip was removed but the PHASE LIST was not: it is the guard and the cockpit column spec, so `pipeline-phases.js` survives as data while the page's sections became the visual expression of it. | 2026-11-20 (direction; reasoning is the artefact) | 🟢 enforced for the mechanical half — `src/core/pipeline-phases.test.js` fails when a phase claims a section id that nothing renders, so deleting a panel without updating the phase list is a red build. 🔴 **NOT guarded:** nothing detects a paragraph creeping back between sections, and nothing checks the folded summary states the values the form actually holds. |
| 236 | **A COCKPIT IS A SPEC, NOT A PAGE — every column declares `ready` / `skeleton` / `absent` WITH A REASON, and a column that cannot render fails instead of showing an empty box.** The existing `/development/meta-stack-cockpit` is 27 KB with seven hardcoded columns; a second cockpit written the same way would be a second 27 KB page and the two would drift. `src/core/cockpit/column-spec.js` derives columns from `pipeline-phases.js` and refuses: an omitted phase (which would look like a phase with nothing to show), a `ready` column with no renderer, a `skeleton`/`absent` column with no note, and an unknown state. `/lab/movers-cockpit` is the skeleton that proves the seam: three columns render real data (session, configuration, outcome, stored run) and the rest say which renderer is missing. ⚠️ **A SKELETON RATHER THAN A BUILD** at the owner's direction — the shape can be judged on a real monitor before any renderer is written, and a skeleton that refuses to fake content is the version that stays honest while empty. | 2026-11-20 (direction) | 🟢 enforced — `src/core/cockpit/column-spec.test.js` (6 cases) canaries both silent-failure directions: an omitted phase is reported by name, and `ready` without a renderer is refused. 🔴 **NOT guarded:** nothing checks that a `ready` renderer shows the data its phase `provides`, and nothing prevents a future cockpit page from skipping the spec entirely and writing its own columns. |

| 237 | **THE EXIT DECISION IS EXTRACTED, NOT REWRITTEN — one formula now serves the live guardian and the backtest, and the STRENGTH difference between them is stated where a result is read.** The same two columns (`simTslActivationPct`, `simTslDistancePct`) were read by two implementations computing the same trigger price: `stop-guardian.observeSimulatedTsl()` (live) and the backtest's `StopPlan` loop. `nest/src/common/trading/exit-guardian/simulated-tsl.ts` is now the ONE decision — `simulatedTslState()` for the live observer (profit, watermark, trigger, armed, touched) and `trailTriggerPrice()` for the engine's tighten step — and the guardian's own 90-case suite passes unchanged, which is the proof that extraction changed no behaviour. ⚠️ **THE DIVERGENCE FOUND WHILE EXTRACTING IS THE MORE SERIOUS ONE:** live, the simulated trail RECORDS a decision point and moves nothing (`sim_tsl_event.action` is hardcoded `recorded`); the engine MOVES the stop to the trigger and exits there. So a run of a `simTslEnabled` bot shows trailing protection the live bot does not have — a safety net in a result that does not exist in the account. | 241 | **THE PER-BAR MANAGEMENT RULES LIVE IN THE SHARED CORE, SO THE ENGINE IS A DRIVER AND NOT A SECOND IMPLEMENTATION.** The engine managed an open position inside a ~180-line inline loop — excursion, the stop in force, the target, the bot's time stop, then the tighten-for-the-next-bar step — every one a rule the live path also applies, with no way to reuse any of it except by copying. `common/trading/exit-guardian/position-bar.ts` now owns the whole decision as a pure function (`managePositionBar`), and the engine's loop is a call to it plus a `break`. ⚠️ **THE ORDER IS THE DECISION AND IS RECORDED IN THE MODULE:** excursion on the RAW extremes before any test (so a trade that exits still reports how far it went — dropping the exit bar biases the excursion statistics toward survivors); the stop IN FORCE from an EARLIER bar (tightening from this bar's high and then testing this bar's low is look-ahead, because OHLC cannot order an intrabar high against an intrabar low); the target; the TIME stop at the close LAST, because a level the market genuinely traded through is more defensible than a decision to leave at the close; then the tighten for the NEXT bar. `maxHoldHours` arithmetic is passed IN (`heldTooLong`) because it belongs to `max-hold.ts` with its own inclusive-boundary spec — the core decides only WHERE it applies. ⚠️ **ONE BUG FIXED WHILE EXTRACTING, AND IT COULD NOT HAVE CHANGED A STORED NUMBER:** the lock-at-breakeven step used `Math.max(candidate, entry)` on BOTH sides, so for a SHORT it was `min(stop, max(stop, entry)) = stop` — it could never tighten. | 272 | **A `gap` IN THE PARITY REGISTER IS NOW JUDGED ON THE ROW'S OWN ID, NOT ON A HAND-CHOSEN PROBE TOKEN — BECAUSE THE TOKEN WAS WRONG AND THE REGISTER LIED FOR AS LONG AS IT EXISTED.** The `sim-tsl` row claimed the backtest engine does not model the simulated trailing stop (`gap`, "not modelled"). Proved behaviourally rather than by reading: same bot, same session, only `simTslActivationPct`/`simTslDistancePct` filled → 18 of 22 trades exited `TRAIL`, P&L 1,628.73 → 379.28 (11-19) and 222.69 → 182.08 (11-20). **Two consequences.** (1) The row is corrected to `divergent`: the ENGINE moves the stop, the LIVE guardian only RECORDS the moment (#936, `action` hardcoded `'recorded'`, `sim_tsl_event` 0 rows), so a run with a trail configured shows protection the account does not have — the dangerous direction. (2) `checkRow()` now also asks, for every `gap` row, whether any engine file **imports a module whose basename spells the mechanism**: narrow on purpose, it reads imports and not words, so `pyramid` (a genuine gap) still passes while `sim-tsl` fails. Canaried in the only order that proves anything — the new rule was run against the UNFIXED register and failed on the real defect (`gap ROT: … → './sim-tsl'`), then the row was corrected and it passed; `--self-test` grew from 9 to **11 cases**, including the real `sim-tsl` row as a positive and `pyramid` as a negative control. | A register row is a claim about two implementations, and a claim resting on a guessed identifier is unproven. | 273 | **A DISPLAY REQUIREMENT IS A SELECT UNTIL A QUERY IS PROVED IMPOSSIBLE — A NEW TABLE IS JUSTIFIED ONLY BY STATE THAT CANNOT BE RECOMPUTED.** The owner's objection (2026-11-24, paraphrased): asking for data to be displayed keeps producing more functions writing more tables that duplicate what is already stored, when clear queries over existing tables would do. Its `winner` / `longComposite` / `shortComposite` / `finalSignal` exist nowhere else (measured: `decision_log` carries no long-vs-short comparison at all; its reasons are allocation, cooldown and cap text). `decision_trail`'s defect is an **unwired writer**, not redundancy, and filling it is justified by this row's own test because "which side won, and by how much" is a decision made at a moment and cannot be recomputed. ⚠️ **The same session produced a second correction worth keeping:** I declared the decision chain "not joinable" after measuring `trade.ticket` (**15 of 991**) — there is also `trade.brokerTicket` (**varchar, 695 of 991**), which is the column the writers populate, and via it the chain joins at **94.5%** (`trade.brokerTicket` → `decision_log.ticket`, 362 of 383 trades inside coverage) and **100%** for `stop_adjustment`. Checking one column and declaring the table broken is the "read the column AND its type" rule applied to an audit rather than to a query. **The test, applied before any new column or table:** could a `SELECT` produce this number? If yes, query it. If no, state in one sentence what is lost by not storing it — and only then add it. A *decision made at a moment* (why this side won) is not recomputable and may be stored; a value derivable from rows that already exist may not. ⚠️ **Applies directly to the planned Decision Trail page:** it must not need a single new table. If `decision_log` can answer the LONG/SHORT question alone, then `decision_trail` should be **retired, not filled** — filling a duplicate is the defect this row exists to prevent. | Keeps one fact in one place, and stops a display request from becoming a second source of truth that drifts. The alternative — a table per screen — is how the repo reached 13 unmodelled tables and a queue that cannot be joined. | 2026-11-24 | 🔴 **UNGUARDED** — no validator refuses a new table or column that duplicates an existing one. The rule is a review obligation, stated at #1347. A candidate guard (fail a new table whose columns duplicate another's) is deliberately NOT built: the owner has asked for less process, not more. |
| 274 | **A PARITY-REGISTER LABEL THAT CLAIMS A SIDE IS INERT MUST BE CONTRADICTED BY A BROKER-MUTATING CALL, OR THE BUILD FAILS.** The `trail` row's live label read *"the Stop Guardian, which only RECORDS the decision"*. It is false: `applyModify()` calls `modifyStop(pos, newSl)` and only records + notifies **on success**, and `stop_adjustment` holds **5,678 rows of which 5,673 carry a `Consensus …` reason** (measured 2026-11-24). The record-only behaviour belongs to the **separate** sim-TSL observer (`observeSimulatedTsl`, `sim_tsl_event`, 0 rows). The error ran in the **understating** direction — the one that costs a reader their caution, because told the live side is inert they read a run's trail as *more* protection than the account has, when the guardian is in fact the most active stop mechanism on the account. **The rule now:** if a `live.label` matches /only (record|observ|display|log)/, none of that row's live files may contain a broker-mutating call (`modifyStop(`, `applyModify(`, `modifyPosition(`, `sendOrder(`, `placeOrder(`); otherwise it fails naming the row and the call. Narrow on purpose — it reads a small named list, so it fires on a call and not on the word "stop", and the sim-TSL label survives because that file genuinely places nothing. ⚠️ **The rule was written, self-tested with FABRICATED rows, and then observed to PASS against the real register — because `parseRegister()` never captured `label`, so the rule's input was always `undefined`.** The canary landed, printed `canary landed: true`, and the gate stayed green: a guard blind in the only state that matters. Fixed by parsing `label`, by making a labelless parse exit **2 (cannot judge)** rather than pass, and by adding two self-test cases that assert the parse carries labels and that its absence is detectable. **Also recorded:** `guardianStopDecision()` — the composed rule extracted "so a backtest can call the same one" — is called by **nothing but its own spec**; the live service imports the three helpers and composes them inline, and the engine does not call it at all. A shared rule with no production caller is a demonstration, not parity. | A register's value is that its labels are checkable. Two failure modes are now closed: a label that understates the live mechanism, and a rule that reads a field the parser drops. | 2026-11-24 | 🟢 `validate-parity-register.cjs` in `prebuild`. Canaried on the REAL register in the only order that proves anything: label reverted → `exit 1` naming `modifyStop()`; restored byte-for-byte → `exit 0`; a removed label → `exit 2 CANNOT JUDGE`. `--self-test` 13 → **15 cases**. |
| 275 | **A DECLARED EXIT BOUNDARY DOES NOT MAKE THE TIME STOP REACHABLE — IT PRE-EMPTS IT, AND THE ARITHMETIC IS `boundary − cutoff`.** `timeStopReachable()` returned `true` whenever `exitBeforeUtc` was set, on the reasoning that "the clock flattens the position anyway". That is backwards: the boundary closes the position *before* the hold can, so the fact made the stop unreachable was being reported as the reason it was fine. **Measured, not argued** — two movers sessions, same bot, one field changed each time: at `exitBeforeUtc: '20:45'` with `cutoffUtc: '15:30'`, a **6-hour** hold produced **zero** `TIME` exits (identical 1,628.73 / 222.69 and an identical `SESSION×21 · EOD×1` mix to the 10-hour arm), while a **2-hour** hold produced **TIME×21** and **TIME×17**. So the maximum holding time is `boundary − cutoff` = **5.25 h**, and every hold ≥ 5h15m is dead on this configuration — which is why the long bot's `maxHoldHours: 10` had never fired and why the third arm's prediction ("some TIME exits appear") was **falsified**, correctly, by the measurement. **The fix:** `ViabilityInput` gains `cutoffUtc`, and `maxHoldingMinutes()` returns `min(sessionSpan, boundary − cutoff)`; `timeStopReachable` compares the hold against that. Three "cannot judge" paths still return `true` and must: no declared hold, an unknown span, and a boundary with **no cutoff** or an unreadable one — because a boundary alone cannot say how far away it is. The `why` sentence now names **which** constraint binds (`… spans 6.4h, with a 20:45 UTC boundary leaving at most 5.25h, so its time stop cannot fire at all`), and does so on **every** unreachable verdict rather than only `mark-only`: a run with 21 of 22 decision exits was carrying `timeStopReachable: false` beside a sentence that said nothing about why. | A diagnostic that says "reachable" about an unreachable stop is the same defect class as a total that is a mark: it is plausible output with no error. This one also explains a strategy symptom directly — the movers long bot has never had a time exit because 10h > 5.25h. **No trading value changed**: `maxHoldHours` is untouched, nothing is refused, and the only outputs altered are a boolean and a sentence. Register row 23 therefore does not apply, and the A/B that tested the hold used copied bots (`Movers V1 Long Bot — session exit`, `— 6h hold`, `— 2h hold (mechanism test)`), all `active: 0`, `magic` NULL, unseeded. | 2026-11-24 | 🟢 `backtest-viability.spec.ts` **19 cases** — including the boundary-pre-empts-the-hold case, the exact-limit 5.25h case, and four cannot-judge paths. Canaried by restoring the old `input.exitBeforeUtc ? null : …` behaviour: **1 failed, 17 passed** at the boundary case, restored byte-for-byte → **18 passed** (then 19 with the new why assertion). Served payload verified live: `reachable: false` with the 5.25h sentence. |
| 276 | **A CONFIG TABLE ROW MUST AGREE WITH ITSELF: `applied` SAYS WHETHER THE MECHANISM RUNS, NOT WHETHER THE SWITCH IS ON.** `trade-config.ts` hardcoded `applied: true` for the trailing-stop row with a comment arguing "the engine really does move the stop to the trail" — true only when a plan can be BUILT. For `simTslEnabled = 1` with both percentages NULL, `simTslPlan()` returns null and the engine moves nothing, while the row said **`applied: true`** and its own `effect` said **"NO trailing stop is applied"**. One row asserting two opposite things. ⚠️ **And the row could not have said anything else, because the CAUSE was one layer down:** `resolveBotForRun()`'s Prisma `select` omitted `simTslActivationPct` and `simTslDistancePct`, so `tslConfigured` was `undefined`-driven and **always false** — the configured branch was dead code, and every bot was told its trail did nothing. Measured live on the two arms that prove it: the **incumbent** (percentages NULL) and the **trail probe** (2% / 1%) both returned `applied: true` and the same "NO trailing stop is applied" sentence — while their runs produced **`EOD×22`** and **`TRAIL×18`** respectively. After the fix each row matches its own exits: `applied: false` + "NO trailing stop is applied" for the incumbent; `applied: true` + "The stop is moved up behind the price" for the probe. **The engine was never wrong** — it fetches the bot by id separately; only the table the owner reads to judge a run was wrong, about the one mechanism he opens it to check. **Two rules:** (1) `applied` = the mechanism runs, and a switch without its inputs is not a mechanism; (2) **a column missing from a `select` is a column the table cannot read** — add a bot field to `TradeConfigBot` and it must be added to that projection in the same change. | A row that contradicts itself is worse than a missing row: the reader takes whichever half confirms what they expected. And a dead `else` branch is invisible — it reads as "no bot is ever configured", which is a claim about the data, not about the projection. | 2026-11-24 | 🟢 `trade-config.spec.ts` now asserts `applied === false` for the inert and half-configured trail; `trade-config-coverage.spec.ts` was itself corrected — its "every honoured field is applied" canary demanded `true` for a switch that is legitimately OFF, forcing the lie — and now requires a non-applied honoured row to say `NOT APPLIED`, which the next test enforces for every row. Canaried by restoring `applied: true`: **2 failed, 13 passed**; restored byte-for-byte → **15 passed**. Full backtest module: **589 passed**. |
| 277 | **AN ARMED RULE THAT CANNOT FIRE MUST SAY SO WHERE IT IS SET, AND REUSE THE ARITHMETIC THAT DECIDES IT.** Row 275 fixed the *verdict* (`timeStopReachable`). The annotation lives in `applyHoldReachability()` and runs **after** the run, because the dataset's span is unknown when the table is first built, and it **reuses `maxHoldingMinutes()`** rather than repeating `boundary − cutoff` — a second copy of that arithmetic is how two numbers drift and only one gets the next fix. Three "cannot judge" paths say nothing at all (no hold declared, boundary with no cutoff, unreadable time); a **null span still judges**, because a boundary is a hard clock limit that bounds the hold on its own — that correction came from a test that failed on my own expectation, not from the code being wrong. | The table is what the owner opens to judge a run. | 278 | **A LINT GATE MUST REPORT A DEFECT A HUMAN WILL FIX — FORMATTING IS NOT ONE, AND A RULE THAT HAS NEVER PASSED IS NOT A GATE.** `nest/eslint.config.mjs` carried `"prettier/prettier": ["error", { endOfLine: "auto" }]` over a backend that has **never** been prettier-wrapped at that print width. Measured 2026-11-25: `npm --prefix nest run lint` reported **31,583 problems, of which 30,355 were `prettier/prettier`** — and `npx eslint .`, the obvious command, **OOMs at a 4 GB heap** and prints a crash dump that contains no violations at all, which reads as "clean". Both facts are why CI has been red on every push (#1320): a rule producing ~30,000 cosmetic errors on every run cannot be read, so the **1,228 warnings and 12,691 genuine errors beneath it were invisible**. **The change:** `prettier/prettier` → `off`, with `npm run format` (write, already existed) and a new `npm run format:check` (report) keeping the intent out of the build path. ⚠️ **This does NOT make lint green** — it drops the noise from 31,583 to 12,691 and makes the remainder visible for the first time. ⚠️ **And it deliberately does NOT touch the largest remaining family:** `no-unsafe-member-access` 6,230 · `no-unsafe-assignment` 3,562 · `no-unsafe-call` 944 · `no-unsafe-return` 479 = **11,215 of 12,691, 88%**, every one a consequence of `any` in a config that already sets `"@typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any": "off"`. Downgrading them to reach a green light is the allowlisted-guard failure; the contradiction between permitting `any` and erroring on its consequences is a **policy question for the owner**, stated on #1320 with the full ranked table. | A gate's value is the decisions it produces. When 96% of a gate's output is cosmetic, the gate trains its reader to skim, which is the same as no gate — the repo's own "a light that is always red is indistinguishable from no light". Separating format from correctness also means a formatting disagreement can never again hide a type hole. | 2026-11-25 | 🔴 **UNGUARDED by design** — nothing fails if someone sets `prettier/prettier` back to `error`. The reversal note in the config says not to, because the rule goes red again immediately (30,355 errors) unless `npm run format` has been run first. A guard is deliberately NOT added: the honest state is that lint is still red for a reason awaiting a decision, and a gate that asserted "lint is green" would have to be disabled to keep the build working — which is the thing this row forbids. |
